\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})-\int\sum_{i=1}^{d}\log\!\big((1-t)+t\lambda_{i}(x)\big)\,d\mu_{0}(x).

Moreover, for
t\in(0,1)
,

\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{(1-\lambda_{i}(x))^{2}}{\big((1-t)+t\lambda_{i}(x)\big)^{2}}\,d\mu_{0}(x)\geq 0,

whenever differentiation under integral is justified.

Proof

First formula is Proposition
9
plus

\det((1-t)I+tDT)=\prod_{i}((1-t)+t\lambda_{i})
.
Differentiate:

\frac{d}{dt}\left[-\log((1-t)+t\lambda_{i})\right]=-\frac{\lambda_{i}-1}{(1-t)+t\lambda_{i}},

\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\left[-\log((1-t)+t\lambda_{i})\right]=\frac{(\lambda_{i}-1)^{2}}{((1-t)+t\lambda_{i})^{2}}\geq 0.

Integrate over
\mu_{0}
.

Theorem 0..11
(Relative entropy curvature under uniformly convex confinement)

Let
V\in C^{2}
with
\nabla^{2}V\succeq\kappa I
, and

\mathcal{F}_{V}(\mu)=\mathcal{H}(\mu)+\int V\,d\mu+\log Z_{V}.

Under Assumption
0..8
,

\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{V}(\mu_{t})=\int\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{t}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\,d\mu_{t}+\int\langle\nabla^{2}V\,\nabla\phi_{t},\nabla\phi_{t}\rangle\,d\mu_{t}\geq\kappa\int|\nabla\phi_{t}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}.

Proof

Differentiate
\int V\,d\mu_{t}
:

\frac{d}{dt}\int V\,d\mu_{t}=\int\nabla V\cdot\nabla\phi_{t}\,d\mu_{t}.

Differentiate again; using CE and geodesic equation yields

\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}\int V\,d\mu_{t}=\int\langle\nabla^{2}V\,\nabla\phi_{t},\nabla\phi_{t}\rangle\,d\mu_{t}.

Add Theorem
0..10
and constant cancellation for
\log Z_{V}
.
Lower bound follows from
\nabla^{2}V\succeq\kappa I
.

Remark 3
(Implication for entropy-rate constraints)

Convexity of
t\mapsto\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})
implies that, once a lower bound on the initial
slope
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(0^{+})
is enforced, the same lower bound propagates in averaged form:

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{s})\geq(t-s)\,\inf_{r\in[s,t]}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(r),

which is a key ingredient for verifying global entropy-budget feasibility in Section
C
.

B.4. Entropy dissipation formula

We derive exact entropy-balance identities under transport–diffusion dynamics,
identify the Fisher-information production term, and establish sharp inequalities
used later in existence, stability, and mode-coverage arguments.

Assumption 0..12
(Regular transport–diffusion regime)

Let
(\rho_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
satisfy

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}b_{t})=\varepsilon\,\Delta\rho_{t}\quad\text{in }(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\qquad\varepsilon\geq 0,

with:

1.

\rho_{t}\in C^{1}_{t}C^{2}_{x}
,
\rho_{t}>0
,
\int\rho_{t}\,dx=1
;

2.

b\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(0,T;W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))
;

3.

all boundary terms at infinity vanish in integrations by parts
(e.g., via finite second moment and suitable decay).

Set
\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}\,dx
.

Definition 15
(Fisher information)

For
\mu=\rho\,dx
with
\rho>0
and
\sqrt{\rho}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, define

\mathcal{I}(\mu):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}\,dx=4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla\sqrt{\rho}|^{2}\,dx.

If the integral diverges, set
\mathcal{I}(\mu)=+\infty
.

Proposition 11
(Exact entropy balance: advection–diffusion)

Under Assumption
0..12
,

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{t}(x)\,\nabla\!\cdot b_{t}(x)\,dx-\varepsilon\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

Equivalently,

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=-\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}\cdot b_{t}\,d\mu_{t}-\varepsilon\int\frac{|\nabla\rho_{t}|^{2}}{\rho_{t}}\,dx.

Proof

Differentiate
\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int\rho_{t}\log\rho_{t}\,dx
:

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int(\partial_{t}\rho_{t})(1+\log\rho_{t})\,dx.

Insert PDE:

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}=-\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}b_{t})+\varepsilon\Delta\rho_{t}.

Hence

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}=-\int\nabla\!\cdot(\rho b)\,(1+\log\rho)\,dx+\varepsilon\int\Delta\rho\,(1+\log\rho)\,dx=:A+D.

For advection term
A
:

A=\int\rho b\cdot\nabla\log\rho\,dx=\int b\cdot\nabla\rho\,dx=-\int\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot b\,dx

or equivalently
A=-\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot b\,d\mu
.

For diffusion term
D
:

D=-\varepsilon\int\nabla\rho\cdot\nabla\log\rho\,dx=-\varepsilon\int\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}\,dx=-\varepsilon\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

Combining gives both forms.

Corollary 7
(Pure transport entropy rate)

If
\varepsilon=0
(continuity equation
\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\cdot(\rho b)=0
),
then

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int\rho_{t}\,\nabla\!\cdot b_{t}\,dx.

In particular, if
\nabla\!\cdot b_{t}=0
a.e., entropy is conserved.

Proof

Immediate from Proposition
11
.

Corollary 8
(Pure diffusion entropy dissipation)

If
b\equiv 0
,
\partial_{t}\rho=\varepsilon\Delta\rho
, then

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=-\varepsilon\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\leq 0.

Thus
\mathcal{H}
is nonincreasing and dissipates at Fisher-information rate.

Proof

Set
b=0
in Proposition
11
.

Proposition 12
(Integrated entropy-budget identity)

Under Assumption
0..12
, for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{s})=\int_{s}^{t}\!\!\int\rho_{r}\,\nabla\!\cdot b_{r}\,dx\,dr-\varepsilon\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{r})\,dr.

Hence if

\int\rho_{r}\,\nabla\!\cdot b_{r}\,dx\geq-\lambda\quad\text{a.e. }r,

then

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{s})\geq-\lambda(t-s)-\varepsilon\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{r})\,dr.

Proof

Integrate Proposition
11
over
[s,t]
.
The inequality follows from the assumed lower bound on mean divergence term.

Lemma 8
(Entropy production decomposition for gradient drift)

Suppose
b_{t}=-\nabla U_{t}
with
U_{t}\in C^{2}
.
Then

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=-\int\rho_{t}\,\Delta U_{t}\,dx-\varepsilon\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

If
\nabla^{2}U_{t}\succeq\alpha I
, then
\Delta U_{t}\geq\alpha d
, so

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\leq-\alpha d-\varepsilon\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

Proof

From Proposition
11
,

\int\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot b\,dx=-\int\rho\,\Delta U\,dx.

If
\nabla^{2}U\succeq\alpha I
, then
\Delta U\geq\alpha d
, and since
\int\rho=1
,

-\int\rho\,\Delta U\,dx\leq-\alpha d.

Add
-\varepsilon\mathcal{I}
.

Proposition 13
(Dissipation of relative entropy under Langevin dynamics)

Let

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}=\nabla\!\cdot\big(\rho_{t}\nabla V\big)+\varepsilon\Delta\rho_{t},

and define Gibbs
\gamma_{\varepsilon,V}(dx)=Z^{-1}e^{-V(x)/\varepsilon}dx
.
Then

\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma_{\varepsilon,V}}(\mu_{t})=-\varepsilon\int\left|\nabla\log\frac{\rho_{t}}{\gamma_{\varepsilon,V}}\right|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}\leq 0.

Proof

Write Fokker–Planck
[
29
]
in gradient-flow form:

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}=\varepsilon\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\left(\rho_{t}\nabla\log\frac{\rho_{t}}{\gamma_{\varepsilon,V}}\right).

Differentiate relative entropy:

\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})=\int\partial_{t}\rho_{t}\,\log\frac{\rho_{t}}{\gamma}\,dx

(using
\int\partial_{t}\rho_{t}\,dx=0
).
Integrate by parts:

\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})=-\varepsilon\int\rho_{t}\left|\nabla\log\frac{\rho_{t}}{\gamma}\right|^{2}dx.

Theorem 0..13
(Entropy dissipation bound under LSI)

Assume
\gamma
satisfies logarithmic Sobolev inequality
[
1
]

\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\nu)\leq\frac{1}{2\kappa}\int\left|\nabla\log\frac{d\nu}{d\gamma}\right|^{2}\,d\nu\quad(\kappa>0).

Then any solution of the
\gamma
-reversible Fokker–Planck equation satisfies

\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})\leq e^{-2\kappa\varepsilon t}\,\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{0}).

Proof

From Proposition
13
,

\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})=-\varepsilon\,\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t}),\quad\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}(\mu):=\int\left|\nabla\log\frac{d\mu}{d\gamma}\right|^{2}d\mu.

LSI gives
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})\geq 2\kappa\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})
, so

\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})\leq-2\kappa\varepsilon\,\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t}).

Apply Grönwall
[
16
]
:

\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{t})\leq e^{-2\kappa\varepsilon t}\operatorname{Ent}_{\gamma}(\mu_{0}).

Proposition 14
(Distributional entropy inequality for weak solutions)

Let
\rho
be a weak solution of

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\cdot(\rho b)=\varepsilon\Delta\rho

with
\rho\log\rho\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1})
,
\sqrt{\rho}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1})
, and

b\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(dt\,d\mu_{t})
,
(\nabla\cdot b)^{-}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(dt\,d\mu_{t})
.
Then for nonnegative
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T))
,

-\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\eta^{\prime}(t)\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\!\int\rho_{t}\nabla\!\cdot b_{t}\,dx\,dt-\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

Mollify in space-time:
\rho^{\delta}
solves approximate smooth equation with commutator error
r^{\delta}\to 0
.
Apply Proposition
11
to
\rho^{\delta}
, multiply by
\eta
, integrate in
t
:

-\int\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t}^{\delta})\eta^{\prime}=\int\eta\!\int\rho_{t}^{\delta}\nabla\!\cdot b_{t}\,dx\,dt-\varepsilon\int\eta\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}^{\delta})\,dt+\int\eta\,R^{\delta}(t)\,dt,

where
R^{\delta}\to 0
from commutator control under DiPerna–Lions hypotheses
[
11
]
.
Use lower semicontinuity of Fisher information and weak convergence of
\rho^{\delta}
to pass
\delta\downarrow 0
,
obtaining inequality (“
\leq
”) in distributional form.

Consequence for later sections.

The identity

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot b_{t}]-\varepsilon\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})

is the core quantitative bridge between entropy-rate constraints, diffusion-style regularization,
and anti-collapse guarantees. In Section
C
, it enters the KKT system as the active inequality

\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
; in Sections
G
–
I
, it yields explicit lower-density and failure bounds.

B.5. Fisher information connection

This subsection establishes the precise analytic coupling between entropy rate,
Fisher information, and Wasserstein metric slope. These identities are used later
for (i) entropy-budget feasibility, (ii) compactness/coercivity, (iii)
\Gamma
-limits,
and (iv) anti-collapse lower-density bounds.

Definition 16
(Fisher information and score field)

For
\mu=\rho\,dx
with
\rho>0
,
\sqrt{\rho}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, define

\mathcal{I}(\mu):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\,d\mu.

The score field is

s_{\mu}(x):=\nabla\log\rho(x),

so that
\mathcal{I}(\mu)=\|s_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}
.

Lemma 9
(Equivalent Fisher representations)

For
\mu=\rho\,dx
as above:

\mathcal{I}(\mu)=4\int|\nabla\sqrt{\rho}|^{2}\,dx=\sup_{\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left\{2\int\nabla\!\cdot\varphi\,d\mu-\int|\varphi|^{2}\,d\mu\right\}.

Proof

First identity:

\nabla\sqrt{\rho}=\frac{\nabla\rho}{2\sqrt{\rho}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad 4|\nabla\sqrt{\rho}|^{2}=\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}.

Integrate.

For the dual representation, integration by parts gives

\int\nabla\!\cdot\varphi\,d\mu=\int\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot\varphi\,dx=-\int\varphi\cdot\nabla\rho\,dx=-\int\varphi\cdot\nabla\log\rho\,d\mu.

Hence

2\int\nabla\!\cdot\varphi\,d\mu-\int|\varphi|^{2}\,d\mu=-2\!\int\varphi\cdot s_{\mu}\,d\mu-\int|\varphi|^{2}\,d\mu\leq\int|s_{\mu}|^{2}\,d\mu=\mathcal{I}(\mu),

by completing squares:

-2\varphi\!\cdot s_{\mu}-|\varphi|^{2}=|s_{\mu}|^{2}-|\varphi+s_{\mu}|^{2}.

Taking
\varphi_{n}\to-s_{\mu}
in
L^{2}(\mu)
with smooth compactly supported approximants
attains the supremum.

Theorem 0..14
(Metric slope of entropy equals square-root Fisher information)

For
\mu=\rho\,dx\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{ac}
with
\mathcal{I}(\mu)<\infty
,
the local Wasserstein slope of
\mathcal{H}
satisfies

|\partial\mathcal{H}|(\mu)^{2}=\mathcal{I}(\mu).

Proof

Upper bound.

Take any smooth CE perturbation
\partial_{t}\nu_{t}+\nabla\cdot(\nu_{t}v_{t})=0
,
\nu_{0}=\mu
.
From first variation:

\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{H}(\nu_{t})=\int s_{\mu}\cdot v_{0}\,d\mu.

By Cauchy–Schwarz:

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{H}(\nu_{t})\right|\leq\|s_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(\mu)}\,\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}.

Using characterization of metric slope as supremum directional derivative normalized
by metric speed implies

|\partial\mathcal{H}|(\mu)\leq\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(\mu)}.

Lower bound.

Choose direction
v_{0}=s_{\mu}
(approximated by gradient fields in
T_{\mu}\mathcal{P}_{2}
).
Then directional derivative equals

\int s_{\mu}\cdot s_{\mu}\,d\mu=\mathcal{I}(\mu),

while metric speed at
t=0
is
\|s_{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(\mu)}
.
Hence

|\partial\mathcal{H}|(\mu)\geq\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(\mu)}.

Combine both bounds.

Corollary 9
(Entropy chain-rule bound along CE trajectories)

Let
(\mu_{t},v_{t})\in\mathrm{CE}([0,T])
, with
\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})
absolutely continuous and

\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})<\infty
a.e. Then for a.e.
t
,

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\right|\leq\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})}\,\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}.

Consequently, for any
\alpha>0
,

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\geq-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha}{2}\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}^{2}\quad\text{a.e.}

Proof

First inequality is chain rule with metric slope:

\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\right|\leq|\partial\mathcal{H}|(\mu_{t})\,|\dot{\mu}_{t}|\leq\sqrt{\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})}\,\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})},

using Theorem
0..14
and
|\dot{\mu}_{t}|\leq\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}
.
Second inequality follows from Young
[
46
]
:

ab\leq\frac{1}{2\alpha}a^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}b^{2},\quad a=\sqrt{\mathcal{I}},\ b=\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}.

Proposition 15
(De Bruijn identity (Gaussian smoothing))

Let
\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx
solve heat equation

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}=\Delta\rho_{t},\qquad t>0,\quad\mu_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{ac}.

Then

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=-\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\quad\text{for }t>0.

Equivalently, for Shannon entropy
S(\mu):=-\mathcal{H}(\mu)
,

\frac{d}{dt}S(\mu_{t})=\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

Proof

Set
b=0,\varepsilon=1
in Proposition
11
(entropy balance):

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=-\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

Sign-flip gives Shannon form.

Theorem 0..15
(Entropy–Fisher dissipation estimate under entropy-rate constraint)

Assume
(\mu_{t},v_{t})\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
, and in weak sense

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda\quad\text{a.e. }t.

Then for every
\alpha>0
,

\frac{1}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt\leq\lambda T+\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})+\frac{\alpha}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}^{2}\,dt.

Proof

From Corollary
9
,

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\geq-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha}{2}\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}^{2}.

Also by assumption:

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda.

Using the first inequality and integrating:

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})\geq-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt-\frac{\alpha}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}dt.

Rearrange:

\frac{1}{2\alpha}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt\leq\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})+\frac{\alpha}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|^{2}dt.

Adding the nonnegative slack
\lambda T
keeps inequality valid and makes dependence
on budget explicit, yielding the stated bound.

Proposition 16
(Lower density control from Fisher-information integrability)

Fix
R>0
,
t\in(0,T]
, and assume
\rho_{t}\in W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
,
\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})<\infty
.
Then for a.e.
x,y\in B_{R}
,

|\log\rho_{t}(x)-\log\rho_{t}(y)|\leq C_{d,R}\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})^{1/2}.

Hence if
\int_{B_{R}}\rho_{t}\,dx\geq m_{R}>0
, then

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{B_{R}}\rho_{t}\geq m_{R}\,|B_{R}|^{-1}\exp\!\big(-2C_{d,R}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})^{1/2}\big).

Proof

For smooth positive density:

\nabla\log\rho_{t}=\frac{\nabla\rho_{t}}{\rho_{t}},\qquad\|\nabla\log\rho_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}^{2}=\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

On bounded domains, weighted-to-unweighted control plus Poincaré-type estimate
[
37
]
for

\log\rho_{t}
along line segments gives oscillation bound

\operatorname*{osc}_{B_{R}}\log\rho_{t}\leq C_{d,R}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})^{1/2}.

Let
a=\essinf_{B_{R}}\rho_{t}
,
b=\esssup_{B_{R}}\rho_{t}
. Then
b\leq ae^{C}
,
C:=2C_{d,R}\mathcal{I}^{1/2}
.
Since
\int_{B_{R}}\rho_{t}\geq m_{R}
, we have
m_{R}\leq b|B_{R}|\leq ae^{C}|B_{R}|
, so

a\geq m_{R}|B_{R}|^{-1}e^{-C}.

Approximate weak densities by mollification and pass to limit.

Remark 4
(Why this subsection is structurally central)

Sections
C
–
F
use Theorem
0..14
and
Corollary
9
to control entropy-rate constraints
in the variational problem.
Sections
G
–
I
use Proposition
16

to convert entropy/Fisher bounds into explicit anti-collapse guarantees.

C. Entropy-Controlled Flow Matching Formulation

C.1. Variational problem

We now define the population-level entropy-controlled flow matching problem as a
constrained dynamic optimization in Wasserstein space, with precise admissible class,
objective, and entropy-rate feasibility conditions.

Data and reference marginals.

Fix
T>0
, source/target marginals

\mu_{0},\mu_{T}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{ac}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad\mu_{0}=\rho_{0}dx,\ \mu_{T}=\rho_{T}dx.

Reference flow-matching drift.

Let
u^{\star}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a given measurable reference velocity
(the population target field induced by the interpolation law used in FM), satisfying

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u_{t}^{\star}(x)|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}(x)\,dt<\infty

for all admissible
\mu
considered below.

Definition 17
(Admissible trajectory-control pairs)

For fixed
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
, define

\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):=\left\{(\mu,v):\begin{array}[]{l}\mu\in C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\ \mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx\ \text{a.e. }t,\\
v\in L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t};\mathbb{R}^{d}),\\
\partial_{t}\mu_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}v_{t})=0\ \text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\\
\mu_{|t=0}=\mu_{0},\ \mu_{|t=T}=\mu_{T}\end{array}\right\}.

Definition 18
(Entropy-rate feasible class)

For
\lambda\geq 0
, define

\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):=\left\{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\in AC([0,T]),\ \frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda\ \text{a.e.}\right\}.

Equivalent integrated form:

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{s})\geq-\lambda(t-s),\quad 0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

Definition 19
(Population FM misfit functional)

For
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
, define

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|v_{t}(x)-u_{t}^{\star}(x)|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}(x)\,dt.

Definition 20
(Entropy-controlled FM problem)

Given
\lambda\geq 0
, solve

\boxed{\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)}

with value

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};u^{\star}):=\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v).

Remark 5
(Expansion and relation to kinetic action)

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)=\frac{1}{2}\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt-\int u^{\star}\!\cdot v\,d\mu dt+\frac{1}{2}\int|u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt.

The first term is Benamou–Brenier kinetic action, the second is alignment with FM target,
and the third is a
\mu
-weighted normalization term.

Assumption 0..16
(Integrability and coercivity envelope)

Assume:

1.

u^{\star}
has at most linear growth:

|u_{t}^{\star}(x)|\leq a_{t}+b_{t}|x|,\quad a,b\in L^{2}(0,T),\ b\geq 0;

2.

admissible curves satisfy uniform second-moment bound (from
A.2
/
A.3
):

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}m_{2}(\mu_{t})<\infty;

3.

endpoint entropies are finite:

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0}),\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})<\infty.

Lemma 10
(Nonemptiness criterion for entropy-feasible set)

If there exists
(\bar{\mu},\bar{v})\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
with

\mathcal{H}(\bar{\mu}_{t})\in AC([0,T])
and

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{t\in(0,T)}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\bar{\mu}_{t})\geq-\lambda,

then
\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})\neq\emptyset
, hence

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}<+\infty
.

Proof

Immediate from Definition
18
:
(\bar{\mu},\bar{v})\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
.
Then

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}\leq\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\bar{\mu},\bar{v})<\infty

by
L^{2}
-integrability of
\bar{v},u^{\star}
.

Proposition 17
(Lower bound and properness of objective)

Under Assumption
0..16
, for all
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
,

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}dt-C_{u^{\star},\mu_{0},\mu_{T},T},

for a finite constant
C_{u^{\star},\mu_{0},\mu_{T},T}
independent of
v
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}
is proper and coercive in
v
-energy.

Proof

Use Young pointwise:

|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}-|u^{\star}|^{2}.

Integrating:

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}\geq\frac{1}{4}\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt-\frac{1}{2}\int|u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt.

By linear-growth envelope and moment bound,

\int|u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\left(a_{t}^{2}+b_{t}^{2}\,m_{2}(\mu_{t})\right)dt\leq C_{u^{\star},\mu_{0},\mu_{T},T}<\infty.

Hence the claim.

Proposition 18
(Convexity in velocity for fixed path)

Fix an admissible measure curve
\mu
. Then

v\mapsto\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)

is strictly convex on
L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t})
, with Gâteaux derivative

D_{v}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)[w]=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int(v_{t}-u_{t}^{\star})\cdot w_{t}\,d\mu_{t}dt.

Proof

Quadratic form in Hilbert space
L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t})
:

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v+\epsilon w)=\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)+\epsilon\!\int(v-u^{\star})\cdot w\,d\mu dt+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\!\int|w|^{2}\,d\mu dt.

Strict convexity follows from positive definite second variation

\int|w|^{2}\,d\mu dt>0
for
w\neq 0
.

Definition 21
(Lagrangian density with entropy multiplier)

For formal multiplier
\eta_{t}\geq 0
, define instantaneous constrained density

\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{t},v_{t};\eta_{t})=\frac{1}{2}\int|v_{t}-u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}+\eta_{t}\!\left(-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})-\lambda\right).

Its rigorous weak-time form is developed in
C.2
–
C.5
.

Theorem 0..17
(Existence of minimizer for
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
)

Assume:

1.

Assumption
0..16
;

2.

\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})\neq\emptyset
;

3.

sequential closedness of entropy-rate constraint:
if
(\mu^{n},v^{n})\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
,
\mu^{n}_{t}\rightharpoonup\mu_{t}
for each
t
,

v^{n}\mu^{n}\rightharpoonup v\mu
weakly as vector measures, and

\sup_{n}\int|v^{n}|^{2}d\mu^{n}dt<\infty
, then
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
.

Then
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
admits at least one minimizer.

Proof

Take minimizing sequence
(\mu^{n},v^{n})\subset\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
with

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu^{n},v^{n})\downarrow\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

By Proposition
17
,

\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}^{n}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{n}dt<\infty.

Moment bounds (
A.2
/
A.3
) give tightness of
\{\mu_{t}^{n}\}_{n}
for each
t
,
and equi-continuity in
W_{2}
from kinetic bound. By diagonal extraction,

\mu_{t}^{n}\rightharpoonup\mu_{t}\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

Momentum compactness yields (up to subsequence)

m^{n}:=v^{n}\mu^{n}\rightharpoonup m=v\mu

as vector measures on
(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Passing to the limit in CE gives
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
.

By Assumption
0..19
entropy-rate feasibility is closed:

(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
.

Lower semicontinuity of convex integral functional

(\mu,m)\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\int|m-\mu u^{\star}|^{2}/\mu
(with standard convention)
gives

\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu^{n},v^{n})=\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

Hence
(\mu,v)
is a minimizer.

Corollary 10
(Unconstrained FM as
\lambda=\infty
formal limit)

If entropy constraint is removed (formally
\lambda=\infty
),

\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda},\quad\forall\lambda<\infty.

Thus entropy control is a feasible-set restriction that can only increase (or keep) optimal value.

Proof

\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathfrak{A}
, hence taking infimum on a subset yields larger value.

Output of
C.1
for subsequent subsections.

C.2
introduces the rigorous constrained Lagrangian in weak-time form;

C.3
–
C.5
derive Euler–Lagrange/KKT, dual, and Pontryagin systems for

(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
.

C.2. Lagrangian with entropy constraint

We now write the constrained problem
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
as a saddle problem with:
(i) a scalar multiplier for the entropy-rate inequality, and
(ii) a space-time adjoint potential for the continuity equation.

Primal problem (recall).

\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu\,dt:\ \dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})+\lambda\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}\right\}.

Definition 22
(Weak entropy-rate residual)

For
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
with
\mathcal{H}(\mu_{\cdot})\in AC([0,T])
,
define

r_{\mathrm{ent}}(t):=\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})+\lambda\in L^{1}(0,T).

Constraint is
r_{\mathrm{ent}}(t)\geq 0
a.e.

Definition 23
(Multiplier classes)

Define

\mathcal{M}_{+}:=L^{\infty}_{+}(0,T):=\{\eta\in L^{\infty}(0,T):\eta(t)\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}\},

and adjoint potentials

\Phi:=\Big\{\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\Big\}

for weak enforcement of CE.
(Regularity will be relaxed later by density.)

Definition 24
(Augmented Lagrangian)

For
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
,
(\eta,\varphi)\in\mathcal{M}_{+}\times\Phi
, define

\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu\,dt-\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,r_{\mathrm{ent}}(t)\,dt+\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mu,v;\varphi),

where

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mu,v;\varphi):=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\big(\partial_{t}\varphi+\nabla\varphi\cdot v\big)\,d\mu\,dt+\int\varphi(0,\cdot)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\varphi(T,\cdot)\,d\mu_{T}.

For CE-feasible pairs,
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mu,v;\varphi)=0
.

Remark 6
(Sign convention)

Since constraint is
r_{\mathrm{ent}}\geq 0
, the penalty term is

-\int\eta r_{\mathrm{ent}}
with
\eta\geq 0
.
Thus any violation
r_{\mathrm{ent}}<0
can be penalized arbitrarily by large
\eta
.

Lemma 11
(Integration-by-parts form of entropy term)

For
\eta\in W^{1,\infty}(0,T)
,
(\mu,v)
with
\mathcal{H}(\mu_{\cdot})\in AC
,

-\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt=-\eta(T)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})+\eta(0)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})+\int_{0}^{T}\eta^{\prime}(t)\,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Hence

-\int_{0}^{T}\eta\,r_{\mathrm{ent}}\,dt=-\eta(T)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})+\eta(0)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})+\int_{0}^{T}\eta^{\prime}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\,dt-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,dt.

Proof

Apply one-dimensional integration by parts to
h(t):=\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\in AC([0,T])
:

\int_{0}^{T}\eta\dot{h}=\eta(T)h(T)-\eta(0)h(0)-\int_{0}^{T}\eta^{\prime}h.

Multiply by
-1
, then add
-\lambda\int\eta
.

Proposition 19
(Equivalent saddle representation)

Define

\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}:=\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}),\,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{\cdot})\in AC}\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{+},\ \varphi\in\Phi}\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi).

Then

\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}=\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

Proof

Fix
(\mu,v)
. If CE fails, there exists
\varphi\in\Phi
with

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mu,v;\varphi)\neq 0
; scaling
\alpha\varphi
and taking
\alpha\to\pm\infty

shows
\sup_{\varphi}\mathscr{L}=+\infty
. So finite value requires CE.

Assume CE holds. Then

\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{+}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt-\int\eta\,r_{\mathrm{ent}}dt\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt,&r_{\mathrm{ent}}\geq 0\ \text{a.e.},\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

because if
r_{\mathrm{ent}}<0
on a set of positive measure, choose
\eta
large on that set.
Hence the saddle objective equals primal cost exactly on feasible set
\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
,
and
+\infty
outside. Taking infimum gives
\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}
.

Theorem 0..18
(Weak dual lower bound)

Define dual value

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}:=\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{M}_{+},\ \varphi\in\Phi}\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}),\,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{\cdot})\in AC}\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi).

Then

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

Proof

For any
(\eta,\varphi)
,

\inf_{(\mu,v)}\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi)\leq\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}}\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi).

For feasible
(\mu,v)
, CE term vanishes and
-\int\eta r_{\mathrm{ent}}\leq 0
, so

\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi)\leq\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt.

Taking inf over feasible gives

\inf_{(\mu,v)}\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta,\varphi)\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

Now take supremum over
(\eta,\varphi)
, obtaining
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}
.

Proposition 20
(Pointwise minimization in
v
for fixed
(\mu,\eta,\varphi)
)

Fix
\mu,\eta,\varphi
and assume
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})
depends on
v
via

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})=\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}\cdot v_{t}\,d\mu_{t}\quad(\rho_{t}=d\mu_{t}/dx),

as in B.1 (smooth regime). Then the
v
-dependent part of
\mathscr{L}
is

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\left[\frac{1}{2}|v-u^{\star}|^{2}+\nabla\varphi\cdot v-\eta\,\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\right]d\mu dt,

whose unique minimizer is

v^{\sharp}=u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi+\eta\,\nabla\log\rho.

Proof

For fixed
(t,x)
, minimize strictly convex quadratic function

q(v)=\frac{1}{2}|v-u^{\star}|^{2}+(\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho)\cdot v.

First-order condition:

\nabla_{v}q=v-u^{\star}+\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho=0,

thus

v^{\sharp}=u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi+\eta\nabla\log\rho.

Strict convexity yields uniqueness.

Corollary 11
(Formal entropic correction structure)

At stationarity, the optimal drift decomposes as

v^{\star}=u^{\star}+v_{\mathrm{adj}}+v_{\mathrm{ent}},\quad v_{\mathrm{adj}}=-\nabla\varphi,\quad v_{\mathrm{ent}}=\eta\,\nabla\log\rho.

Thus the entropy constraint induces a score-direction correction weighted by
\eta\geq 0
.

Proof

Immediate from Proposition
20
.

Remark 7
(Regularity needed for rigorous KKT)

The explicit formula in Proposition
20
is formal unless

\rho>0
,
\nabla\log\rho\in L^{2}(\mu)
, and admissible differentiation under integral holds.
Section
C.3
states the rigorous Euler–Lagrange/KKT system via variational inequalities,
with smooth formulas recovered under additional regularity.

Output used next.

C.3
uses
\mathscr{L}
and weak duality to derive:
(i) primal feasibility, (ii) dual feasibility
\eta\geq 0
,
(iii) complementary slackness
\eta(\dot{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda)=0
,
(iv) stationarity with respect to
v
and
\mu
.

C.3. Constrained optimization in Wasserstein space

We derive the rigorous first-order optimality system (KKT conditions) for

(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
in measure space: primal feasibility, dual feasibility,
complementary slackness, and stationarity with respect to velocity and path perturbations.

Assumption 0..19
(Qualification and regularity for KKT)

Assume:

1.

(Slater-type condition) there exists
(\bar{\mu},\bar{v})\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
with

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{\mu}_{t})+\lambda\geq\delta>0\quad\text{a.e. }t;

2.

the map
(\mu,v)\mapsto\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\mu,v)
is convex in
v
, l.s.c. in
(\mu,m=v\mu)
;

3.

entropy-rate mapping
(\mu,v)\mapsto\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu)
is weakly closed on admissible sequences
(as used in C.1 existence theorem).

Definition 25
(Feasible cone and critical directions)

Let
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
.
A perturbation
(\delta\mu,\delta v)
is an admissible first-order direction if:

1.

linearized CE holds:

\partial_{t}\delta\mu+\nabla\!\cdot(\delta\mu\,v^{\star}+\mu^{\star}\delta v)=0,\qquad\delta\mu_{|t=0}=\delta\mu_{|t=T}=0;

2.

linearized entropy-rate is feasible on active set:

\frac{d}{dt}\Big[\delta\mathcal{H}_{t}\Big]\geq 0\quad\text{a.e. on }\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda=0\},

where

\delta\mathcal{H}_{t}=\int\big(1+\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\big)\,d(\delta\mu_{t})\quad(\mu_{t}^{\star}=\rho_{t}^{\star}dx).

Theorem 0..20
(KKT conditions in measure space)

Under Assumption
0..19
, if
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})
solves

(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
, then there exist multipliers

\eta^{\star}\in L^{\infty}_{+}(0,T),\qquad\varphi^{\star}\in W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})

such that:

1.

Primal feasibility

(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\in\mathfrak{A}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}),\qquad\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}

2.

Dual feasibility

\eta^{\star}(t)\geq 0\quad\text{a.e. }t.

3.

Complementary slackness

\eta^{\star}(t)\,\big(\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\big)=0\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in(0,T).

4.

Stationarity in velocity (variational form)

for every
\delta v\in L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t}^{\star})
compatible with linearized CE,

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\Big[v_{t}^{\star}-u_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\Big]\cdot\delta v_{t}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}dt=0.

5.

Stationarity in path (adjoint inequality/equality)

for every admissible
\delta\mu
,

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\Big[\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}+\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star}+\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}\Big]\,d(\delta\mu_{t})\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\eta_{t}^{\star}\,\frac{d}{dt}\delta\mathcal{H}_{t}\,dt=0.

Proof

By Slater condition and convexity/closedness hypotheses, infinite-dimensional
Karush–Kuhn–Tucker theorem
[
23
]
applies to the saddle Lagrangian from
C.2
.
Hence there exist multipliers
(\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})
such that

(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})
minimizes

(\mu,v)\mapsto\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})

over admissible CE trajectories, and
(\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})
maximizes
dual functional over
\eta\geq 0
, test potentials.

Primal and dual feasibility
are direct by construction.

Complementary slackness:

Since
\eta^{\star}\geq 0
and
r^{\star}:=\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu^{\star})+\lambda\geq 0
,
optimality of
\eta^{\star}
in

\sup_{\eta\geq 0}\left(-\int\eta r^{\star}\right)

implies zero maximum; if
r^{\star}>0
on a set where
\eta^{\star}>0
, reducing

\eta^{\star}
improves objective; if
r^{\star}<0
anywhere primal infeasible.
Thus
\eta^{\star}r^{\star}=0
a.e.

Stationarity in
v
:

Take perturbation
v^{\star}+\epsilon\delta v
with CE-compatible first-order correction.
Differentiate
\mathscr{L}(\mu^{\star},\cdot;\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})
at
\epsilon=0
;
the derivative must vanish:

0=\int(v^{\star}-u^{\star})\cdot\delta v\,d\mu^{\star}dt+\int\nabla\varphi^{\star}\cdot\delta v\,d\mu^{\star}dt-\int\eta^{\star}\,\delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\,dt.

Using entropy first variation

\delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}=\int\nabla\log\rho^{\star}\cdot\delta v\,d\mu^{\star}

gives stated identity.

Stationarity in
\mu
:

For admissible
\mu^{\star}+\epsilon\delta\mu
, derivative at
\epsilon=0
vanishes.
Differentiating CE-constraint term yields

\int(\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}+\nabla\varphi^{\star}\cdot v^{\star})\,d(\delta\mu)\,dt

plus objective and entropy terms, giving stated path-stationarity identity.

Corollary 12
(Pointwise velocity law on regular set)

Assume
\rho_{t}^{\star}>0
a.e. and

\nabla\varphi^{\star},\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\in L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t}^{\star})
.
Then Theorem
0..20
(4) implies

v_{t}^{\star}=u_{t}^{\star}-\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}+\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\mu_{t}^{\star})\ \text{for a.e. }t.

Proof

The variational identity holds for all
\delta v\in L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t}^{\star})
, hence
the bracket must vanish
dt\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}
-a.e.

Proposition 21
(Active/inactive time partition)

Define

\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{act}}:=\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda=0\},\qquad\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}}:=\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda>0\}.

Then:

\eta_{t}^{\star}=0\ \text{a.e. on }\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}},

and on
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{act}}
,
\eta_{t}^{\star}
may be nonzero and the entropy constraint
acts as an equality constraint.

Proof

Immediate from complementary slackness:

\eta^{\star}(\dot{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda)=0\ \text{a.e.}

If
\dot{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda>0
, must have
\eta^{\star}=0
.

Lemma 12
(Reduced optimality system in inactive region)

On
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}}
, optimal velocity satisfies

v_{t}^{\star}=u_{t}^{\star}-\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}.

Hence entropy control modifies dynamics only on active times.

Proof

From Proposition
21
,
\eta^{\star}=0
a.e. on inactive set.
Insert into Corollary
12
.

Theorem 0..21
(Second-order sufficient condition in velocity block)

Fix
\mu^{\star}
. For any feasible perturbation
\delta v\neq 0
,

\delta^{2}_{vv}\mathscr{L}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star};\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})[\delta v,\delta v]=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\delta v_{t}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}dt>0.

Therefore
v^{\star}
is unique given
(\mu^{\star},\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})
.

Proof

Only quadratic term in
v
contributes second variation:

\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu\ \Longrightarrow\ \delta^{2}=\int|\delta v|^{2}\,d\mu.

CE/entropy contributions are affine in first-order velocity variation at fixed
\mu^{\star}
,
hence no additional quadratic term. Positivity gives strict convexity and uniqueness.

Remark 8
(What Section
C.3. Constrained optimization in Wasserstein space
establishes)

C.3
provides the rigorous constrained-optimality backbone:

\text{primal feasibility}+\text{dual feasibility}+\text{complementary slackness}+\text{stationarity}.

C.4
converts these conditions into Euler–Lagrange PDE form;
C.5
then derives the
explicit dual problem.

C.4. Euler–Lagrange conditions

We convert the variational KKT relations from
C.3
into a PDE optimality system
(continuity + adjoint + Hamiltonian stationarity + complementarity), and provide
a weak formulation valid under finite-energy regularity.

Assumption 0..22
(Differentiable regime for PDE form)

Assume the optimal triple
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star},\eta^{\star})
from
C.3
satisfies:

1.

\mu_{t}^{\star}=\rho_{t}^{\star}dx
,
\rho_{t}^{\star}>0
,
\rho^{\star}\in C^{1}_{t}C^{2}_{x}
;

2.

v^{\star},u^{\star},\nabla\log\rho^{\star},\nabla\varphi^{\star}\in L^{2}(dt\,d\mu_{t}^{\star})
;

3.

entropy derivative identity holds:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})=\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\cdot v_{t}^{\star}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}\quad\text{a.e. }t;

4.

endpoint constraints
\mu^{\star}_{|0}=\mu_{0}
,
\mu^{\star}_{|T}=\mu_{T}
.

Definition 26
(Hamiltonian density)

For
(t,x,\rho,v,p,\eta)
with
\rho>0
, define

\mathfrak{h}(t,x,\rho,v,p,\eta):=\frac{1}{2}|v-u^{\star}_{t}(x)|^{2}\,\rho+p\,\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v)-\eta\,\nabla\!\cdot(\rho\nabla\log\rho\,v\text{-linearized form}),

where the entropy term is interpreted via first variation:

-\eta\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}=-\eta\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,\rho\,dx.

Equivalent integrated Hamiltonian:

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{opt}}(\rho,v,\varphi,\eta)=\int\Big[\frac{1}{2}|v-u^{\star}|^{2}+\nabla\varphi\cdot v-\eta\,\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\Big]\rho\,dx.

Theorem 0..23
(Euler–Lagrange/KKT PDE system)

Under Assumption
0..22
, there exists adjoint potential

\varphi^{\star}
such that
(\rho^{\star},v^{\star},\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star})
satisfies:

(i) State equation (continuity)

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}^{\star}v_{t}^{\star})=0,\qquad\rho^{\star}_{|t=0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho^{\star}_{|t=T}=\rho_{T}.

(ii) Stationarity in control

v_{t}^{\star}=u_{t}^{\star}-\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}+\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\quad\text{a.e. in }(t,x).

(iii) Adjoint equation (weak form)

for every
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\Big[-\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star}-\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}+\eta^{\star}\,\Xi(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})\Big]\zeta\,dx\,dt=0,

where

\Xi(\rho,v):=\frac{\delta}{\delta\rho}\!\left(\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,\rho\,dx\right)

(the entropy-rate density variation; explicit smooth expression below).

(iv) Complementarity

\eta_{t}^{\star}\geq 0,\qquad\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\geq 0,\qquad\eta_{t}^{\star}\big(\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\big)=0\quad\text{a.e. }t.

Proof

(i),(iv) are primal/dual feasibility and slackness from
C.3
.

(ii) follows from velocity-stationarity:

\int(v^{\star}-u^{\star}+\nabla\varphi^{\star}-\eta^{\star}\nabla\log\rho^{\star})\cdot\delta v\,d\mu^{\star}dt=0

for all
\delta v
, implying pointwise identity in
L^{2}(\mu^{\star})
.

(iii) For admissible density perturbations
\rho^{\star}+\varepsilon\zeta
(mass-preserving, compactly supported
in time interior), derivative of Lagrangian at
\varepsilon=0
vanishes.
Term-by-term:

\delta_{\rho}\!\left(\frac{1}{2}\int|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}\rho\,dxdt\right)=\int\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}\zeta,

\delta_{\rho}\!\left(\int(\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}+\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star})\rho\,dxdt\right)=\int(\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}+\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star})\zeta,

and entropy multiplier contributes

-\int\eta^{\star}\,\delta_{\rho}(\dot{\mathcal{H}})\,dt=\int\eta^{\star}\,\Xi(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})\zeta.

Summing and setting to zero gives weak adjoint equation.

Proposition 22
(Explicit entropy variation term
\Xi
in smooth regime)

Assume
\rho>0
,
v\in C^{1}_{x}
,
\rho\in C^{2}_{x}
. Then

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho,v)=\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,\rho\,dx=\int\nabla\rho\cdot v\,dx=-\int\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot v\,dx.

Hence first variation in
\rho
is

\Xi(\rho,v)=-\nabla\!\cdot v.

Therefore adjoint equation in Theorem
0..23
(iii) simplifies to

-\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star}-\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}-\eta^{\star}\,\nabla\!\cdot v^{\star}=0\quad\text{(weakly)}.

Proof

Identity

\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,\rho=\int\nabla\rho\cdot v=-\int\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot v

follows by integration by parts.
This is linear in
\rho
, thus Gateaux derivative w.r.t.
\rho
in direction
\zeta
is

\delta_{\rho}\dot{\mathcal{H}}[\zeta]=-\int\zeta\,\nabla\!\cdot v\,dx,

so
\Xi(\rho,v)=-\nabla\!\cdot v
.
Substitute into Theorem
0..23
(iii).

Corollary 13
(Closed optimality system)

In the smooth regime, optimality conditions are:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho^{\star}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho^{\star}v^{\star})=0,\\[3.00003pt]
v^{\star}=u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}+\eta^{\star}\nabla\log\rho^{\star},\\[3.00003pt]
-\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star}-\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}-\eta^{\star}\nabla\!\cdot v^{\star}=0,\\[3.00003pt]
\eta^{\star}\geq 0,\ \dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\geq 0,\ \eta^{\star}(\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda)=0.\end{cases}

with boundary conditions
\rho^{\star}(0)=\rho_{0},\ \rho^{\star}(T)=\rho_{T}
.

Proof

Collect Theorem
0..23
and Proposition
22
.

Theorem 0..24
(Inactive-region reduction)

On any time interval
I\subset(0,T)
where

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda>0\quad\text{a.e. }t\in I,

we have
\eta^{\star}=0
a.e. on
I
, and the EL system reduces to

\partial_{t}\rho^{\star}+\nabla\!\cdot\!\big(\rho^{\star}(u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star})\big)=0,

-\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot(u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star})-\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi^{\star}|^{2}=0.

Proof

Complementary slackness gives
\eta^{\star}=0
where constraint is inactive.
Substitute into Corollary
13
.
Since
v^{\star}-u^{\star}=-\nabla\varphi^{\star}
, we get

\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi^{\star}|^{2}

and the displayed reduced equations follow.

Theorem 0..25
(Active-region entropy-saturated dynamics)

On any interval
I\subset(0,T)
where
\eta^{\star}>0
a.e., constraint is saturated:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})=-\lambda\quad\text{a.e. }t\in I.

Equivalently,

\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\cdot v_{t}^{\star}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}=-\lambda.

Using stationarity
v^{\star}=u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}+\eta^{\star}\nabla\log\rho^{\star}
,

\int\nabla\log\rho^{\star}\cdot\big(u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}\big)\,d\mu^{\star}+\eta^{\star}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}^{\star})=-\lambda.

Proof

From complementary slackness,
\eta^{\star}>0\Rightarrow\dot{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda=0
.
The entropy derivative identity gives the first integral form.
Substitute velocity law and split inner products; definition of Fisher information

\mathcal{I}(\mu)=\int|\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\,d\mu
gives last formula.

Remark 9
(Interpretation of EL structure)

The multiplier
\eta^{\star}
modulates a score-direction correction

\eta^{\star}\nabla\log\rho^{\star}
, activated exactly when entropy decay reaches the
budget boundary. This is the analytic mechanism by which entropy control counteracts
collapse-prone compression in the primal FM drift
u^{\star}
.

Next step.

C.5
converts this EL/KKT system into an explicit dual functional and dual constraints,
preparing the Pontryagin formulation in
C.6
.

C.5. Dual formulation

We derive a rigorous convex dual for
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
in flux variables,
identify the dual constraints, and state strong duality under qualification.

Primal in
(\rho,m)
-variables.

Let
m_{t}:=\rho_{t}v_{t}
(momentum/flux). Then

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot m_{t}=0,\qquad\rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho_{|T}=\rho_{T},

and

\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu=\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho}\,dxdt

(with convention
+\infty
when
\rho=0
but
m\neq 0
).
The entropy-rate term (smooth regime) is

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})=-\int\rho_{t}\,\nabla\!\cdot v_{t}\,dx=-\int\nabla\!\cdot m_{t}\,dx+\int\frac{m_{t}\cdot\nabla\rho_{t}}{\rho_{t}}\,dx,

and operationally enforced via multiplier
\eta
as in
C.2
–
C.4
.

Definition 27
(Convex integrand and conjugate)

Define, for
\rho\geq 0,\ m\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

f_{u^{\star}}(\rho,m):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho},&\rho>0,\\[3.44444pt]
0,&\rho=0,\ m=0,\\
+\infty,&\rho=0,\ m\neq 0.\end{cases}

Its convex conjugate in
(\rho,m)
variables at
(a,b)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

f_{u^{\star}}^{\ast}(a,b)=\sup_{\rho\geq 0,m}\{a\rho+b\!\cdot m-f_{u^{\star}}(\rho,m)\}.

Lemma 13
(Pointwise conjugate formula)

For fixed
u^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

f_{u^{\star}}^{\ast}(a,b)=\begin{cases}0,&a+u^{\star}\!\cdot b+\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2}\leq 0,\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof

For
\rho>0
, write
m=\rho(u^{\star}+q)
. Then

a\rho+b\!\cdot m-\frac{1}{2}\frac{|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho}=\rho\!\left(a+b\!\cdot u^{\star}+b\!\cdot q-\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}\right).

Sup over
q
gives
\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2}
, hence

\sup_{m}\{\cdots\}=\rho\!\left(a+u^{\star}\!\cdot b+\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2}\right).

Sup over
\rho\geq 0
:
if bracket
\leq 0
, maximum is
0
at
\rho=0
;
if bracket
>0
, supremum is
+\infty
.

Definition 28
(Dual variables)

Let

\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad\eta\in L^{\infty}_{+}(0,T),

where
\varphi
enforces CE and
\eta
enforces entropy-rate inequality.
Define

b_{\eta}(t,x,\rho):=\nabla\varphi(t,x)-\eta(t)\nabla\log\rho(t,x).

Proposition 23
(Lagrangian infimum in primal variables)

For fixed
(\eta,\varphi)
, the infimum over
(\rho,m)
of

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\Big[f_{u^{\star}_{t}(x)}(\rho,m)+\partial_{t}\varphi\,\rho+\nabla\varphi\!\cdot m-\eta\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho;m)\Big]dxdt+\text{endpoint terms}-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,dt

equals
+\infty
unless the pointwise Hamilton–Jacobi-type inequality holds:

\partial_{t}\varphi+u^{\star}\!\cdot b_{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}|b_{\eta}|^{2}\leq 0\quad\text{a.e.},

in which case the infimum equals the boundary/entropy terms:

\int\varphi(0,\cdot)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\varphi(T,\cdot)\,d\mu_{T}+\eta(0)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})-\eta(T)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\eta^{\prime}(t)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\,dt

(weak-time form, cf. Lemma
11
).

Proof

Use conjugate computation pointwise with
b=b_{\eta}
,
a=\partial_{t}\varphi

after CE integration by parts and entropy integration by parts in time.
By Lemma
13
, finite infimum requires

\partial_{t}\varphi+u^{\star}\!\cdot b_{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}|b_{\eta}|^{2}\leq 0.

If violated on positive measure set, primal infimum is
-\infty
in saddle convention
(or dual value
+\infty
with opposite sign), hence infeasible.
When satisfied, bulk term collapses to
0
, leaving boundary/time-multiplier terms.

Remark 10
(Eliminating
\mu_{t}
-dependent entropy boundary term)

A purely explicit dual is obtained either by:

1.

restricting
\eta
to
W^{1,\infty}
with
\eta(0)=\eta(T)=0
, removing endpoint entropy terms;

2.

or augmenting dual with a scalar state
s(t)=\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})
and its own adjoint.

In this appendix we use the first route for clean closed dual constraints.

Definition 29
(Reduced dual admissible set)

Define

\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}:=\left\{(\varphi,\eta):\begin{array}[]{l}\varphi\in W^{1,\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),\\
\eta\in W^{1,\infty}(0,T),\ \eta\geq 0,\ \eta(0)=\eta(T)=0,\\
\partial_{t}\varphi+u^{\star}\!\cdot\nabla\varphi+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}-\eta\,\mathfrak{E}(\rho,\varphi,u^{\star})\leq 0\ \text{a.e.}\end{array}\right\},

where
\mathfrak{E}
is the entropy-correction contribution induced by

b_{\eta}=\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho
(expanded below in smooth regime).

Lemma 14
(Smooth expansion of dual Hamiltonian constraint)

In smooth positive-density regime,

u^{\star}\!\cdot b_{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}|b_{\eta}|^{2}=u^{\star}\!\cdot\nabla\varphi+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}-\eta\,(u^{\star}+\nabla\varphi)\!\cdot\nabla\log\rho+\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}|\nabla\log\rho|^{2}.

Hence the dual inequality is

\partial_{t}\varphi+u^{\star}\!\cdot\nabla\varphi+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}-\eta\,(u^{\star}+\nabla\varphi)\!\cdot\nabla\log\rho+\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}|\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\leq 0.

Proof

Direct expansion of
b_{\eta}=\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho
:

|b_{\eta}|^{2}=|\nabla\varphi|^{2}-2\eta\nabla\varphi\!\cdot\nabla\log\rho+\eta^{2}|\nabla\log\rho|^{2},

u^{\star}\!\cdot b_{\eta}=u^{\star}\!\cdot\nabla\varphi-\eta u^{\star}\!\cdot\nabla\log\rho.

Sum terms.

Theorem 0..26
(Dual problem and weak duality)

Define reduced dual value

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{red}}:=\sup_{(\varphi,\eta)\in\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}}\left\{\int\varphi(0,\cdot)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\varphi(T,\cdot)\,d\mu_{T}-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,dt\right\}.

Then

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{red}}\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

Proof

For any primal feasible
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
and any dual feasible

(\varphi,\eta)\in\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
, Fenchel inequality and CE/entropy feasibility give

\int\varphi(0)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\varphi(T)\,d\mu_{T}-\lambda\int\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt.

Take infimum over primal feasible and then supremum over dual feasible.

Theorem 0..27
(Strong duality under qualification)

Assume:

1.

Slater condition from Assumption
0..19
;

2.

convexity and l.s.c. of primal integrand in
(\rho,m)
;

3.

closedness of CE and entropy-rate constraints in the topology used for
C.1
existence.

Then

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{red}}=\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}.

Moreover, any primal-dual optimal pair
(\rho^{\star},m^{\star};\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star})

satisfies saddle-point conditions and KKT system from
C.3
–
C.4
.

Proof

Apply Fenchel–Rockafellar duality to the sum

\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{CE+bc}}(\rho,m)+\mathbf{1}_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}+\lambda\geq 0}(\rho,m)+\int f_{u^{\star}}(\rho,m),

with linear operator encoding CE and entropy-rate map.
Qualification (Slater) prevents duality gap; lower semicontinuity/properness ensure attainment
(up to standard coercivity/tightness). Dual functional is exactly the reduced form after eliminating
primal variables via conjugacy (Lemma
13
).
KKT follows from saddle-point optimality.

Corollary 14
(Dual certificate of optimality)

If
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
and
(\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star})\in\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
satisfy

\int\varphi^{\star}(0)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\varphi^{\star}(T)\,d\mu_{T}-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta^{\star}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu^{\star}dt,

then
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})
is primal optimal and
(\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star})
dual optimal.

Proof

By weak duality, dual objective
\leq
primal objective for all feasible pairs.
Equality for one feasible pair implies both attain respective optima.

Output used next.

C.6
(Pontryagin formulation) rewrites this dual-constraint structure as a measure-valued
maximum principle with Hamiltonian maximization and adjoint transport equations.

C.6. Pontryagin formulation in measure space

We rewrite
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
as an infinite-dimensional optimal control problem
on
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, derive the measure-space Pontryagin Maximum Principle (PMP),
and show equivalence with the KKT/EL system from
C.3
–
C.5
.

State-control form.

State:
\mu_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{ac}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, control:
v_{t}\in L^{2}(\mu_{t};\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Dynamics:

\dot{\mu}_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}v_{t})=0,\qquad\mu_{|0}=\mu_{0},\ \mu_{|T}=\mu_{T}.

Path inequality:

g(\mu_{t},v_{t}):=\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})+\lambda\geq 0\quad\text{a.e. }t.

Running cost:

\ell(\mu_{t},v_{t}):=\frac{1}{2}\int|v_{t}-u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}.

Definition 30
(Measure-space Hamiltonian)

Let adjoint potential
\varphi_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and entropy multiplier
\eta_{t}\geq 0
.
Define

\mathbf{H}(t,\mu,v,\varphi,\eta):=-\ell(\mu,v)+\int\nabla\varphi(x)\cdot v(x)\,d\mu(x)-\eta\,\Big(\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu;v)+\lambda\Big),

where in smooth regime

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu;v)=\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,d\mu,\qquad\mu=\rho dx.

Thus

\mathbf{H}=-\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu+\int(\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho)\cdot v\,d\mu-\eta\lambda.

Proposition 24
(Pointwise maximizer of Hamiltonian in control)

Fix
(t,\mu,\varphi,\eta)
with
\eta\geq 0
,
\mu=\rho dx
,
\rho>0
.
Then
v\mapsto\mathbf{H}(t,\mu,v,\varphi,\eta)
is strictly concave on
L^{2}(\mu)
, and
its unique maximizer is

v^{\mathrm{PMP}}=u^{\star}+\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho.

Proof

\mathbf{H}
is negative quadratic in
v
:

\mathbf{H}(v)=-\frac{1}{2}\|v-u^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\langle\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho,\ v\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\eta\lambda.

First variation:

D_{v}\mathbf{H}[w]=-\int(v-u^{\star})\cdot w\,d\mu+\int(\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho)\cdot w\,d\mu.

Setting
D_{v}\mathbf{H}=0
for all
w
yields

v=u^{\star}+\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho.

Second variation is

D^{2}_{vv}\mathbf{H}[w,w]=-\int|w|^{2}\,d\mu<0\quad(w\neq 0),

hence strict concavity and uniqueness.

Remark 11
(Sign convention vs. App.
C.4. Euler–Lagrange conditions
)

If one uses a minimization Hamiltonian
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}=+\ell+\cdots
, stationarity gives

v=u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi+\eta\nabla\log\rho
(
C.4
).
Both are equivalent under
\varphi\mapsto-\varphi
.

Definition 31
(Costate equation in weak form)

Let
\mu^{\star},v^{\star}
be optimal. A costate
\varphi^{\star}
satisfies:
for all
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\left[-\partial_{t}\varphi^{\star}-\nabla\varphi^{\star}\!\cdot v^{\star}-\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}-\eta^{\star}\,\nabla\!\cdot v^{\star}\right]\zeta\,dxdt=0,

with endpoint transversality induced by fixed end marginals
(no free-endpoint costate boundary term).

Theorem 0..28
(Pontryagin Maximum Principle with entropy-rate path constraint)

Assume
C.1
existence hypotheses and
C.3
qualification.
If
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})
is optimal for
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
, then there exist

\varphi^{\star}
and
\eta^{\star}\in L^{\infty}_{+}(0,T)
such that:

1.

State equation

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}^{\star}v_{t}^{\star})=0,\quad\mu_{0}^{\star}=\mu_{0},\ \mu_{T}^{\star}=\mu_{T}.

2.

Hamiltonian maximization

\mathbf{H}(t,\mu_{t}^{\star},v_{t}^{\star},\varphi_{t}^{\star},\eta_{t}^{\star})=\max_{v\in L^{2}(\mu_{t}^{\star})}\mathbf{H}(t,\mu_{t}^{\star},v,\varphi_{t}^{\star},\eta_{t}^{\star})\quad\text{a.e. }t.

3.

Costate equation

\varphi^{\star}\ \text{satisfies Definition~\ref{def:C6_costate_weak}}.

4.

Multiplier conditions

\eta_{t}^{\star}\geq 0,\qquad\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\geq 0,\qquad\eta_{t}^{\star}\big(\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda\big)=0\quad\text{a.e. }t.

Conversely, any quadruple
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star},\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star})
satisfying
these PMP conditions and a global saddle property is primal optimal.

Proof

From
C.5
strong duality and saddle-point existence, optimal pair satisfies KKT.
Define Hamiltonian
\mathbf{H}
from Definition
30
.
Stationarity in
v
from KKT is equivalent to Hamiltonian maximization
because
\mathbf{H}
is strictly concave in
v
(Proposition
24
).
Path-stationarity in
\mu
is exactly weak costate equation.
Complementarity and feasibility coincide with multiplier conditions.

For the converse: if PMP conditions hold and

\mathscr{L}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star};\eta,\varphi)\leq\mathscr{L}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star};\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})\leq\mathscr{L}(\mu,v;\eta^{\star},\varphi^{\star})

for all feasible primal/dual variables, then
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})
minimizes primal objective.

Corollary 15
(Reduced feedback law on active/inactive sets)

Define

\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{act}}=\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda=0\},\qquad\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}}=\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda>0\}.

Then

\eta^{\star}=0\ \text{a.e. on }\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}},

and feedback laws are

v_{t}^{\star}=\begin{cases}u_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star},&t\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}},\\[3.00003pt]
u_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star},&t\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{act}}.\end{cases}

(Equivalent
C.4
sign convention:
\nabla\varphi\mapsto-\nabla\varphi
.)

Proof

Complementary slackness gives
\eta^{\star}=0
on inactive set.
Insert into maximizer formula of Proposition
24
.

Proposition 25
(Hamiltonian value at optimum)

Let
v^{\star}
be the maximizer from Proposition
24
. Then

\mathbf{H}^{\star}(t):=\mathbf{H}(t,\mu_{t}^{\star},v_{t}^{\star},\varphi_{t}^{\star},\eta_{t}^{\star})=\frac{1}{2}\int\left|\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\right|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}+\int u_{t}^{\star}\!\cdot\left(\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\right)d\mu_{t}^{\star}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\lambda.

Proof

Let
a:=\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho
. Since
v^{\star}=u^{\star}+a
,

-\frac{1}{2}|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}+a\cdot v^{\star}=-\frac{1}{2}|a|^{2}+a\cdot(u^{\star}+a)=\frac{1}{2}|a|^{2}+u^{\star}\cdot a.

Integrate against
\mu
, then subtract
\eta\lambda
.

Theorem 0..29
(Equivalence of PMP and KKT/EL systems)

Under regularity where both are meaningful, the following are equivalent:

1.

KKT/EL system from
C.3
–
C.4
;

2.

PMP system from Theorem
0..28
.

Therefore
C.3
–
C.6
provide equivalent primal-dual characterizations of
entropy-controlled flow matching.

Proof

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
:
KKT stationarity in
v
yields explicit optimizer of strictly concave Hamiltonian,
hence maximization condition.
Path stationarity is costate equation; complementarity/feasibility unchanged.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
:
Hamiltonian maximization implies first-order stationarity in
v
.
Costate equation is path-stationarity.
Multiplier sign + complementarity give inequality KKT part.
Thus all KKT conditions hold.

Transition to Section
D
.

With the PMP system established, Section
D
proves equivalence between this entropy-constrained
dynamic control problem and Schrödinger bridge / KL-control formulations.

D. Equivalence to Schrödinger Bridge

D.1. Static Schrödinger problem

We formalize the static Schrödinger problem, derive its dual, establish existence/uniqueness,
and prepare the bridge to the entropy-controlled dynamic formulation in
D.2
–
D.4
.

Reference path law and endpoint coupling.

Fix
T>0
. Let
R\in\mathcal{P}(C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d}))
be a reference path measure
(Markov, non-degenerate). Denote endpoint marginal of
R
:

R_{0T}:=(X_{0},X_{T})_{\#}R\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),

and endpoint marginals
R_{0},R_{T}
.

Given prescribed
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, define coupling set

\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):=\{\pi\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}):\pi_{0}=\mu_{0},\ \pi_{T}=\mu_{T}\}.

Assumption 0..30
(Reference coupling regularity)

Assume:

1.

R_{0T}
has strictly positive density
r_{0T}(x,y)
w.r.t.
dx\,dy
;

2.

\mu_{0}\ll R_{0},\ \mu_{T}\ll R_{T}
;

3.

there exists at least one
\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
with

\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})<\infty
.

Definition 32
(Static Schrödinger problem)

The static Schrödinger problem is
[
26
]

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R_{0T}):=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})}\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T}),

where

\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int\log\!\left(\frac{d\pi}{dR_{0T}}\right)\,d\pi,&\pi\ll R_{0T},\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proposition 26
(Strict convexity and uniqueness of static minimizer)

Under Assumption
0..30
, problem

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}
admits a unique minimizer
\pi^{\star}
.

Proof

\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
is convex and narrowly compact (tightness from fixed marginals).

\mathrm{KL}(\cdot\|R_{0T})
is l.s.c. under narrow convergence and strictly convex on

\{\pi:\pi\ll R_{0T}\}
. Properness follows from Assumption
0..30
(3).
Hence direct method gives existence, and strict convexity yields uniqueness.

Theorem 0..31
(Static Schrödinger dual (entropy-transport duality))

Define

\mathcal{J}(\phi,\psi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi(x)\,d\mu_{0}(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi(y)\,d\mu_{T}(y)-\log\!\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{\phi(x)+\psi(y)}\,dR_{0T}(x,y)\right).

Then

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R_{0T})=\sup_{\phi,\psi\in C_{b}}\mathcal{J}(\phi,\psi).

Moreover, dual maximizers
(\phi^{\star},\psi^{\star})
exist up to additive gauge

(\phi,\psi)\mapsto(\phi+c,\psi-c)
.

Proof

Use Fenchel duality for relative entropy:

\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})=\sup_{f\in C_{b}}\left\{\int f\,d\pi-\log\int e^{f}\,dR_{0T}\right\}.

Incorporate marginal constraints with Lagrange potentials
\phi(x),\psi(y)
, i.e.

f(x,y)=\phi(x)+\psi(y)
. Then

\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})}\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})=\sup_{\phi,\psi}\left[\int\phi\,d\mu_{0}+\int\psi\,d\mu_{T}-\log\int e^{\phi+\psi}\,dR_{0T}\right].

No duality gap follows from convexity/l.s.c. and feasibility (Assumption
0..30
).
Gauge invariance is immediate since
\phi+\psi
unchanged by
(+c,-c)
.

Proposition 27
(Schrödinger factorization of optimizer)

Let
(\phi^{\star},\psi^{\star})
be dual optimizers and set

f(x):=e^{\phi^{\star}(x)},\qquad g(y):=e^{\psi^{\star}(y)},\qquad Z:=\int f(x)g(y)\,dR_{0T}(x,y).

Then

d\pi^{\star}(x,y)=\frac{f(x)g(y)}{Z}\,dR_{0T}(x,y).

Conversely, any minimizer must be of this form.

Proof

At dual optimum, primal optimizer satisfies exponential tilting condition from
Fenchel equality:

\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}\propto e^{\phi^{\star}+\psi^{\star}}.

Normalization yields
Z
.
Conversely, any density of form
fg/Z
with correct marginals satisfies KKT stationarity
for constrained KL minimization; by uniqueness of minimizer, it is
\pi^{\star}
.

Definition 33
(Schrödinger system (static scaling equations))

The pair
(f,g)
in Proposition
27
satisfies

\mu_{0}(dx)=f(x)\!\int g(y)\,R_{0T}(dx,dy),\qquad\mu_{T}(dy)=g(y)\!\int f(x)\,R_{0T}(dx,dy),

in measure form.
Equivalently (when
R_{0T}
has kernel
k_{T}(x,y)
w.r.t.
dxdy
):

\rho_{0}(x)=f(x)\int k_{T}(x,y)g(y)\,dy,\qquad\rho_{T}(y)=g(y)\int k_{T}(x,y)f(x)\,dx.

Theorem 0..32
(Characterization by cyclical optimality in entropic sense)

Let
\pi^{\star}
solve static SB. Then for any
\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
,

\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})-\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{\star}\|R_{0T})=\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|\pi^{\star})\geq 0.

Hence
\pi^{\star}
is the unique I-projection of
R_{0T}
onto
\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
.

Proof

Since
\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}=f(x)g(y)/Z
,

\log\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}=\phi^{\star}(x)+\psi^{\star}(y)-\log Z.

Then

\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})=\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|\pi^{\star})+\int\log\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}\,d\pi.

For
\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
, the last integral equals

\int\phi^{\star}\,d\mu_{0}+\int\psi^{\star}\,d\mu_{T}-\log Z=\int\log\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}\,d\pi^{\star}=\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{\star}\|R_{0T}),

because
\pi,\pi^{\star}
share marginals. Rearrangement gives identity.

Corollary 16
(Stability w.r.t. endpoint perturbations)

Let
(\mu_{0}^{n},\mu_{T}^{n})\to(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
narrowly, with uniform second moments and
finite SB values. Let
\pi_{n}^{\star}
be corresponding static SB minimizers.
Then (up to subsequence)

\pi_{n}^{\star}\rightharpoonup\pi^{\star},

where
\pi^{\star}
is the minimizer for
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
.
If the limit minimizer is unique, full sequence converges.

Proof

Fixed marginals imply tightness of
\{\pi_{n}^{\star}\}
. Extract convergent subsequence.
Lower semicontinuity of KL gives liminf inequality; recovery by testing near-optimal
couplings yields optimality of limit. Uniqueness implies no subsequence ambiguity.

Remark 12
(Connection to entropic OT)

If
R_{0T}(dxdy)\propto e^{-c(x,y)/\varepsilon}\,dxdy
, static SB equals entropic OT:

\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})}\left\{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int c\,d\pi+\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|dxdy)\right\}+\text{const}.

For Brownian reference,
c(x,y)=\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2T}
(up to scaling), linking SB to
quadratic-cost entropic transport.

Output used next.

D.2
lifts static optimizer
\pi^{\star}
to path space and proves equivalence with the
dynamic Schrödinger problem (path-space KL minimization).

D.2. Dynamic Schrödinger formulation

We pass from the static endpoint-coupling problem to path space, prove static–dynamic
equivalence, and derive the controlled Fokker–Planck (current-velocity) representation
that will be matched with entropy-controlled FM in
D.3
–
D.4
.

Reference path measure.

Let
R\in\mathcal{P}(\Omega)
,
\Omega:=C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, be a non-degenerate
Markov diffusion law (e.g., Brownian with variance
\varepsilon>0
):

dX_{t}=\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t}\quad\text{under }R

(or, more generally, reversible diffusion with smooth generator).
Let
R_{0T}=(X_{0},X_{T})_{\#}R
.

Definition 34
(Dynamic Schrödinger problem)

Given endpoint marginals
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
, define
[
36
]

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R):=\inf\left\{\mathrm{KL}(P\|R):P\in\mathcal{P}(\Omega),\ P_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P_{T}=\mu_{T}\right\}.

Assumption 0..33
(Feasibility and finite entropy)

Assume:

1.

there exists
P
with
P_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P_{T}=\mu_{T},\ \mathrm{KL}(P\|R)<\infty
;

2.

endpoint relative entropies w.r.t.
R_{0},R_{T}
are finite.

Theorem 0..34
(Static–dynamic equivalence)

Under Assumption
0..33
,

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R)=\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R_{0T}),

and the dynamic minimizer
P^{\star}
is uniquely characterized by

\frac{dP^{\star}}{dR}=\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}(X_{0},X_{T}),

where
\pi^{\star}
is the static minimizer from
D.1
.

Proof

Disintegrate
R
and
P
w.r.t. endpoints:

R(d\omega)=R^{xy}(d\omega)\,R_{0T}(dxdy),\qquad P(d\omega)=P^{xy}(d\omega)\,\pi(dxdy),

where
\pi=(X_{0},X_{T})_{\#}P
, and
R^{xy}
is the diffusion bridge kernel.
Chain rule for relative entropy on product/disintegrated measures yields

\mathrm{KL}(P\|R)=\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})+\int\mathrm{KL}(P^{xy}\|R^{xy})\,\pi(dxdy).

Second term is
\geq 0
, so

\mathrm{KL}(P\|R)\geq\mathrm{KL}(\pi\|R_{0T})\geq\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R_{0T}).

Taking inf over admissible
P
:

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}\geq\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}.

For reverse inequality, choose
\pi=\pi^{\star}
(static optimizer) and set

P^{\star}(d\omega):=R^{xy}(d\omega)\,\pi^{\star}(dxdy).

Then
P^{\star}
has prescribed endpoints and

\mathrm{KL}(P^{\star}\|R)=\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{\star}\|R_{0T})+\int\mathrm{KL}(R^{xy}\|R^{xy})\,\pi^{\star}(dxdy)=\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}.

Hence equality and optimality. Uniqueness follows from strict convexity of KL.
Radon–Nikodým identity
[
35
]
follows by construction:

\frac{dP^{\star}}{dR}(\omega)=\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}(X_{0}(\omega),X_{T}(\omega)).

Corollary 17
(Schrödinger system on path space)

If static optimizer factorizes as

\frac{d\pi^{\star}}{dR_{0T}}(x,y)=f(x)g(y),

then

\frac{dP^{\star}}{dR}=f(X_{0})g(X_{T}).

Proof

Immediate from Theorem
0..34
.

Definition 35
(Forward/backward harmonic functions)

Define

\alpha_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{R}\!\left[f(X_{0})\mid X_{t}=x\right],\qquad\beta_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{R}\!\left[g(X_{T})\mid X_{t}=x\right].

Then time-
t
density of
P^{\star}
satisfies

\rho_{t}^{\star}(x)=\alpha_{t}(x)\beta_{t}(x)\,r_{t}(x),

where
r_{t}
is the density of
R_{t}
.

Proposition 28
(Schrödinger bridge drift representation)

Assume
R
is Brownian with diffusivity
\varepsilon
:

dX_{t}=\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t}\quad(R).

Then under
P^{\star}
,
X_{t}
solves

dX_{t}=b_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t}^{P^{\star}},\qquad b_{t}^{\star}(x)=2\varepsilon\,\nabla\log\beta_{t}(x).

Equivalently (time-reversed form) with
\alpha_{t}
:

\tilde{b}_{t}^{\star}(x)=-2\varepsilon\,\nabla\log\alpha_{t}(x).

Proof

From Doob
h
-transform:

\frac{dP^{\star}}{dR}\bigg|_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}\propto\beta_{t}(X_{t}),

so transformed generator is

\mathcal{L}_{t}^{\star}\phi=\varepsilon\Delta\phi+2\varepsilon\nabla\log\beta_{t}\cdot\nabla\phi,

hence SDE drift
b_{t}^{\star}=2\varepsilon\nabla\log\beta_{t}
.
Backward form follows symmetrically using
\alpha_{t}
.

Definition 36
(Current velocity and osmotic velocity)

Let
\rho_{t}^{\star}
be bridge density. Define forward/backward drifts

b_{t}^{\star},\tilde{b}_{t}^{\star}
, then

v_{t}^{\mathrm{cur}}:=\frac{b_{t}^{\star}+\tilde{b}_{t}^{\star}}{2},\qquad v_{t}^{\mathrm{osm}}:=\frac{b_{t}^{\star}-\tilde{b}_{t}^{\star}}{2}=\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}.

Proposition 29
(Controlled Fokker–Planck and continuity form)

Under
P^{\star}
,
\rho_{t}^{\star}
satisfies

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}^{\star}b_{t}^{\star})=\varepsilon\Delta\rho_{t}^{\star}.

Equivalently, in current velocity form:

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{\star}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}^{\star}v_{t}^{\mathrm{cur}})=0,\qquad v_{t}^{\mathrm{cur}}=b_{t}^{\star}-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}.

Proof

First equation is standard Fokker–Planck for SDE with drift
b_{t}^{\star}
, diffusion
2\varepsilon I
.
Rearrange:

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho b)-\varepsilon\Delta\rho=\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot\big(\rho(b-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho)\big)=0,

since
\rho\nabla\log\rho=\nabla\rho
. Define
v^{\mathrm{cur}}
accordingly.

Theorem 0..35
(Benamou–Schrödinger dynamic representation)

Assume Brownian reference with diffusivity
\varepsilon
. Then

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R)=\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{0}\|R_{0})+\inf_{(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{A}(\rho_{0},\rho_{T})}\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}|^{2}\,\rho_{t}\,dxdt,

subject to

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}v_{t})=\varepsilon\Delta\rho_{t},\quad\rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho_{|T}=\rho_{T}.

Equivalent current-velocity CE form:

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho u)=0,\quad u=v-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho,

with action

\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|u+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\,\rho.

Proof

Girsanov theorem
[
19
]
for drift control
v
relative to Brownian reference gives

\mathrm{KL}(P^{v}\|R)=\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{0}\|R_{0})+\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{P^{v}}\!\int_{0}^{T}|v_{t}(X_{t})|^{2}dt

for admissible weak solutions. Rewriting expectation in Eulerian form gives

\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|v|^{2}\rho.

Infimum over all controlled drifts achieving endpoint constraints yields formula.
Current-velocity rewrite follows by substitution
u=v-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho
.

Corollary 18
(Entropy-regularized kinetic action)

For smooth
(\rho,u)
,

\int|u+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\,\rho=\int|u|^{2}\,\rho+2\varepsilon\int u\cdot\nabla\rho+\varepsilon^{2}\int\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}.

Hence

\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|u+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\rho=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|u|^{2}\rho+\frac{1}{2}\int u\cdot\nabla\log\rho\,d\mu+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\int\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

Expand square pointwise and integrate; use

\rho\nabla\log\rho=\nabla\rho
and Fisher information definition.

Remark 13
(Bridge to Section C)

The action in Theorem
0..35
has the same structural components
as Section
C
:
kinetic term + score-coupling term + Fisher term.
This is the analytic gateway for proving equivalence between entropy-controlled FM
and KL-control / Schrödinger bridge under matched parameterization (
D.3
–
D.4
).

D.3. KL-control interpretation

We express entropy-controlled flow matching as a path-space KL-control problem,
derive the exact decomposition of the objective into control-energy and entropy/Fisher terms,
and identify the parameter mapping to Schrödinger bridge.

Controlled diffusion model.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Consider controlled process

dX_{t}=\big(u_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})+w_{t}(X_{t})\big)\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t},

with law
P^{w}
on
\Omega=C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, initial law
P^{w}_{0}=\mu_{0}
, and terminal constraint

P^{w}_{T}=\mu_{T}
. Let
R^{\star}
be reference diffusion with drift
u^{\star}
:

dX_{t}=u_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t},\qquad X_{0}\sim\mu_{0}.

Assume Novikov/Girsanov conditions
[
43
]
hold for admissible
w
.

Definition 37
(KL-control objective)

Define

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};u^{\star}):=\inf_{w:\,P^{w}_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P^{w}_{T}=\mu_{T}}\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star}).

Theorem 0..36
(Girsanov energy identity)

For admissible
w
,

\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\,\mathbb{E}_{P^{w}}\!\left[\int_{0}^{T}|w_{t}(X_{t})|^{2}\,dt\right].

Equivalently, in Eulerian variables
(\rho_{t},w_{t})
:

\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|w_{t}(x)|^{2}\,\rho_{t}(x)\,dxdt,

where
\rho_{t}=(P^{w}_{t})
-density.

Proof

By Girsanov
[
19
]
,

\frac{dP^{w}}{dR^{\star}}=\exp\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{T}w_{t}(X_{t})\cdot dW_{t}^{R^{\star}}-\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}|w_{t}(X_{t})|^{2}dt\right).

Taking
P^{w}
-expectation of log-likelihood ratio (or equivalent form under
P^{w}
) yields

\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{P^{w}}\!\int|w|^{2}dt.

Eulerian form is Fubini/disintegration:

\mathbb{E}_{P^{w}}\!\int_{0}^{T}|w_{t}(X_{t})|^{2}dt=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|w_{t}(x)|^{2}\rho_{t}(x)\,dxdt.

Definition 38
(Current velocity decomposition)

Let
\rho_{t}
be time marginals under
P^{w}
. Define current velocity

v_{t}:=u_{t}^{\star}+w_{t}-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho_{t}.

Then
(\rho,v)
satisfies continuity equation

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}v_{t})=0,\qquad\rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho_{|T}=\rho_{T}.

Proof

Fokker–Planck under controlled drift
u^{\star}+w
:

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot\big(\rho(u^{\star}+w)\big)=\varepsilon\Delta\rho.

Rearrange:

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot\!\left(\rho(u^{\star}+w-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho)\right)=0.

Hence definition of
v
.

Proposition 30
(Exact algebraic bridge to FM misfit)

For any smooth admissible
(\rho,v)
, set

w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho.

Then

\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|w|^{2}\,d\mu dt=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt+\frac{1}{2}\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot(v-u^{\star})\,d\mu dt+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

Pointwise expansion:

|a+\varepsilon b|^{2}=|a|^{2}+2\varepsilon a\cdot b+\varepsilon^{2}|b|^{2},

with
a=v-u^{\star}
,
b=\nabla\log\rho
. Integrate and divide by
4\varepsilon
.
Use
\int|b|^{2}d\mu=\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})
.

Corollary 19
(Entropy-rate substitution)

Using

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})=\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}\cdot v_{t}\,d\mu_{t}

(B.1), Proposition
30
becomes

\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|w|^{2}\,d\mu dt=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt-\frac{1}{2}\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

Split

\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot(v-u^{\star})\,d\mu=\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,d\mu-\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu,

replace first term by
\dot{\mathcal{H}}
, integrate in time.

Theorem 0..37
(KL-control representation of entropy-controlled FM)

Assume smooth positive densities and finite Fisher action.
Then minimizing
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
:

\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}}\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt

is equivalent (up to explicit additive/penalty terms) to minimizing

4\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})

over controlled drifts
w
with same endpoints, with correspondence

w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho.

More precisely:

\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt=2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})-\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt+\varepsilon\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

Multiply Corollary
19
by
4\varepsilon
:

4\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}=\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}+2\varepsilon\int\dot{\mathcal{H}}-2\varepsilon\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}+\varepsilon^{2}\int\mathcal{I}.

Rearrange for
\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}
, then divide by
2
.

Proposition 31
(Effect of entropy-rate inequality)

If
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
, then

\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt=\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})\geq-\lambda T.

Therefore, in Theorem
0..37
, the correction

-\varepsilon\int\dot{\mathcal{H}}
is bounded above by
\varepsilon\lambda T
.

Proof

Absolute continuity of
t\mapsto\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})
and constraint

\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
a.e. imply

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})=\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}\,dt\geq-\lambda T.

Multiply by
-\varepsilon
.

Corollary 20
(Special case
u^{\star}\equiv 0
)

When
u^{\star}\equiv 0
,

\frac{1}{2}\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt=2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R)-\varepsilon\big(\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})\big)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Hence ECFM objective equals KL-control up to endpoint entropy and Fisher corrections.

Proof

Set
u^{\star}=0
in Theorem
0..37
.

Remark 14
(Interpretation)

The entropy-controlled FM constraint selects, among FM-aligned transports,
those whose KL-control realization against
R^{\star}
avoids excessive entropy dissipation.
The multiplier
\eta
in Section
C
is therefore a time-dependent shadow price
for KL-regularity pressure induced by endpoint-constrained stochastic control.

Output used next.

D.4
uses these identities to prove formal equivalence between
ECFM with entropy-rate control and Schrödinger bridge under matched parameter scaling,
including precise correspondence of optimal drifts and path laws.

D.4. Proof of equivalence under entropy-rate constraint

We prove that entropy-controlled flow matching (ECFM) is equivalent to a
Schrödinger-bridge/KL-control problem under explicit parameter matching and
regularity assumptions. The result is stated in two layers:
(i) exact variational equivalence with correction terms;
(ii) exact identity in the matched gauge where correction terms are absorbed.

Assumption 0..38
(Matched regular regime)

Assume:

1.

u^{\star}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(dt\,d\mu_{t})
with at most linear growth in
x
;

2.

admissible
(\mu,v)
satisfy
\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx
,
\rho_{t}>0
,
\rho\in C^{1}_{t}C^{2}_{x}
,

\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt<\infty
;

3.

CE holds:
\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v)=0
,
\rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\rho_{|T}=\rho_{T}
;

4.

entropy-rate constraint:
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda
a.e.;

5.

KL-control is with reference diffusion

dX_{t}=u_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t},\qquad X_{0}\sim\mu_{0},

and controlled drift correction
w
such that

dX_{t}=(u_{t}^{\star}+w_{t})(X_{t})\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t},\qquad X_{T}\sim\mu_{T}.

Definition 39
(Primal values)

Define ECFM value

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}:=\inf_{(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu\,dt,

and KL-control value

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon}:=\inf_{w:\,P^{w}_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P^{w}_{T}=\mu_{T}}\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star}).

Define map between Eulerian and stochastic controls:
