\boxed{w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad v=u^{\star}+w-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho.

Lemma 15
(Feasible-set correspondence)

Under Assumption
0..38
, the map in Definition
39

is a bijection between:

1.

smooth CE paths
(\rho,v)
with fixed endpoints;

2.

controlled FP paths
(\rho,w)
solving

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot\big(\rho(u^{\star}+w)\big)=\varepsilon\Delta\rho

with same endpoints.

Proof

Given
(\rho,v)
, define
w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho
. Then

\rho(u^{\star}+w)=\rho v+\varepsilon\nabla\rho.

Hence

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho(u^{\star}+w))=\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v)+\varepsilon\Delta\rho=\varepsilon\Delta\rho,

using CE.

Conversely, given
(\rho,w)
, define
v=u^{\star}+w-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho
. Then

\rho v=\rho(u^{\star}+w)-\varepsilon\nabla\rho.

So

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v)=\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho(u^{\star}+w))-\varepsilon\Delta\rho=0.

Both constructions are inverse of each other.

Lemma 16
(Exact objective identity)

For corresponding
(\rho,v)\leftrightarrow(\rho,w)
,

\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt=2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})-\varepsilon\!\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt+\varepsilon\!\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

From
D.3
Girsanov identity:

\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})=\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\int|w|^{2}\,d\mu dt.

Insert
w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho
and expand:

|w|^{2}=|v-u^{\star}|^{2}+2\varepsilon\,\nabla\log\rho\cdot(v-u^{\star})+\varepsilon^{2}|\nabla\log\rho|^{2}.

Integrate:

4\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}=\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}+2\varepsilon\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,d\mu dt-2\varepsilon\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Use
\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,d\mu=\dot{\mathcal{H}}
, rearrange.

Theorem 0..39
(Variational equivalence with explicit correction functional)

Define correction

\mathfrak{C}(\mu;u^{\star},\varepsilon):=-\varepsilon\!\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt+\varepsilon\!\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Then

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=\inf_{w:\,P_{0}^{w}=\mu_{0},\ P_{T}^{w}=\mu_{T},\ \text{induced }\mu\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}}\left\{2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})+\mathfrak{C}(\mu;u^{\star},\varepsilon)\right\}.

Hence ECFM and KL-control are equivalent optimization problems up to the explicit
state-dependent correction
\mathfrak{C}
.

Proof

By Lemma
15
, feasible sets correspond bijectively.
By Lemma
16
, objectives differ by
\mathfrak{C}
exactly.
Taking infima over corresponding feasible classes yields identity.

Proposition 32
(Entropy-budget bound on correction)

For any
(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\lambda}
,

-\varepsilon\!\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\,dt\leq\varepsilon\lambda T.

Therefore

\mathfrak{C}(\mu;u^{\star},\varepsilon)\leq\varepsilon\lambda T+\varepsilon\!\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

Integrate
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
:

\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}\,dt=\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})\geq-\lambda T.

Multiply by
-\varepsilon
. Remaining terms are unchanged.

Definition 40
(Matched gauge)

We say
(u^{\star},\varepsilon,\lambda)
is in matched gauge if, on the admissible class,

\mathfrak{C}(\mu;u^{\star},\varepsilon)=C_{0}

for a constant
C_{0}
independent of
(\mu,v)
(e.g., via calibrated reference drift,
fixed entropy endpoints, and absorbed Fisher/score terms in baseline energy).

Theorem 0..40
(Exact equivalence in matched gauge)

Under Assumption
0..38
and matched gauge (Definition
40
),

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=2\varepsilon\,\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon}+C_{0}.

Moreover, a pair
(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})
is ECFM-optimal iff the induced controlled law

P^{w^{\star}}
with

w^{\star}=v^{\star}-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho^{\star}

is KL-optimal.

Proof

From Theorem
0..39
:

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=\inf_{w}\{2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})+\mathfrak{C}(\mu_{w})\}.

If
\mathfrak{C}(\mu_{w})=C_{0}
for all admissible
w
,

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=2\varepsilon\inf_{w}\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})+C_{0}=2\varepsilon\,\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon}+C_{0}.

Argmin correspondence follows from adding/removing same constant.

Corollary 21
(Identification with Schrödinger bridge)

When
R^{\star}
is the reference path measure in D.2,

\mathsf{K}_{\varepsilon}=\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R^{\star})=\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R^{\star}_{0T}).

Hence, in matched gauge,

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=2\varepsilon\,\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}+C_{0}=2\varepsilon\,\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{stat}}+C_{0}.

Proof

First equality is definition of KL-control value under endpoint constraints.
Second equality is
D.2
static–dynamic equivalence theorem.
Substitute into Theorem
0..40
.

Theorem 0..41
(Equivalence of optimality systems)

Assume smoothness so both Eulerian KKT-PMP (Section
C
) and SB optimal drift equations (Section
D.2
)
are valid. Under correspondence

w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho,

the ECFM optimality system is equivalent to the SB/KL-control optimality system:

1.

CE for
(\rho,v)

\Longleftrightarrow
FP for
(\rho,u^{\star}+w)
;

2.

Hamiltonian stationarity in
v

\Longleftrightarrow
quadratic minimization in
w
;

3.

entropy multiplier
\eta
enforces active entropy-rate boundary, matching
the KL regularization pressure through the score term.

Proof

(1) is Lemma
15
.
(2) follows from strict convex quadratic relationship between
v-u^{\star}
and

w-\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho
.
(3) from complementary slackness in
C
and identity in Lemma
16
:
the entropy-rate term is precisely the part coupling FM action to KL-control.
Thus systems are equivalent under variable transformation.

Remark 15
(What has been proved in Section D)

D.1
–
D.4
establish:

•

static SB
=
dynamic SB;

•

dynamic SB
=
KL-control relative to reference diffusion;

•

ECFM
=
KL-control plus explicit entropy/Fisher/score correction;

•

in matched gauge, ECFM is exactly equivalent (up to additive constant) to SB.

This is the precise formal meaning of “equivalence to Schrödinger bridge under entropy control.”

E. Convergence to Entropic OT Geodesics

E.1. Statement of theorem

We state the convergence theorem showing that entropy-controlled flow matching
selects (under the Schrödinger/KL correspondence established in Section
D
)
the entropic optimal transport interpolation (Schrödinger interpolation), and
that its current-velocity trajectories converge in the natural action topology.

Assumption 0..42
(Entropic transport regime)

Fix
T>0
,
\varepsilon>0
,
\lambda\geq 0
, and
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{ac}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Assume:

1.

The reference path law is Brownian-with-drift
R^{\star}
:

dX_{t}=u_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t},\qquad X_{0}\sim\mu_{0},

with
u^{\star}
satisfying linear growth and integrability from
C.1
/
D.4
.

2.

The ECFM problem
(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda})
admits minimizers

(\mu^{\lambda},v^{\lambda})
, and Section
D
equivalence holds.

3.

The associated KL/SB problem has unique minimizer
P^{\varepsilon,\lambda}

(with marginals
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
), and
\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}:=(X_{t})_{\#}P^{\varepsilon,\lambda}

has density
\rho_{t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in C^{1}_{t}C^{2}_{x}
,
>0
.

4.

Finite Fisher action:

\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon,\lambda})\,dt<\infty.

Definition 41
(Entropic OT geodesic (Schrödinger interpolation))

Given
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
and reference
R^{\star}
, the entropic OT geodesic is

(\bar{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\in[0,T]}:=\big((X_{t})_{\#}\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}\big)_{t\in[0,T]},

where
\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}
is the unique dynamic SB minimizer:

\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}\in\arg\min\left\{\mathrm{KL}(P\|R^{\star}):P_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P_{T}=\mu_{T}\right\}.

Definition 42
(Current velocity of entropic geodesic)

Let
\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}
be density of
\bar{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}
, and

\bar{b}_{t}^{\varepsilon}
the forward drift of
\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}
. Define

\bar{v}_{t}^{\varepsilon}:=\bar{b}_{t}^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\nabla\log\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}.

Then

\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\!\cdot(\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\bar{v}_{t}^{\varepsilon})=0,\quad\bar{\rho}_{|0}^{\varepsilon}=\rho_{0},\ \bar{\rho}_{|T}^{\varepsilon}=\rho_{T}.

Theorem 0..43
(Convergence/identification to entropic OT geodesic)

Under Assumption
0..42
, let
(\mu^{\lambda},v^{\lambda})
be any ECFM minimizer.
Define corresponding control

w^{\lambda}:=v^{\lambda}-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho^{\lambda}.

Then:

1.

Path-law identification.

The induced controlled law
P^{\lambda}:=P^{w^{\lambda}}
is a minimizer of dynamic SB/KL problem:

P^{\lambda}\in\arg\min\{\mathrm{KL}(P\|R^{\star}):P_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P_{T}=\mu_{T}\}.

Hence by uniqueness:

P^{\lambda}=\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}.

2.

Marginal-curve identification.

\mu_{t}^{\lambda}=\bar{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon},\qquad\forall t\in[0,T].

3.

Velocity identification (a.e.).

v_{t}^{\lambda}=\bar{v}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon})\ \text{for a.e. }t.

4.

Action identity.

\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}^{\lambda}-u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\lambda}dt=2\varepsilon\,\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T};R^{\star})+C_{0},

with
C_{0}
the matched-gauge constant of
D.4
(or explicit correction functional
if unmatched gauge is retained).

5.

Geodesic characterization.

The ECFM minimizer trajectory coincides with the unique entropic OT geodesic:

(\mu_{t}^{\lambda})_{t\in[0,T]}=(\bar{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\in[0,T]}.

Theorem 0..44
(Strict convexity/uniqueness consequence)

Assume strict convexity of dynamic entropic action in flux variables

(\rho,m)
under fixed endpoint marginals (equivalently uniqueness of SB minimizer).
Then the ECFM minimizer path is unique:

\text{if }(\mu^{1},v^{1}),(\mu^{2},v^{2})\in\arg\min(\mathrm{ECFM}_{\lambda}),\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mu_{t}^{1}=\mu_{t}^{2}\ \forall t,\ \ v^{1}=v^{2}\ \ dt\,d\mu\text{-a.e.}

Corollary 22
(Entropic geodesic PDE system for ECFM optimizer)

Let
(\mu^{\lambda},v^{\lambda})
be ECFM-optimal and

\mu^{\lambda}=\bar{\mu}^{\varepsilon}
. Then there exist Schrödinger potentials

(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t})
such that

\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}=\alpha_{t}\beta_{t}r_{t}^{\star},

and

v_{t}^{\lambda}=u_{t}^{\star}+2\varepsilon\nabla\log\beta_{t}-\varepsilon\nabla\log\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}=u_{t}^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\frac{\beta_{t}}{\alpha_{t}},

with CE:

\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\!\cdot(\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}v_{t}^{\lambda})=0.

Corollary 23
(Metric-space interpretation)

The optimal ECFM curve
(\mu_{t}^{\lambda})
is the unique minimizer of the
entropic Benamou–Schrödinger action among endpoint-constrained curves; hence it is
the geodesic in the entropic transport geometry induced by
\mathrm{KL}(\cdot\|R^{\star})
.

Remark 16
(Role of
\lambda
)

\lambda
acts as an admissibility selector in ECFM. Whenever the selected ECFM minimizer exists
and Section
D
equivalence applies, the resulting optimal path is exactly the
entropic geodesic for
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
and
\varepsilon
. Different
\lambda
can change
feasible set activity patterns (active/inactive entropy intervals) while preserving
the same identified SB path under matched optimality conditions.

Remark 17
(What is proved in E.2–E.5)

Subsections
E.2
–
E.5
provide:

•

convex-duality proof of Theorem
0..43
,

•

regularity assumptions ensuring strict convexity and uniqueness,

•

quantitative stability estimates for perturbations,

•

structural consequences for computer-vision generative trajectories.

E.2. Proof via convex duality

We prove Theorem
0..43
using:
(i) primal flux convexity,
(ii) Fenchel–Rockafellar duality,
(iii) the exact ECFM
\leftrightarrow
KL/SB correspondence from
D.4
.

Flux formulation.

Write
\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx
,
m_{t}:=\rho_{t}v_{t}
.
Define convex kinetic integrand

f_{u^{\star}}(t,x,\rho,m):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{|m-\rho u_{t}^{\star}(x)|^{2}}{\rho},&\rho>0,\\[3.44444pt]
0,&\rho=0,\ m=0,\\
+\infty,&\rho=0,\ m\neq 0.\end{cases}

Then ECFM objective is

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho,m):=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int f_{u^{\star}}(t,x,\rho_{t},m_{t})\,dxdt.

Definition 43
(Primal admissible set in flux variables)

Define

\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}:=\Big\{(\rho,m):\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot m=0,\ \rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho_{|T}=\rho_{T},\ \dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})+\lambda\geq 0\ \text{a.e.},\ \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho,m)<\infty\Big\}.

Then

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=\inf_{(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho,m).

Lemma 17
(Convexity and lower semicontinuity)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}
is convex and weakly l.s.c. on

L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-flux pairs
(\rho,m)
with
\rho\geq 0
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}
is convex and weakly closed under the compactness topology
from
C.1
.

Proof

Convexity/l.s.c. of
f_{u^{\star}}
in
(\rho,m)
are standard (perspective of quadratic form).
Integral of convex l.s.c. integrand remains convex l.s.c.
CE and endpoint constraints are linear/closed.
Entropy-rate feasibility is weakly closed by
C.1
assumption.
Hence
\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}
is convex and closed.

Definition 44
(Dual functional)

For test potential
\varphi
and multiplier
\eta\geq 0
, define

\mathcal{D}(\varphi,\eta):=\int\varphi(0,\cdot)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\varphi(T,\cdot)\,d\mu_{T}-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,dt

subject to Hamilton–Jacobi-type inequality (from C.5)

\partial_{t}\varphi+u^{\star}\!\cdot b_{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}|b_{\eta}|^{2}\leq 0,\qquad b_{\eta}=\nabla\varphi-\eta\nabla\log\rho,

in the weak admissible sense.
Define dual value

\mathsf{D}_{\lambda}:=\sup_{(\varphi,\eta)\ \mathrm{admissible}}\mathcal{D}(\varphi,\eta).

Theorem 0..45
(Strong duality for ECFM)

Under Slater qualification (
C.3
) and Assumption
0..42
,

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=\mathsf{D}_{\lambda},

and both primal and dual optimizers exist.

Proof

Apply Fenchel–Rockafellar to

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}+\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{CE+bc}}+\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{ent\ rate}},

with linear operator encoding CE and entropy-rate map.
By Lemma
17
, functionals are proper convex l.s.c.
Slater condition ensures zero duality gap and attainment.
Dual constraints coincide with
C.5
inequality form.

Lemma 18
(Exact ECFM–KL identity at primal level)

For any admissible
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}
, with

v=\frac{m}{\rho},\qquad w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho,

and induced path law
P^{w}
,

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho,m)=2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})+\mathfrak{C}(\rho;u^{\star},\varepsilon),

where

\mathfrak{C}(\rho;u^{\star},\varepsilon)=-\varepsilon\!\int_{0}^{T}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})\,dt+\varepsilon\!\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot u^{\star}\,d\mu dt-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

Proof

This is
D.4
Lemma
16
, rewritten as
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}=\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}d\mu dt
.

Proposition 33
(Transfer of minimizers to KL/SB minimizers)

Assume matched gauge (
D.4
), i.e.
\mathfrak{C}(\rho;u^{\star},\varepsilon)=C_{0}
on admissible class.
If
(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})
minimizes
\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}
, then induced
P^{w^{\star}}
minimizes

\inf\{\mathrm{KL}(P\|R^{\star}):P_{0}=\mu_{0},\ P_{T}=\mu_{T}\}.

Conversely, any KL minimizer induces an ECFM minimizer.

Proof

By Lemma
18
,

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho,m)=2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R^{\star})+C_{0}.

Since additive constant does not affect argmin, minimizers correspond bijectively
through the map
w=v-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho
, whose feasibility bijection is
D.4
Lemma.

Theorem 0..46
(Proof of Theorem
0..43
)

Under Assumption
0..42
and matched gauge:

1.

any ECFM minimizer induces a KL/SB minimizer;

2.

by uniqueness of dynamic SB minimizer
\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}
, induced law equals
\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}
;

3.

therefore marginals coincide:
\mu_{t}^{\lambda}=\bar{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\ \forall t
;

4.

velocities coincide
dt\,d\mu
-a.e. via flux uniqueness:

m_{t}^{\lambda}=\rho_{t}^{\lambda}v_{t}^{\lambda}=\bar{\rho}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\bar{v}_{t}^{\varepsilon};

5.

action identity follows from
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}=2\varepsilon\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}+C_{0}
.

Hence all claims of Theorem
0..43
hold.

Proof

(1) from Proposition
33
.
(2) uniqueness of SB minimizer (
D.1
/
D.2
strict convexity).
(3) equal path laws imply equal one-time marginals.
(4) strict convexity of action in flux under fixed
\rho
and endpoint constraints gives unique minimizing flux, thus unique current velocity a.e.
(5) evaluate identity at optimal pair.

Corollary 24
(Dual certificate of entropic-geodesic optimality)

Let
(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}
and
(\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star})
dual-feasible satisfy

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})=\mathcal{D}(\varphi^{\star},\eta^{\star}).

Then
(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})
is ECFM-optimal and its induced path law is SB-optimal;
hence
(\mu_{t}^{\star})
is the entropic OT geodesic.

Proof

By weak duality,
\mathcal{D}\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}\leq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}
.
Equality implies primal and dual optimality.
Apply Theorem
0..46
.

Proposition 34
(Stability of convergence under approximate optimality)

Let
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}
be
\delta_{n}
-optimal:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{FM}}(\rho^{n},m^{n})\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}+\delta_{n},\qquad\delta_{n}\downarrow 0.

Then induced
P^{w^{n}}
is
(\delta_{n}/(2\varepsilon))
-optimal for SB (matched gauge), and
any limit point is SB-optimal. If SB minimizer is unique,
P^{w^{n}}\Rightarrow\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}
.

Proof

From exact identity:

2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w^{n}}\|R^{\star})+C_{0}\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}+\delta_{n}=2\varepsilon\,\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}+C_{0}+\delta_{n}.

Hence

\mathrm{KL}(P^{w^{n}}\|R^{\star})\leq\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{dyn}}+\frac{\delta_{n}}{2\varepsilon}.

Compactness/tightness gives convergent subsequences; l.s.c. of KL gives optimality of limits.
Uniqueness yields full convergence.

Remark 18
(What remains in E.3–E.5)

E.3
formalizes regularity assumptions ensuring strict convexity/uniqueness of minimizers.

E.4
provides quantitative stability estimates for perturbations in endpoints/reference drift.

E.5
links these results to practical trajectory properties used in vision generative modeling.

E.3. Regularity assumptions

We collect sufficient analytic conditions ensuring:
(i) well-posedness of ECFM/SB variational problems,
(ii) strict convexity and uniqueness of minimizers,
(iii) validity of PDE and dual/KKT manipulations used in Sections
C
–
E
.

Definition 45
(Regularity class
\mathfrak{R}(T,\varepsilon)
)

A quadruple
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\varepsilon)
belongs to
\mathfrak{R}(T,\varepsilon)
if:

1.

Endpoint densities:

\mu_{i}=\rho_{i}dx,\quad\rho_{i}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ \rho_{i}\geq 0,\ \int\rho_{i}=1,\quad m_{2}(\mu_{i})<\infty,\quad\mathcal{H}(\mu_{i})\in\mathbb{R},\quad i\in\{0,T\}.

2.

Reference drift regularity:

u^{\star}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad|u_{t}^{\star}(x)|\leq a_{t}+b_{t}|x|,\quad a,b\in L^{2}(0,T),\ b\geq 0,

and weak spatial derivative
\nabla_{x}u^{\star}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
.

3.

Admissible density positivity/regularity:

for minimizers
(\rho,v)
,

\rho\in L^{\infty}((0,T);L^{1}\cap L^{p}),\ p>1,\qquad\rho>0\ \text{a.e.},

\sqrt{\rho}\in L^{2}((0,T);H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\quad\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt<\infty.

4.

Entropy absolute continuity:

t\mapsto\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\in AC([0,T])
, and

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})=\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}\cdot v_{t}\,d\mu_{t}\quad\text{a.e. }t.

5.

Coercivity/compactness of action:

sublevels of

(\rho,m)\mapsto\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho}

are tight in
\mathcal{P}_{2}
and weakly compact in flux topology.

6.

Qualification (Slater):

there exists
(\bar{\rho},\bar{v})
satisfying CE/endpoints and

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{\mu}_{t})+\lambda\geq\delta>0\quad\text{a.e. }t.

Assumption 0..47
(Reference diffusion regularity for SB)

For SB representation, assume
R^{\star}
solves

dX_{t}=u_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,dW_{t}

with well-posed martingale problem and strictly positive transition density

p^{\star}(s,x;t,y)
(Aronson-type Gaussian bounds), and
R^{\star}_{0T}
admits positive density.

Assumption 0..48
(Log-Sobolev / displacement-convex control)

Either one of the following holds:

1.

(LSI route) Along admissible
\mu_{t}
, a uniform log-Sobolev constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}
exists:

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t}\mid\gamma)\leq\frac{C_{\mathrm{LSI}}}{2}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}\mid\gamma)

for suitable reference
\gamma
;

2.

(displacement-convex route) Entropy functional is displacement convex along relevant geodesics,
ensuring lower semicontinuity and convex interpolation bounds.

Lemma 19
(Well-definedness of entropy-rate term)

Under Definition
45
(3)-(4),

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\nabla\log\rho_{t}\cdot v_{t}|\,d\mu_{t}dt<\infty,

hence
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})
is well-defined in
L^{1}(0,T)
.

Proof

By Cauchy–Schwarz in
L^{2}(\mu_{t})
:

\int|\nabla\log\rho\cdot v|\,d\mu\leq\left(\int|\nabla\log\rho|^{2}\,d\mu\right)^{1/2}\left(\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu\right)^{1/2}.

Integrate in time and apply Hölder:

\int_{0}^{T}(\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}))^{1/2}\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{t})}\,dt\leq\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}dt\right)^{1/2}<\infty.

Lemma 20
(Weak stability of CE with finite action)

Let
(\rho^{n},m^{n})
satisfy CE and

\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{|m^{n}-\rho^{n}u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho^{n}}<\infty.

If
\rho^{n}\rightharpoonup\rho
narrowly (uniformly in
t
) and
m^{n}\rightharpoonup m
weakly as measures,
then
(\rho,m)
satisfies CE with same endpoints.

Proof

For any
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\left(\partial_{t}\psi\,\rho^{n}+\nabla\psi\cdot m^{n}\right)\,dxdt+\int\psi(0,\cdot)\rho_{0}-\int\psi(T,\cdot)\rho_{T}=0.

Pass to the limit by weak convergence of
\rho^{n},m^{n}
, obtaining CE for
(\rho,m)
.
Endpoint terms persist by fixed boundary data.

Proposition 35
(Strict convexity in flux and uniqueness)

Assume
\rho>0
a.e. on support of admissible minimizers.
Then

(\rho,m)\mapsto\int\frac{|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho}

is strictly convex in
m
(for fixed
\rho
); and jointly convex in
(\rho,m)
.
If, in addition, admissible set is affine in
(\rho,m)
(CE + linearized entropy active set),
the minimizer flux is unique.

Proof

For fixed
\rho>0
, map
m\mapsto|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}/\rho
is strictly convex quadratic.
Joint convexity follows from perspective structure of quadratic norm.
Uniqueness follows from strict convexity on convex feasible set:
if two distinct minimizers existed, midpoint would have strictly lower value.

Theorem 0..49
(Sufficient conditions for uniqueness of ECFM path)

Suppose Definition
45
, Assumptions
0..47
,
0..48
,
and positivity
\rho_{t}>0
a.e. hold for minimizers. Then:

1.

ECFM minimizer exists;

2.

induced KL/SB minimizer is unique;

3.

ECFM marginal path
(\mu_{t})
is unique;

4.

velocity is unique
dt\,d\mu
-a.e.

Proof

Existence: direct method using coercivity + CE stability (Lemma
20
)
+ entropy closedness from Theorem
0..17
(3).
SB uniqueness: strict convexity of KL on path space with fixed endpoints.
Path uniqueness: from D/E identification of ECFM minimizer with unique SB minimizer.
Velocity uniqueness: from strict convexity in flux (Proposition
35
).

Proposition 36
(Regularity upgrade via parabolic smoothing)

Assume controlled FP form

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho(u^{\star}+w))=\varepsilon\Delta\rho

with
u^{\star}+w\in L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{x}
,
\rho_{0}\in L^{p},\ p>1
.
Then for
t>0
:

\rho_{t}\in W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\rho_{t}>0\ \text{a.e.},

and Fisher information is locally integrable in time.

Proof

Standard parabolic regularization for uniformly elliptic operator with drift in
energy class gives instantaneous smoothing and positivity (weak Harnack/Aronson kernel positivity).
Energy inequality yields

\int_{\tau}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}<\infty\quad\forall\tau>0.

Corollary 25
(Validity of KKT/PMP identities)

Under Theorem
0..49
and
Proposition
36
, all identities used in C.3–C.6 hold rigorously:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}=\int\nabla\log\rho\cdot v\,d\mu,\qquad v=u^{\star}-\nabla\varphi+\eta\nabla\log\rho,

and adjoint equations are valid in weak form.

Proof

Entropy-rate identity from Lemma
19
plus CE regularity.
Stationarity/adjoint equations follow from convex duality with attained primal/dual pairs and
density of smooth test functions in energy spaces.

Remark 19
(Minimal vs. stronger assumptions)

The above set is sufficient, not necessary.
For modularity:

•

main theorems may assume only finite-energy weak solutions + Slater;

•

smooth formulas (explicit
\nabla\log\rho
, pointwise EL equations) can be stated under
the stronger class
\mathfrak{R}(T,\varepsilon)
.

Output used next.

E.4
uses these regularity hypotheses to derive quantitative stability:
Lipschitz-type dependence of trajectories and actions on endpoint perturbations, drift perturbations,
and entropy-budget perturbations.

E.4. Uniqueness of minimizer and quantitative stability

We provide quantitative stability estimates in the entropic OT/ECFM regime:

•

uniqueness via strict convexity,

•

Lipschitz-type dependence on endpoint marginals,

•

perturbation bounds w.r.t. reference drift
u^{\star}
,

•

perturbation bounds w.r.t. entropy budget parameter
\lambda
,

•

stability of optimal action and trajectories.

Assumption 0..50
(Uniform integrability envelope)

Assume the regularity conditions of
E.3
hold for all perturbed instances considered below, with
uniform constants:

\sup\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt\leq M_{1},\qquad\sup\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt\leq M_{2},\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}m_{2}(\mu_{t})\leq M_{3}.

Assume also a common LSI/displacement-convex constant
C_{\star}
and common Slater margin
\delta>0
.

Theorem 0..51
(Strict-convex uniqueness in flux form)

For fixed endpoints
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
, fixed
u^{\star},\varepsilon,\lambda
, assume admissible set is convex
and nonempty, and
\rho>0
a.e. for minimizers. Then ECFM has a unique minimizing flux

m^{\star}=\rho^{\star}v^{\star}

(and hence unique
v^{\star}
,
dt\,d\mu^{\star}
-a.e.). Consequently, by
E.2
identification,
the optimal path law equals the unique SB law.

Proof

Objective in flux variables is

\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)=\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{|m-\rho u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho}.

For fixed
\rho>0
, strictly convex in
m
.
Suppose
(\rho^{1},m^{1})\neq(\rho^{2},m^{2})
are minimizers in convex admissible set.
For
\theta\in(0,1)
, midpoint admissible and

\mathcal{A}(\theta z_{1}+(1-\theta)z_{2})<\theta\mathcal{A}(z_{1})+(1-\theta)\mathcal{A}(z_{2})

unless
m^{1}=m^{2}
a.e. where
\rho>0
. Thus
m^{1}=m^{2}
, then CE with same endpoints implies
same trajectory
\rho
(uniqueness of linear transport equation under finite-energy drift class).
Hence uniqueness.

Definition 46
(Problem perturbation family)

For
k\in\{1,2\}
, let instance
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
be determined by

(\mu_{0}^{k},\mu_{T}^{k},u_{k}^{\star},\lambda_{k}),

with optimal ECFM pair
(\mu^{k},v^{k})
, optimal flux
m^{k}=\rho^{k}v^{k}
, value
\mathsf{V}_{k}
.
Define endpoint discrepancy

\Delta_{\mathrm{ep}}:=W_{2}(\mu_{0}^{1},\mu_{0}^{2})+W_{2}(\mu_{T}^{1},\mu_{T}^{2}),

drift discrepancy

\Delta_{u}:=\|u_{1}^{\star}-u_{2}^{\star}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R})}

for radius
R
capturing
1-\eta
mass uniformly (tail handled below), and
budget discrepancy

\Delta_{\lambda}:=|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|.

Proposition 37
(Value sensitivity to drift perturbation)

Under Assumption
0..50
, for same endpoints and same
\lambda
,

|\mathsf{V}_{1}-\mathsf{V}_{2}|\leq C_{1}\,\Delta_{u}+C_{2}\,\Delta_{u}^{2}+\tau_{R},

where
C_{1},C_{2}
depend on
M_{1},M_{3},T
, and tail term
\tau_{R}\to 0
as
R\to\infty
.

Proof

Use optimal
(\rho^{1},v^{1})
as competitor for problem 2:

\mathsf{V}_{2}-\mathsf{V}_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\left(|v^{1}-u_{2}^{\star}|^{2}-|v^{1}-u_{1}^{\star}|^{2}\right)\,d\mu^{1}dt.

Expand:

|a-b|^{2}-|a-c|^{2}=2a\cdot(c-b)+|b|^{2}-|c|^{2}.

Set
a=v^{1},\ b=u_{2}^{\star},\ c=u_{1}^{\star}
:

\mathsf{V}_{2}-\mathsf{V}_{1}\leq\int v^{1}\cdot(u_{1}^{\star}-u_{2}^{\star})\,d\mu^{1}dt+\frac{1}{2}\int(|u_{2}^{\star}|^{2}-|u_{1}^{\star}|^{2})\,d\mu^{1}dt.

Estimate first term by Cauchy–Schwarz and
M_{1}
, second by

\frac{1}{2}\int|u_{1}^{\star}-u_{2}^{\star}|(|u_{1}^{\star}|+|u_{2}^{\star}|)\,d\mu^{1}dt.

Split inside
B_{R}
and complement; bound inside by
L^{2}
norm
\Delta_{u}
, outside by second-moment tails,
giving
\tau_{R}
. Reverse roles
1,2
for absolute value.

Proposition 38
(Monotonicity and Lipschitz bound in
\lambda
)

For fixed
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star})
, feasible sets satisfy

\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}\ \Longrightarrow\ \mathcal{X}_{\lambda_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\lambda_{2}},

hence

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{2}}\leq\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{1}}.

If dual multiplier satisfies
\|\eta^{\star}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{1}(0,T)}\leq C_{\eta}
uniformly on interval
I\subset[0,\infty)
,
then

|\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{1}}-\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{2}}|\leq C_{\eta}\,|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\quad\text{for }\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in I.

Proof

Set inclusion is immediate from weaker entropy-rate lower bound at larger
\lambda
.
For Lipschitz bound, use dual representation

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=\sup_{(\varphi,\eta)\in\mathcal{K}}\Big\{\mathcal{B}(\varphi)-\lambda\int_{0}^{T}\eta(t)\,dt\Big\}.

For any dual-feasible
(\varphi,\eta)
,

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{2}}\geq\mathcal{B}(\varphi)-\lambda_{2}\!\int\eta=\mathcal{B}(\varphi)-\lambda_{1}\!\int\eta-(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\!\int\eta.

Taking
(\varphi,\eta)

\epsilon
-optimal for
\lambda_{1}
, then
\epsilon\downarrow 0
:

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{1}}-\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{2}}\leq(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\int\eta_{\lambda_{1}}^{\star}\leq C_{\eta}|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|.

Swap indices for absolute value.

Theorem 0..52
(Endpoint stability of entropic interpolation)

Consider two endpoint pairs
(\mu_{0}^{k},\mu_{T}^{k})
with common
u^{\star},\varepsilon,\lambda
, and let

(\mu_{t}^{k},v_{t}^{k})
be optimal ECFM trajectories (equiv. SB interpolations).
Then there exists
C_{\mathrm{ep}}>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq C_{\mathrm{ep}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{ep}}.

Moreover, for fluxes:

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\left|\frac{m_{t}^{1}}{\rho_{t}^{1}}-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{\rho_{t}^{2}}\right|^{2}(\rho_{t}^{1}\wedge\rho_{t}^{2})\,dxdt\leq C_{\mathrm{ep}}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{ep}}^{2}.

Proof

Via
E.2
, each optimal trajectory equals unique SB interpolation for corresponding endpoints.
Schrödinger system depends smoothly/Lipschitz-continuously on marginals under positivity and kernel bounds.
Use contraction estimate for entropic interpolation map in Sinkhorn geometry:

\|(\phi^{1},\psi^{1})-(\phi^{2},\psi^{2})\|\leq C\,\Delta_{\mathrm{ep}}.

Translate potential perturbation to density path perturbation by representation

\rho_{t}=\alpha_{t}\beta_{t}r_{t}^{\star}
, yielding
W_{2}
-bound uniformly in
t
.
Flux bound follows by stability of current velocity formula

v_{t}=u_{t}^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\frac{\beta_{t}}{\alpha_{t}},

combined with weighted
L^{2}
estimates from Fisher controls
M_{2}
.

Corollary 26
(Joint perturbation bound for values)

For two instances
\mathfrak{P}_{1},\mathfrak{P}_{2}
,

|\mathsf{V}_{1}-\mathsf{V}_{2}|\leq C_{\mathrm{ep}}\Delta_{\mathrm{ep}}+C_{u}^{(1)}\Delta_{u}+C_{u}^{(2)}\Delta_{u}^{2}+C_{\eta}\Delta_{\lambda}+\tau_{R}.

Proof

Combine endpoint stability transfer (compare via transported competitor), drift sensitivity
(Proposition
37
), and
\lambda
-Lipschitz bound (Proposition
38
).
Tail term from localization.

Proposition 39
(Stability of minimizers (graph convergence))

Let parameters
\theta_{n}=(\mu_{0}^{n},\mu_{T}^{n},u_{n}^{\star},\lambda_{n})\to\theta
in the topology above,
and let
(\rho^{n},m^{n})
be optimal solutions. Then every limit point of
(\rho^{n},m^{n})
is optimal for
\theta
.
If optimal solution for
\theta
is unique, full sequence converges:

\rho^{n}\to\rho^{\star}\ \text{(narrow uniformly in }t),\qquad m^{n}\rightharpoonup m^{\star}.

Proof

Uniform action bounds from optimality + perturbation estimates imply compactness.
CE closedness from
E.3
Lemma.
Lower semicontinuity yields liminf inequality:

\mathcal{A}(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})\leq\liminf_{n}\mathcal{A}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Recovery via perturbed competitors gives matching limsup at value level, hence optimality.
Uniqueness implies convergence of whole sequence.

Theorem 0..53
(Trajectory Lipschitz stability in time-dependent metric)

Let
d_{t}(\mu,\nu):=W_{2}(\mu,\nu)
. Under Assumption
0..50
, for optimal trajectories:

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d_{t}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq L\Big(d_{0}(\mu_{0}^{1},\mu_{0}^{2})+d_{T}(\mu_{T}^{1},\mu_{T}^{2})+\Delta_{u}+\Delta_{\lambda}\Big),

for some
L=L(T,M_{1},M_{2},M_{3},C_{\star},\delta)
.

Proof

Use dynamic plan coupling along two optimal current-velocity fields.
Differentiate squared distance along coupled flow:

\frac{d}{dt}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq 2W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\,\|v_{t}^{1}-v_{t}^{2}\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})},

with
\pi_{t}
optimal coupling. Bound
\|v^{1}-v^{2}\|
by decomposition

(v^{1}-v^{2})=(u_{1}^{\star}-u_{2}^{\star})+\varepsilon\nabla\log(\beta^{1}/\alpha^{1})-\varepsilon\nabla\log(\beta^{2}/\alpha^{2})+\text{budget-response term},

using endpoint/diffusion potential stability and multiplier bound from Proposition
38
.
Apply Grönwall.

Remark 20
(Consequence for training robustness)

These bounds imply that small perturbations in marginals, drift parameterization, or entropy budget
produce controlled changes in optimal trajectories and objective. This is the theoretical backbone for
robustness claims in entropy-controlled generative transport.

Output used next.

E.5
translates these stability/uniqueness results into the strict-convexity geodesic statement and
prepares the
\lambda\to 0
asymptotics of Section
F
.

E.5. Strict geodesics and
\Gamma
-asymptotics

This subsection consolidates Section
E
into a single strict-convex geodesic statement,
derives quantitative corollaries used later for mode-coverage and failure analyses,
and sets the exact functionals for Section
F
(
\lambda\to 0
limit).

Definition 47
(Entropic action functional in current-velocity form)

For
(\rho,v)
with

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v)=0,\qquad\rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho_{|T}=\rho_{T},

define

\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon,u^{\star}}(\rho,v):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,\rho\,dxdt

and feasible class with entropy budget

\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}:=\{(\rho,v):\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})\geq-\lambda\ \text{a.e.}\}.

Theorem 0..54
(Strict-convex geodesic characterization)

Assume
E.3
regularity and
E.4
uniqueness hypotheses. Then for each

(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\varepsilon,\lambda)
, the minimization

\inf_{(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}}\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon,u^{\star}}(\rho,v)

has a unique minimizer
(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})
, and the curve

\mu_{t}^{\star}:=\rho_{t}^{\star}dx

coincides with the unique entropic OT interpolation (Schrödinger geodesic)
between
\mu_{0}
and
\mu_{T}
relative to
R^{\star}
.

Proof

Existence and strong duality:
E.2
.
Uniqueness of minimizing flux/velocity:
E.4
Theorem
0..51
.
Identification with SB interpolation:
E.2
Theorem
0..46
.
Therefore minimizer is exactly unique entropic geodesic.

Corollary 27
(Equivalent characterizations of the optimizer)

The unique optimal trajectory admits all equivalent representations:

1.

ECFM primal:

(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})=\arg\min_{\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}}\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon,u^{\star}}.

2.

KL-control:

w^{\star}=v^{\star}-u^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\rho^{\star},\quad P^{w^{\star}}=\arg\min\{\mathrm{KL}(P\|R^{\star}):P_{0}=\mu_{0},P_{T}=\mu_{T}\}.

3.

SB path law:

P^{w^{\star}}=\bar{P}^{\varepsilon},\qquad\mu_{t}^{\star}=(X_{t})_{\#}\bar{P}^{\varepsilon}.

4.

Schrödinger potentials:

\rho_{t}^{\star}=\alpha_{t}\beta_{t}r_{t}^{\star},\qquad v_{t}^{\star}=u_{t}^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\frac{\beta_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}.

Proof

Directly from
D.2
–
D.4
and
E.2
equivalence.

Proposition 40
(Energy gap controls trajectory discrepancy)

Let
(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}
be any feasible competitor and

(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})
optimal. Then

\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon,u^{\star}}(\rho,v)-\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-v^{\star}|^{2}\,\rho^{\star}\,dxdt-\mathcal{R}(\rho,\rho^{\star}),

where
\mathcal{R}(\rho,\rho^{\star})\geq 0
is a second-order transport remainder
vanishing when
\rho=\rho^{\star}
(e.g., controlled by
\sup_{t}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})
).

Proof

Use convexity expansion around optimizer:

|v-u^{\star}|^{2}=|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}+2(v^{\star}-u^{\star})\cdot(v-v^{\star})+|v-v^{\star}|^{2}.

Integrate against
\rho^{\star}
, then transfer from
\rho^{\star}
to
\rho
via coupling.
First-order term cancels by optimality stationarity (KKT), leaving quadratic term minus
density-mismatch remainder
\mathcal{R}
, bounded by Wasserstein stability estimates in
E.4
.

Corollary 28
(Quantitative near-optimal rigidity)

If

\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon,u^{\star}}(\rho^{n},v^{n})-\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}\to 0,

then (up to subsequence)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{n},\mu_{t}^{\star})\to 0,\qquad\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}^{n}-v_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}dt\to 0.

If the minimizer is unique, convergence holds for full sequence.

Proof

Apply Proposition
40
plus
E.4
graph stability
(Proposition
39
) and uniqueness.

Proposition 41
(Entropy-budget activity decomposition)

Let

\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{act}}^{\star}=\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda=0\},\quad\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}}^{\star}=\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})+\lambda>0\}.

Then:

\eta_{t}^{\star}>0\Rightarrow t\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{act}}^{\star},\qquad t\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{inact}}^{\star}\Rightarrow\eta_{t}^{\star}=0,

and

v_{t}^{\star}=u_{t}^{\star}-\nabla\varphi_{t}^{\star}+\eta_{t}^{\star}\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}.

Hence entropy correction acts only on active times.

Proof

Immediate from complementary slackness and stationarity (
C.3
–
C.6
).

Remark 21
(Computational meaning for vision trajectories)

The optimal transport path used by the model is not an arbitrary interpolant:
it is the unique entropic geodesic selected by the regularized action.
Therefore temporal generation trajectories inherit structural stability and
non-collapse regularization through the entropy-rate active-set mechanism.

Transition to Section F (
\lambda\to 0
asymptotics).

To study recovery of classical OT, define parameterized functionals:

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,v):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,\rho\,dxdt,&(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda},\\[4.30554pt]
+\infty,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

on the ambient topology

\rho^{n}\rightharpoonup\rho\text{ narrowly uniformly in }t,\qquad m^{n}=\rho^{n}v^{n}\rightharpoonup m\text{ weakly as measures}.

Section F proves:

1.

\Gamma
-
\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{F}_{0}
;

2.

minimizers of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
converge to minimizers of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
;

3.

under matched gauge and vanishing entropic correction,
\mathcal{F}_{0}
identifies classical OT action.

F.
\Gamma
-Convergence as
\lambda\to 0

F.1. Definition of
\Gamma
-convergence and functional setting

We fix the ambient topological space, define the
\lambda
-indexed functionals,
and state the precise notion of
\Gamma
-convergence used in Sections
F.2
–
F.5
.

Ambient trajectory-flux space.

Let

\mathcal{Y}:=\Big\{(\rho,m):\rho_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\ \forall t\in[0,T],\ t\mapsto\rho_{t}\text{ narrowly continuous},\ m\in\mathcal{M}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\Big\}.

Write
m_{t}=\rho_{t}v_{t}
when absolutely continuous w.r.t.
\rho_{t}dt
.

Definition 48
(Admissible continuity-equation class)

Define

\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):=\left\{(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{Y}:\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot m_{t}=0\ \text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\ \rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\ \rho_{|T}=\rho_{T}\right\}.

Definition 49
(Entropy-rate feasible set)

For
\lambda\geq 0
, define

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}:=\left\{(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):t\mapsto\mathcal{H}(\rho_{t})\in AC([0,T]),\ \dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})\geq-\lambda\ \text{a.e.}\right\}.

Definition 50
(Action density and functional)

For fixed reference drift
u^{\star}
, define

f_{u^{\star}}(t,x,\rho,m):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{|m-\rho u_{t}^{\star}(x)|^{2}}{\rho},&\rho>0,\\[3.44444pt]
0,&\rho=0,\ m=0,\\
+\infty,&\rho=0,\ m\neq 0.\end{cases}

Then

\mathcal{A}(\rho,m):=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{u^{\star}}(t,x,\rho_{t},m_{t})\,dxdt.

Define
\lambda
-functional

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,m):=\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}(\rho,m),&(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Remark 22
(Monotonic feasible nesting)

If
0\leq\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}
, then

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{2}},\qquad\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{2}}\leq\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{1}}\ \text{pointwise on }\mathcal{Y}.

Topology for
\Gamma
-analysis.

We equip
\mathcal{Y}
with topology
\tau
defined by:

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m)\iff\begin{cases}\rho_{t}^{n}\rightharpoonup\rho_{t}\ \text{narrowly for each }t,\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\rho_{t}^{n},\rho_{t})\to 0\ \text{(or equivalent tight narrow-uniform)},\\
m^{n}\rightharpoonup m\ \text{weakly-* in measures on }(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.\end{cases}

(Any equivalent compactness topology from
C.1
/
E.3
may be used.)

Definition 51
(
\Gamma
-convergence on
(\mathcal{Y},\tau)
)

A family
\{\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda>0}

\Gamma
-converges to
\mathcal{F}_{0}
as

\lambda\downarrow 0
, denoted

\Gamma\text{-}\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{F}_{0},

if for every
z\in\mathcal{Y}
:

1.

(
liminf inequality
) for every
z_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\tau}z
,

\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)\leq\liminf_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z_{\lambda});

2.

(
recovery sequence
) there exists
z_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\tau}z
such that

\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)\geq\limsup_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z_{\lambda}).

Definition 52
(Limit candidate functional)

Define

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m):=\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}(\rho,m),&(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0},\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where

\mathcal{E}_{0}=\left\{(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}):\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}\right\}.

Remark 23
(Interpretation of
\lambda\downarrow 0
)

As
\lambda\to 0
, admissibility tightens from bounded entropy dissipation

\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
to nonnegative entropy production

\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq 0
.
Thus
\Gamma
-limit captures the sharp zero-budget regime.

Assumption 0..55
(Equicoercivity)

For every
c\in\mathbb{R}
, the sublevel union

\bigcup_{0<\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}}\{(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{Y}:\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,m)\leq c\}

is relatively compact in
(\mathcal{Y},\tau)
.

Lemma 21
(Lower semicontinuity of action under
\tau
)

If
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m)
, then

\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{A}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Proof

f_{u^{\star}}
is convex l.s.c. in
(\rho,m)
(perspective quadratic integrand).
Apply standard weak lower-semicontinuity theorem for integral convex functionals on measure-flux pairs.

Lemma 22
(Closedness of CE constraint)

If
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
and

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m)
, then
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
.

Proof

Pass to limit in distributional CE identity:

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int(\partial_{t}\phi\,d\rho^{n}+\nabla\phi\cdot dm^{n})+\int\phi(0)\,d\mu_{0}-\int\phi(T)\,d\mu_{T}=0.

Weak convergence of
\rho^{n},m^{n}
gives the same identity for
(\rho,m)
.

Assumption 0..56
(Closedness of entropy-rate inequality)

If
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{n}}
,
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
, and

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m)
, then
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
.

Remark 24
(Sufficient condition for Assumption
0..56
)

A sufficient condition is:

1.

uniform integrability of
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho^{n})
in
L^{1}(0,T)
,

2.

weak convergence
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho^{n})\rightharpoonup g
in
L^{1}
,

3.

identification
g=\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho)
via entropy-chain-rule stability.

Then
g\geq 0
a.e. since
g_{n}:=\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho^{n})\geq-\lambda_{n}\to 0
.

Theorem 0..57
(
\Gamma
-compactness principle for minimizers)

Assume:

1.

\Gamma
-convergence:

\Gamma\text{-}\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{F}_{0};

2.

equicoercivity (Assumption
0..55
);

3.

each
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
attains a minimizer
z_{\lambda}
.

Then every cluster point
z^{\star}
of
z_{\lambda}
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
, and

\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\min\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\min\mathcal{F}_{0}.

If
\mathcal{F}_{0}
has unique minimizer,
z_{\lambda}\to z^{\star}
in
\tau
.

Proof

Standard fundamental theorem of
\Gamma
-convergence:
equicoercivity gives compactness of minimizers; liminf + recovery give convergence of minima and
minimality of cluster points. Uniqueness implies full convergence.

What is proved next.

•

F.2
proves the liminf inequality for
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}_{0}
.

•

F.3
constructs recovery sequences (limsup inequality).

•

F.4
identifies minimizer convergence and value convergence.

•

F.5
connects
\mathcal{F}_{0}
to classical OT in the zero-entropy-budget limit.

F.2. Lower bound inequality (liminf)

We prove the
\Gamma
-liminf inequality:
for any
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
and
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m)
,

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Theorem 0..58
(Liminf inequality)

Let
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
, and let
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\subset\mathcal{Y}
satisfy

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m).

Then

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Proof

Set

L:=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in[0,+\infty].

If
L=+\infty
, inequality is trivial. Assume
L<\infty
.
Passing to a subsequence (not relabeled), we may assume

\sup_{n}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n})\leq C<\infty.

By definition of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}
, each
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{n}}
, hence:

1.

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
,

2.

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t}^{n})\geq-\lambda_{n}
a.e.

3.

\mathcal{A}(\rho^{n},m^{n})\leq C
.

Step 1: CE and endpoints pass to the limit.

By Lemma
22
,
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
.

Step 2: entropy-rate inequality passes to the limit.

By Assumption
0..56
, from

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{n}}
,
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
, and
\tau
-convergence,
we infer

(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0},\quad\text{i.e.}\quad\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}

Step 3: l.s.c. of action.

By Lemma
21
,

\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{A}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Since each
(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{n}}
,

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n})=\mathcal{A}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Also, from Step 2,
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
, so

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)=\mathcal{A}(\rho,m).

Hence

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)=\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{A}(\rho^{n},m^{n})=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

This proves the liminf inequality.

Corollary 29
(Sequential liminf on arbitrary sequences)

For any sequence
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
and any
\tau
-convergent sequence

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\to(\rho,m)
,

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n}).

Proof

Immediate from Theorem
0..58
.

Proposition 42
(Consequence for infeasible limits)

If
(\rho,m)\notin\mathcal{E}_{0}
, then every
\tau
-convergent sequence

(\rho^{n},m^{n})\to(\rho,m)
with
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
satisfies

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n})=+\infty.

Proof

If
(\rho,m)\notin\mathcal{E}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)=+\infty
.
Apply Theorem
0..58
:

+\infty=\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)\leq\liminf_{n}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(\rho^{n},m^{n}),

hence liminf is
+\infty
.

Lemma 23
(Equivalent liminf in velocity representation)

Suppose
m^{n}=\rho^{n}v^{n}
,
m=\rho v
, and assumptions of Theorem
0..58
hold.
Then

\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{2}\int|v^{n}-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu^{n}dt,

provided
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
; otherwise RHS is
+\infty
along any admissible approximating sequence.

Proof

This is exactly Lemma
21
rewritten via

\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)=\frac{1}{2}\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu dt

on finite-action pairs.

Remark 25
(Role of entropy closedness)

The only nontrivial constraint passage is
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda_{n}\to 0\Rightarrow\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq 0
.
All other components (CE, endpoints, action l.s.c.) follow from standard weak compactness.
Thus Assumption
0..56
is the key structural hypothesis for the liminf step.

Output used next.

F.3
constructs, for every
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
, a recovery sequence

(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})\to(\rho,m)
with

\limsup_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})\leq\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m),

completing the
\Gamma
-convergence proof.

F.3. Recovery sequence construction (limsup inequality)

We prove the
\Gamma
-limsup inequality:
for every
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{Y}
, there exists
(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})\xrightarrow{\tau}(\rho,m)

such that

\limsup_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})\leq\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m).

Theorem 0..59
(Limsup inequality / existence of recovery sequence)

For every
z:=(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{Y}
, there exists
z_{\lambda}:=(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})\in\mathcal{Y}

with
z_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\tau}z
and

\limsup_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z_{\lambda})\leq\mathcal{F}_{0}(z).

Proof

We split into cases.

Case A:
\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)=+\infty
.

Choose constant sequence
z_{\lambda}=z
. Then
z_{\lambda}\to z
, and

\limsup_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z_{\lambda})\leq+\infty=\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)

trivially.

Case B:
\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)<\infty
.

Then
z=(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
, i.e.

(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}),\qquad\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t})\geq 0\ \text{a.e.},\qquad\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)<\infty.

For any
\lambda>0
, since
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq 0
, we also have

\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda,

hence
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
. Therefore

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,m)=\mathcal{A}(\rho,m)=\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m).

Set the constant recovery sequence

(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda}):=(\rho,m)\quad\forall\lambda>0.

Then
z_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\tau}z
, and

\limsup_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z_{\lambda})=\mathcal{F}_{0}(z).

Thus the limsup inequality holds (with equality).

Corollary 30
(Exact recovery on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
)

If
(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
, the constant sequence is an exact recovery sequence:

(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})\equiv(\rho,m),\qquad\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho^{\lambda},m^{\lambda})=\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,m)\ \forall\lambda>0.

Proof

Immediate from Case B in Theorem
0..59
.

Remark 26
(Why no boundary layer is needed)

Because feasible sets are nested:

\mathcal{E}_{0}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}\quad(\lambda>0),

every
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-finite point is already feasible for each
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
,
and both functionals coincide there (
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{F}_{0}
).
Hence unlike singular-perturbation problems, no smoothing/boundary-layer construction is needed.

Proposition 43
(Monotone family and pointwise convergence)

For every
z=(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{Y}
,

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z)\searrow\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)\quad\text{as }\lambda\downarrow 0

(pointwise monotone decrease to the limit functional).

Proof

From feasible-set nesting
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda_{2}}
for

\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}
, we get

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{2}}(z)\leq\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{1}}(z).

If
z\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
, then
z\in\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
for all
\lambda
, and

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z)=\mathcal{A}(z)=\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)
.
If
z\notin\mathcal{E}_{0}
, either CE/action fail (then all
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z)=+\infty
),
or entropy-rate fails at level
0
: then for sufficiently small
\lambda
, constraint

\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
also fails on a positive-measure set, so
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(z)=+\infty

eventually. Thus pointwise limit is
\mathcal{F}_{0}(z)
.

Theorem 0..60
(
\Gamma
-convergence conclusion for Section F.1 setup)

Under Assumption
0..56
and Lemma
21
,

\Gamma\text{-}\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\quad\text{in }(\mathcal{Y},\tau).

Proof

Liminf: Theorem
0..58
.
Limsup: Theorem
0..59
.
Therefore
\Gamma
-convergence holds.

Corollary 31
(Convergence of minima under equicoercivity)

Assume equicoercivity (Assumption
0..55
) and existence of minimizers

z_{\lambda}\in\arg\min\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
. Then

\min\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\to\min\mathcal{F}_{0},

and every cluster point of
z_{\lambda}
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
.
If
\mathcal{F}_{0}
has unique minimizer
z^{\star}
, then

z_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\tau}z^{\star}.

Proof

Apply Theorem
0..57
with
Theorem
0..60
.

Output used next.

F.4
uses Corollary
31
to establish explicit convergence of minimizers,
actions, and optimal trajectories; then
F.5
identifies the limit problem with classical OT.

F.4. Limit functional identification and convergence of minimizers

Using
F.2
–
F.3
, we now identify the
\lambda\downarrow 0
limit minimization problem,
prove convergence of optimal values/minimizers, and derive convergence of trajectories/velocities.

Definition 53
(Optimal values and minimizers)

For
\lambda\geq 0
, define

\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}:=\inf_{(\rho,m)\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,m),\qquad\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}:=\arg\min\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}.

Theorem 0..61
(Convergence of optimal values)

Assume:

1.

\Gamma
-convergence from Theorem
0..60
,

2.

equicoercivity (Assumption
0..55
),

3.

\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}\neq\emptyset
for small
\lambda
, and
\mathcal{M}_{0}\neq\emptyset
.

Then

\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}=\mathsf{V}_{0}.

Proof

By
\Gamma
-convergence + equicoercivity (fundamental theorem), minima converge:

\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\min\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\min\mathcal{F}_{0}.

By Definition
53
, this is exactly
\mathsf{V}_{\lambda}\to\mathsf{V}_{0}
.

Theorem 0..62
(Compactness and convergence of minimizers)

Let
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
, and choose
z_{n}:=(\rho^{n},m^{n})\in\mathcal{M}_{\lambda_{n}}
.
Then:

1.

(z_{n})
is relatively compact in
(\mathcal{Y},\tau)
;

2.

every cluster point
z^{\star}=(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})
belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{0}
;

3.

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}(z_{n})\to\mathcal{F}_{0}(z^{\star})=\mathsf{V}_{0}

along any convergent subsequence.

Proof

Equicoercivity yields compactness of minimizing sequence
(z_{n})
.

\Gamma
-compactness theorem implies cluster points are minimizers of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
.
Value convergence follows from Theorem
0..61
and optimality of
z_{n}
.

Corollary 32
(Full convergence under uniqueness)

If
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\{z^{\star}\}
is a singleton, then for any choice
z_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}
,

z_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\tau}z^{\star}\quad(\lambda\downarrow 0).

Proof

All cluster points must belong to
\mathcal{M}_{0}
, hence equal
z^{\star}
.
Therefore the whole family converges.

Proposition 44
(Limit problem is sharp zero-budget ECFM)

The limit minimization is exactly

\mathsf{V}_{0}=\inf\left\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}dt:\partial_{t}\mu_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}v_{t})=0,\ \mu_{0},\mu_{T}\ \text{fixed},\ \dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}\right\}.

Proof

Immediate from definition of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
(Definition
52
)
and representation
m=\rho v
,
d\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx
.

Theorem 0..63
(Convergence of trajectories)

Let
z_{\lambda_{n}}=(\rho^{\lambda_{n}},m^{\lambda_{n}})\in\mathcal{M}_{\lambda_{n}}
,

z_{\lambda_{n}}\xrightarrow{\tau}z^{\star}=(\rho^{\star},m^{\star})\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Then

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{\lambda_{n}},\mu_{t}^{\star})\to 0.

If
m^{\star}=\rho^{\star}v^{\star}
,
m^{\lambda_{n}}=\rho^{\lambda_{n}}v^{\lambda_{n}}
, then
(up to subsequence, and globally if unique):

v^{\lambda_{n}}\rightharpoonup v^{\star}\quad\text{in weighted }L^{2}(dt\,d\mu^{\star})\ \text{(via flux convergence)}.

Proof

First claim is part of
\tau
-convergence definition.
For velocities, action bounds

\sup_{n}\int\frac{|m^{\lambda_{n}}-\rho^{\lambda_{n}}u^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho^{\lambda_{n}}}<\infty

give weak compactness of fluxes.
With
m^{\lambda_{n}}\rightharpoonup m^{\star}
and
\rho^{\lambda_{n}}\to\rho^{\star}
,
identify limit velocity by Radon–Nikodým decomposition
m^{\star}=\rho^{\star}v^{\star}
.

Proposition 45
(Energy convergence and no-loss of dissipation)

Along minimizing family
z_{\lambda_{n}}\to z^{\star}
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{|m_{t}^{\lambda_{n}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{n}}u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{n}}}\,dxdt=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{|m_{t}^{\star}-\rho_{t}^{\star}u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}}{\rho_{t}^{\star}}\,dxdt.

Proof

Lower bound by l.s.c. (Lemma
21
):

\mathcal{A}(z^{\star})\leq\liminf_{n}\mathcal{A}(z_{\lambda_{n}}).

Upper bound from minimal value convergence:

\limsup_{n}\mathcal{A}(z_{\lambda_{n}})=\limsup_{n}\mathsf{V}_{\lambda_{n}}=\mathsf{V}_{0}=\mathcal{A}(z^{\star}).

Hence liminf=limsup=limit equals
\mathcal{A}(z^{\star})
.

Corollary 33
(Convergence of entropy-rate constraints)

For minimizers
z_{\lambda_{n}}
, any weak
L^{1}
-limit
g
of

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho^{\lambda_{n}})
satisfies
g\geq 0
a.e.
and identifies with
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho^{\star})
under chain-rule stability.
Hence limit minimizer satisfies sharp constraint

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t}^{\star})\geq 0\ \text{a.e.}

Proof

Since
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho^{\lambda_{n}})\geq-\lambda_{n}
,
\lambda_{n}\to 0
,
any weak limit is nonnegative.
Identification with limit entropy derivative follows from Assumption
0..56

(or sufficient condition in Remark
24
).

Remark 27
(Bridge to classical OT)

Section
F
so far gives convergence to the zero-budget constrained action
\mathcal{F}_{0}
.
Section
F5
adds the final identification step:
under matched scaling/vanishing entropic correction,
\mathcal{F}_{0}
reduces to
the classical Benamou–Brenier OT action (possibly with drift gauge removed),
yielding recovery of OT geodesics.

F.5. Classical OT identification and
\lambda\to 0
recovery theorem

We complete Section
F
by identifying the zero-budget limit with classical
Benamou–Brenier optimal transport under a vanishing-entropic-correction regime.

Definition 54
(Classical Benamou–Brenier action)

For endpoint marginals
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
, define

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{BB}}(\rho,v):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}(x)|^{2}\,\rho_{t}(x)\,dxdt,

on

\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})=\{(\rho,v):\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v)=0,\ \rho_{|0}=\rho_{0},\rho_{|T}=\rho_{T}\}.

The dynamic OT value is

\mathsf{W}_{\mathrm{dyn}}^{2}:=\inf_{(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{CE}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{BB}}(\rho,v),

and equals
\frac{1}{2T}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
under standard time-scaling convention.

Definition 55
(Zero-drift gauge and correction term)

In the unbiased gauge
u^{\star}\equiv 0
, define

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,v)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt,&(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{X}_{\lambda},\\
+\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Recall exact identity from
D.3
(with
u^{\star}=0
):

\frac{1}{2}\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt=2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{KL}(P^{w}\|R)-\varepsilon\big(\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})\big)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\,dt.

The last two terms are the entropic correction.

Assumption 0..64
(Vanishing-entropic-correction regime)

Along the
\lambda\downarrow 0
minimizer sequence
(\rho^{\lambda},v^{\lambda})
, assume:

1.

either
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\lambda)\downarrow 0
, or

2.

\varepsilon>0
fixed but

\varepsilon\left|\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T}^{\lambda})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0}^{\lambda})\right|+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}^{\lambda})\,dt\to 0.

Remark 28
(Why Assumption
0..64
is natural)

In entropic OT, classical OT is recovered as diffusion/entropy regularization vanishes.
Assumption
0..64
is the precise dynamic counterpart: KL-control energy
dominates while entropy/Fisher corrections disappear.

Theorem 0..65
(Identification of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
with BB action)

Assume
u^{\star}\equiv 0
,
E.3
regularity, and vanishing-entropic-correction regime.
Then, on CE-admissible limits,

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,v)=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{BB}}(\rho,v),

and minimizers of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
are exactly Benamou–Brenier minimizers.

Proof

From
F.4
,
\mathcal{F}_{0}
is zero-budget constrained kinetic action:

\mathcal{F}_{0}(\rho,v)=\frac{1}{2}\int|v|^{2}\,d\mu dt\quad\text{if }(\rho,v)\in\mathcal{X}_{0}.

Hence
\mathcal{F}_{0}
equals BB action on
\mathcal{X}_{0}\subset\mathcal{CE}
.
By
D.3
identity, discrepancy between kinetic action and scaled KL objective is exactly entropic correction.
Under Assumption
0..64
, this discrepancy vanishes along minimizing sequences.
Therefore limit minimization is governed solely by BB kinetic term.
Thus
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-minimizers coincide with BB minimizers.

Proposition 46
(Constraint saturation in the limit)

Let
(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})
be BB minimizer with finite entropy curve and

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\rho_{t}^{\star})\geq 0
a.e. (or approximable by such curves).
Then
(\rho^{\star},v^{\star})\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
, so zero-budget constraint is non-restrictive on the OT minimizer class.

Proof

If
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq 0
a.e., membership in
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is immediate.
If only approximable, use density of smooth CE curves and lower-semicontinuity of action to pass to limit.

Theorem 0..66
(Recovery of classical OT geodesic as
\lambda\to 0
)

Let
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
, and choose minimizers

(\rho^{\lambda_{n}},v^{\lambda_{n}})\in\arg\min\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{n}}.

Assume equicoercivity, uniqueness of BB minimizer
(\rho^{\mathrm{OT}},v^{\mathrm{OT}})
,
and Assumption
0..64
. Then

\rho^{\lambda_{n}}\xrightarrow{\tau}\rho^{\mathrm{OT}},\qquad\frac{1}{2}\int|v^{\lambda_{n}}|^{2}\,d\mu^{\lambda_{n}}dt\to\frac{1}{2}\int|v^{\mathrm{OT}}|^{2}\,d\mu^{\mathrm{OT}}dt=\mathsf{W}_{\mathrm{dyn}}^{2}.

Hence the ECFM trajectory converges to the classical Wasserstein geodesic.

Proof

By
F.3
–
F.4
, minimizers converge (up to subsequences) to minimizers of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
, with value convergence.
By Theorem
0..65
,
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-minimizers are BB minimizers.
Uniqueness of BB minimizer yields full convergence to
(\rho^{\mathrm{OT}},v^{\mathrm{OT}})
.
Energy convergence follows from
F.4
no-loss proposition.

Corollary 34
(Static formulation limit)

Under the same hypotheses,

\lim_{\lambda\downarrow 0}\inf\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2T}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{T}),

and endpoint couplings converge (via dynamic plans) to an optimal quadratic-cost transport plan.

Proof

Benamou–Brenier theorem identifies BB value with quadratic OT cost.
Dynamic-plan compactness gives convergence of endpoint couplings to OT-optimal plan.

Remark 29
(With nonzero drift
u^{\star}
)

If
u^{\star}\neq 0
, apply a gauge transform to co-moving coordinates (or subtract reference flow):
the limit identifies a drift-corrected OT problem. When the drift contribution vanishes in the limit,
classical OT is recovered; otherwise the limit is OT in the transformed frame.

Remark 30
(Section F conclusion)

Sections
F.1
–
F.5
establish:

1.

\Gamma
-convergence
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}\to\mathcal{F}_{0}
as
\lambda\downarrow 0
;

2.

convergence of minima and minimizers;

3.

identification of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
with classical BB action under vanishing entropic correction;

4.

recovery of classical OT/Wasserstein geodesics from entropy-controlled flow matching.

Transition to Section
G
.

With the asymptotic limit resolved, Section
G
proves the mode-coverage theorem:
entropy-rate control prevents singular concentration and supplies quantitative lower-mass bounds
on modes along the transport trajectory.

G. Mode Coverage Theorem

G.1. Formal definition of mode collapse and mode-coverage statement

We formalize mode collapse as loss of mass on designated modal regions and state
the quantitative coverage theorem induced by the entropy-rate constraint.

Definition 56
(Modal partition / mode sets)

Let
\{A_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
be pairwise disjoint Borel subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{d}

with positive target masses:

\mu_{T}(A_{k})=\pi_{k}>0,\qquad\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\leq 1.

(Residual mass outside
\cup_{k}A_{k}
is allowed.)

Definition 57
(Mode mass process)

For a trajectory
(\mu_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
, define per-mode mass

M_{k}(t):=\mu_{t}(A_{k}),\qquad k=1,\dots,K.

Definition 58
(Hard mode collapse)

A trajectory exhibits hard collapse on mode
k
over interval
I\subset[0,T]

if there exists
t_{\star}\in I
such that

M_{k}(t_{\star})=0\quad\text{while}\quad\mu_{T}(A_{k})=\pi_{k}>0.

Global hard collapse occurs if this holds for at least one
k
.

Definition 59
(Soft
(\delta,\tau)
-mode collapse)

Given
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\tau\in(0,T]
, trajectory has soft collapse on mode
k
if

\left|\left\{t\in[0,T]:M_{k}(t)\leq\delta\,\pi_{k}\right\}\right|\geq\tau.

Definition 60
(Uniform mode coverage)

A trajectory satisfies uniform
\underline{c}
-coverage on
\{A_{k}\}
if

M_{k}(t)\geq\underline{c}\,\pi_{k},\qquad\forall t\in[0,T],\ \forall k\in\{1,\dots,K\},

for some
\underline{c}\in(0,1]
.

Assumption 0..67
(Regular modal geometry)

Assume:

1.

each
A_{k}
has Lipschitz boundary and finite diameter;

2.

there exist disjoint open neighborhoods
U_{k}\supset A_{k}
with separation

\mathrm{dist}(U_{i},U_{j})\geq\Delta_{\mathrm{sep}}>0
for
i\neq j
;

3.

along admissible trajectories,
\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx
with
\rho_{t}>0
a.e.,

\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\in AC
, and finite Fisher action.

Assumption 0..68
(Entropy-controlled admissibility)

Trajectory
(\mu_{t},v_{t})
is ECFM-admissible:

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}v_{t})=0,\qquad\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda\ \text{a.e.}

for some
\lambda\geq 0
, with fixed endpoints
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
.

Definition 61
(Collapsed comparison profile)

Fix mode
k
. Define
\bar{\mu}^{(k,\eta)}
as any measure obtained from
\mu_{t}
by
removing an
\eta
-fraction of mass from
A_{k}
and redistributing it within

\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus A_{k}
, preserving total mass and finite second moment.

Lemma 24
(Entropy drop under mode depletion)

Let
m:=M_{k}(t)\in(0,1)
. If mode mass is reduced to
(1-\eta)m
,
\eta\in(0,1)
,
while keeping outside mass fixed up to renormalization, then entropy decreases by at least

\Delta\mathcal{H}_{k}\geq c_{k}\,m\,\eta\,\log\frac{1}{m}

for a geometry-dependent
c_{k}\in(0,1]
, up to higher-order
O(\eta^{2})
terms.

Remark 31

Lemma
24
is proved in
G.2
using convexity of
s\mapsto s\log s
,
localization to
A_{k}
, and bounded-distortion redistribution outside
A_{k}
.

Theorem 0..69
(Mode-coverage theorem under entropy-rate control)

Under Assumptions
0..67
–
0..68
, let

(\mu_{t},v_{t})
be an ECFM minimizer (equivalently SB interpolation in Section E).
Then for each mode
k
with
\pi_{k}=\mu_{T}(A_{k})>0
, there exists

\underline{c}_{k}=\underline{c}_{k}\!\left(\pi_{k},\lambda,T,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0}),\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T}),\text{geom}(A_{k})\right)>0

such that

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}M_{k}(t)\ \geq\ \underline{c}_{k}\,\pi_{k}.

In particular, hard collapse is impossible on any positive-target mode.

Theorem 0..70
(Quantitative soft-collapse exclusion)

For any
\delta\in(0,1)
, define

\tau_{\max}(\delta,k):=\frac{\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})+\lambda T}{c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta\,\log\!\frac{1}{\delta}}.

Then along any ECFM-admissible optimizer,

\left|\left\{t:M_{k}(t)\leq\delta\pi_{k}\right\}\right|\leq\tau_{\max}(\delta,k).

Hence prolonged soft collapse is excluded unless entropy budget is sufficiently loose.

Corollary 35
(Global multi-mode coverage)

Let
\pi_{\min}:=\min_{k}\pi_{k}
,
c_{\min}:=\min_{k}c_{k}
. Then

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\min_{1\leq k\leq K}\frac{M_{k}(t)}{\pi_{k}}\geq\underline{c}_{\mathrm{glob}}:=\underline{c}_{\mathrm{glob}}(c_{\min},\pi_{\min},\lambda,T,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{0}),\mathcal{H}(\mu_{T})).

Therefore the trajectory preserves all target modes at uniformly positive relative mass.

Corollary 36
(No implicit mode collapse in the zero-budget limit)

If
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
and
(\mu^{\lambda_{n}}_{t})
are corresponding minimizers converging to

(\mu_{t}^{0})
(Section F), then
(\mu_{t}^{0})
satisfies the same non-collapse lower bound
with
\lambda=0
. Hence zero-budget limit retains mode coverage.

Remark 32
(Connection to generative modeling)

In generative transport terms,
A_{k}
represent semantic modes/classes/attributes.
Theorem
0..69
states that entropy-rate control enforces
non-vanishing occupancy of each target mode throughout generation time, preventing
implicit single-mode concentration typical of unconstrained deterministic flows.

What is proved next (
G.2
–
G.4
).

•

G.2
proves Lemma
24
(entropy drop from localized mass depletion).

•

G.3
proves Theorems
0..69
and
0..70

using entropy-budget integration and contradiction.

•

G.4
derives density-floor and stability bounds (
\rho_{t}
lower-envelope on modal sets).

G.2. Entropy barrier against singular mass concentration

We prove that depleting mass on a target mode induces a quantifiable entropy loss.
Combined with the entropy-rate budget, this yields a barrier against collapse.

Notation.

Fix a mode
A:=A_{k}
and write

m:=\mu(A)\in(0,1),\qquad\mu=\rho\,dx,\qquad\mu=\mu|_{A}+\mu|_{A^{c}}.

Let normalized restrictions be

\mu_{A}:=\frac{1}{m}\mu|_{A},\qquad\mu_{A^{c}}:=\frac{1}{1-m}\mu|_{A^{c}}.

Then

\mu=m\,\mu_{A}+(1-m)\,\mu_{A^{c}}.

Lemma 25
(Entropy decomposition by region)

For any absolutely continuous
\mu
and Borel set
A
with
m=\mu(A)\in(0,1)
,

\mathcal{H}(\mu)=m\log m+(1-m)\log(1-m)+m\,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A})+(1-m)\,\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A^{c}}).

Proof

Let
\rho_{A},\rho_{A^{c}}
be densities of
\mu_{A},\mu_{A^{c}}
. Then

\rho=m\,\rho_{A}\mathbf{1}_{A}+(1-m)\,\rho_{A^{c}}\mathbf{1}_{A^{c}}.

Hence

\int\rho\log\rho=\int_{A}m\rho_{A}\log(m\rho_{A})+\int_{A^{c}}(1-m)\rho_{A^{c}}\log((1-m)\rho_{A^{c}}),

which expands to the claimed identity because
\int_{A}\rho_{A}=\int_{A^{c}}\rho_{A^{c}}=1
.

Lemma 26
(Binary entropy monotonicity on low-mass regime)

Define
b(m):=m\log m+(1-m)\log(1-m)
,
m\in(0,1)
.
For
m\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
b^{\prime}(m)=\log\frac{m}{1-m}\leq 0
, and for
\eta\in(0,1)
,

b((1-\eta)m)-b(m)\leq-\eta m\log\frac{1-m}{m}\leq-\eta m\log\frac{1}{2m}.

Proof

By convexity of
b
,

b((1-\eta)m)-b(m)\leq-\eta m\,b^{\prime}(m)=-\eta m\log\frac{m}{1-m}=-\eta m\log\frac{1-m}{m}.

If
m\leq\frac{1}{2}
, then
(1-m)/m\geq 1/(2m)
, giving second bound.

Definition 62
(Controlled depletion transform)

Given
\mu
and
A
, define depleted measure
\tilde{\mu}
with parameter
\eta\in(0,1)
:

\tilde{\mu}(A)=(1-\eta)m,\qquad\tilde{\mu}(A^{c})=1-(1-\eta)m.

Inside
A
, keep shape:

\tilde{\mu}|_{A}=(1-\eta)\mu|_{A}.

Outside
A
, redistribute depleted mass by mixing with a reference
\nu\ll dx
,
\nu(A)=0
:

\tilde{\mu}|_{A^{c}}=\mu|_{A^{c}}+\eta m\,\nu.

Assumption 0..71
(Bounded-distortion redistribution)

Assume
\nu
satisfies

\mathcal{H}(\nu)\leq C_{\nu},\qquad\int_{A^{c}}\rho_{A^{c}}\log\frac{\rho_{A^{c}}}{\nu}\,dx\leq C_{\mathrm{mix}}.

This controls entropy increase due to outside redistribution.

Proposition 47
(Entropy drop under mode depletion)

Under Definition
62
and Assumption
0..71
,
for
m\leq\frac{1}{2}
,

\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mu})-\mathcal{H}(\mu)\leq-\eta m\log\frac{1}{2m}+\eta m\,C_{\mathrm{out}}+C_{2}\,\eta^{2}m,

where
C_{\mathrm{out}},C_{2}
depend only on
C_{\nu},C_{\mathrm{mix}}
.
Consequently, if
m\leq m_{\star}:=\frac{1}{2}e^{-2C_{\mathrm{out}}}
and
\eta\leq\eta_{\star}
,

\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mu})-\mathcal{H}(\mu)\leq-\frac{1}{2}\,\eta m\log\frac{1}{m}.

Proof

Apply Lemma
25
to
\mu,\tilde{\mu}
:

\Delta\mathcal{H}=\underbrace{b((1-\eta)m)-b(m)}_{(\mathrm{I})}+\underbrace{(1-\eta)m\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A})-m\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A})}_{(\mathrm{II})}+\underbrace{\tilde{w}\,\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mu}_{A^{c}})-(1-m)\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A^{c}})}_{(\mathrm{III})},

with
\tilde{w}=1-(1-\eta)m
.

Term
(\mathrm{I})
: by Lemma
26
,

(\mathrm{I})\leq-\eta m\log\frac{1}{2m}.

Term
(\mathrm{II})=-\eta m\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A})
, absorbed into
O(\eta m)
since
\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A})
bounded below on regular class.

Term
(\mathrm{III})
: outside normalized density is mixture

\tilde{\mu}_{A^{c}}=\frac{1-m}{\tilde{w}}\mu_{A^{c}}+\frac{\eta m}{\tilde{w}}\nu.

Convexity of entropy for mixtures plus KL-control in Assumption
0..71
gives

\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mu}_{A^{c}})\leq\frac{1-m}{\tilde{w}}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{A^{c}})+\frac{\eta m}{\tilde{w}}\mathcal{H}(\nu)+C_{\mathrm{mix}}\frac{\eta m}{\tilde{w}}+O((\eta m)^{2}),

hence

(\mathrm{III})\leq\eta m\,C_{\mathrm{out}}+C_{2}\eta^{2}m.

Combine terms to obtain first inequality.
For second inequality, choose
m_{\star},\eta_{\star}
so positive terms are at most

\frac{1}{2}\eta m\log(1/m)
.

Corollary 37
(Proof of Lemma
24
)

Under regular modal geometry and bounded-distortion redistribution,
there exists
c_{k}\in(0,1]
such that for mode
A_{k}
, depletion by fraction
\eta
yields

\Delta\mathcal{H}_{k}:=\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mu})-\mathcal{H}(\mu)\leq-\,c_{k}\,m\,\eta\,\log\frac{1}{m}+O(\eta^{2}m).

Equivalently, entropy drops by at least

-\Delta\mathcal{H}_{k}\geq c_{k}\,m\,\eta\,\log\frac{1}{m}-O(\eta^{2}m).

Proof

Immediate from Proposition
47
, with constants absorbed into
c_{k}
.

Theorem 0..72
(Instantaneous concentration barrier)

Let
t\mapsto\mu_{t}
be admissible with
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda
a.e.
Fix mode
A_{k}
. If over a short interval
[t,t+h]
, mode mass drops from
m
to
(1-\eta)m
,
and redistribution satisfies Assumption
0..71
, then

\frac{\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t+h})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})}{h}\leq-\frac{c_{k}\,m\,\eta\,\log(1/m)}{h}+O\!\left(\frac{\eta^{2}m}{h}\right).

Therefore such depletion is impossible whenever RHS
<-\lambda
.

Proof

Apply Corollary
37
to the endpoint pair
(\mu_{t},\mu_{t+h})
under the depletion map.
Divide by
h
. Entropy-rate constraint forbids average rate below
-\lambda
, yielding the condition.

Remark 33
(Mechanism)

Mode depletion forces negative entropy jump of order
m\eta\log(1/m)
.
The budget
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
caps admissible entropy loss per unit time.
Hence sufficiently strong/fast concentration is infeasible, creating a quantitative anti-collapse barrier.

Output used next.

G.3
integrates Theorem
0..72
over time to prove
Theorem
0..69
and Theorem
0..70

(global lower bounds and soft-collapse duration bounds).

G.3. Proof of mode-coverage and soft-collapse exclusion theorems

We now prove Theorems
0..69
and
0..70

using the entropy-drop estimate from
G.2
and the global entropy budget.

Setup.

Fix a mode
A_{k}
with terminal mass
\pi_{k}=\mu_{T}(A_{k})>0
.
Define

M(t):=\mu_{t}(A_{k}),\qquad t\in[0,T].

Let

E(t):=\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t}),\qquad\dot{E}(t)\geq-\lambda\ \text{a.e.}

and denote net entropy budget

\mathfrak{B}:=E(0)-E(T)+\lambda T\geq 0.

(Indeed
E(T)-E(0)\geq-\lambda T
.)

Lemma 27
(Integrated entropy-loss bound)

For every measurable
I\subset[0,T]
,

-\int_{I}\dot{E}(t)\,dt\leq\mathfrak{B}.

Proof

-\int_{I}\dot{E}\leq-\int_{0}^{T}\dot{E}=E(0)-E(T)\leq E(0)-E(T)+\lambda T=\mathfrak{B}.

Lemma 28
(Pointwise dissipation lower bound on low-mass times)

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. Assume
M(t)\leq\delta\pi_{k}
and modal geometry assumptions of
G.1
/
G.2
.
Then there exists
c_{k}>0
such that at a.e. such
t
,

-\dot{E}(t)\ \geq\ c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta\,\log\frac{1}{\delta}\quad\text{up to controlled higher-order terms}.

After absorbing higher-order terms into
c_{k}
, we keep the same form.

Proof

At time
t
, compare
\mu_{t}
to a hypothetical profile with restored modal mass to scale
\pi_{k}

over an infinitesimal step. By
G.2
(Corollary
37
and
Theorem
0..72
), depletion at level
M(t)
incurs entropy-loss rate
bounded below by
c_{k}M(t)\log(1/M(t))
.
Since
M(t)\leq\delta\pi_{k}
and
x\log(1/x)
is increasing on
(0,e^{-1}]
,
for
\delta\pi_{k}
in that regime (or by truncation constants otherwise),

M(t)\log\frac{1}{M(t)}\geq\pi_{k}\delta\log\frac{1}{\delta}-C_{\pi_{k}}\delta.

Absorb
C_{\pi_{k}}\delta
into constants to obtain stated bound.

Theorem 0..73
(Proof of soft-collapse exclusion)

Under Assumptions
0..67
–
0..68
,
for every
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\left|\{t\in[0,T]:M(t)\leq\delta\pi_{k}\}\right|\leq\frac{\mathfrak{B}}{c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta\,\log(1/\delta)}=:\tau_{\max}(\delta,k).

Hence Theorem
0..70
holds.

Proof

Define low-mass set

S_{\delta}:=\{t\in[0,T]:M(t)\leq\delta\pi_{k}\}.

By Lemma
28
, a.e. on
S_{\delta}
,

-\dot{E}(t)\geq c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta\,\log\frac{1}{\delta}.

Integrate over
S_{\delta}
:

c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta\,\log\frac{1}{\delta}\ |S_{\delta}|\leq\int_{S_{\delta}}(-\dot{E}(t))\,dt\leq\mathfrak{B}

by Lemma
27
. Rearrangement gives the claim.

Corollary 38
(Exclusion of prolonged near-empty modes)

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\tau>0
. If

\mathfrak{B}<c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta\,\log(1/\delta)\,\tau,

then

|\{t:M(t)\leq\delta\pi_{k}\}|<\tau.

Proof

Immediate from Theorem
0..73
.

Theorem 0..74
(Proof of mode-coverage lower bound)

Under the same assumptions, there exists
\underline{c}_{k}>0
such that

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}M(t)\geq\underline{c}_{k}\,\pi_{k}.

Therefore hard collapse is impossible (
M(t)=0
cannot occur for any
t
).

Proof

Assume contrary: for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
t_{n}
with

M(t_{n})\leq\frac{\pi_{k}}{n}.

Set
\delta_{n}:=1/n
. Then
t_{n}\in S_{\delta_{n}}
, so
|S_{\delta_{n}}|>0
.
By Theorem
0..73
,

|S_{\delta_{n}}|\leq\frac{\mathfrak{B}}{c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta_{n}\,\log(1/\delta_{n})}=\frac{\mathfrak{B}}{c_{k}\,\pi_{k}}\cdot\frac{n}{\log n}.

This upper bound alone does not contradict positivity of
|S_{\delta_{n}}|
; we need
a local-time argument near each
t_{n}
: by CE regularity and finite action,
M(\cdot)\in AC([0,T])
,
and

|\dot{M}(t)|\leq\int_{\partial A_{k}}|v_{t}\cdot n|\,\rho_{t}\,d\sigma

in trace sense, yielding finite
L^{1}
-bound on
|\dot{M}|
. Hence every deep dip to
\delta_{n}\pi_{k}

creates a nontrivial interval
I_{n}
where
M\leq 2\delta_{n}\pi_{k}
, with length bounded below by

|I_{n}|\geq\frac{\delta_{n}\pi_{k}}{\|\dot{M}\|_{L^{\infty}\text{ or controlled }L^{1}\text{ scale}}}.

On
I_{n}
, Lemma
28
gives dissipation at least

c_{k}\,\pi_{k}\,\delta_{n}\log(1/\delta_{n}).

Therefore entropy loss over
I_{n}
is bounded below by

\gtrsim|I_{n}|\,\delta_{n}\log(1/\delta_{n})\gtrsim\delta_{n}^{2}\log(1/\delta_{n}).

Summing over infinitely many
n
along a subsequence of separated dips would force
total entropy loss beyond budget
\mathfrak{B}
, contradiction.
Hence
\inf_{t}M(t)\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}
for some
\underline{c}_{k}>0
.

Finally, hard collapse
M(t_{\star})=0
implies existence of arbitrarily small
\delta
with

t_{\star}\in S_{\delta}
, violating the established positive lower bound.

Remark 34

A more compact contradiction uses compactness of minimizers and lower semicontinuity:
if a sequence of admissible trajectories had
\inf_{t}M(t)\to 0
, any limit would exhibit
