hard collapse, which is excluded by the entropy barrier in
G.2
plus positive terminal mass
\pi_{k}
.

Corollary 39
(Proof of global multi-mode coverage)

For finitely many modes
\{A_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
, let
\underline{c}_{\mathrm{glob}}:=\min_{k}\underline{c}_{k}
.
Then

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\min_{k}\frac{\mu_{t}(A_{k})}{\pi_{k}}\geq\underline{c}_{\mathrm{glob}}>0.

Proof

Apply Theorem
0..74
to each
k
, then take minimum.

Corollary 40
(Zero-budget persistence)

If
\lambda_{n}\downarrow 0
, minimizers
\mu^{\lambda_{n}}\to\mu^{0}
, then
\mu^{0}
satisfies
the same mode lower bounds with
\lambda=0
, proving Corollary
36
.

Proof

Constants depend continuously/monotonically on
\lambda
through
\mathfrak{B}
.
Pass to the limit using trajectory convergence from Section
F
.

Output used next.

G.4
strengthens set-mass coverage to density-floor estimates on modal neighborhoods and
derives perturbation-stable lower bounds under endpoint/drift noise.

G.4. Stability of density minima and robust mode floors

We strengthen set-mass coverage to local density-floor bounds on modal neighborhoods
and prove perturbation stability of these floors.

Standing notation.

Fix
k\in\{1,\dots,K\}
, mode set
A_{k}
, and an interior compact core

K_{k}\Subset A_{k},\qquad\mathrm{dist}(K_{k},\partial A_{k})\geq r_{k}>0.

Let
\mu_{t}=\rho_{t}dx
denote the optimal ECFM/SB trajectory.

Assumption 0..75
(Local parabolic regularity around modes)

On each cylinder
[0,T]\times U_{k}
(open
U_{k}\supset A_{k}
),
\rho
solves the controlled FP equation

\partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho b)=\varepsilon\Delta\rho,\qquad b=u^{\star}+w,

with

b\in L^{q}_{t}L^{p}_{x}([0,T]\times U_{k}),\quad\frac{2}{q}+\frac{d}{p}<1,\quad p,q>2,

and
\rho\in L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
,
\rho>0
a.e.

Lemma 29
(From mode mass to local
L^{1}
lower bound)

Assume mode coverage from
G.3
:

\mu_{t}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k},\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

Then for any
K_{k}\Subset A_{k}
, there exists
\underline{m}_{k}>0
such that

\int_{K_{k}}\rho_{t}(x)\,dx\geq\underline{m}_{k},\qquad\forall t\in[0,T].

Proof

Choose
K_{k}
so that
|A_{k}\setminus K_{k}|
is small. By absolute continuity and uniform integrability
(from finite entropy/Fisher control), mass cannot concentrate entirely in an arbitrarily thin boundary layer.
Hence a fixed fraction of
\mu_{t}(A_{k})
lies in
K_{k}
, uniformly in
t
:

\int_{K_{k}}\rho_{t}\geq\theta_{k}\,\mu_{t}(A_{k})\geq\theta_{k}\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}=:\underline{m}_{k}.

Theorem 0..76
(Interior density floor on modal cores)

Under Assumptions
0..67
,
0..75
, and Lemma
29
,
for each
K_{k}\Subset A_{k}
, there exists
\underline{\rho}_{k}>0
such that

\rho_{t}(x)\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}\quad\text{for a.e. }(t,x)\in[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k},\ \forall\tau\in(0,T/2).

If initial/terminal traces are compatible with the same lower bound class, the estimate extends to all
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof

By Assumption
0..75
, FP is uniformly parabolic on
U_{k}
.
Apply local weak Harnack inequality to nonnegative solutions on nested cylinders

Q^{-}\Subset Q^{+}\subset(0,T)\times U_{k}
:

\left(\frac{1}{|Q^{-}|}\int_{Q^{-}}\rho^{\alpha}\right)^{1/\alpha}\leq C_{H}\inf_{Q^{+}}\rho,

equivalently

\inf_{Q^{+}}\rho\geq C_{H}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{|Q^{-}|}\int_{Q^{-}}\rho^{\alpha}\right)^{1/\alpha}.

Using
\alpha\in(0,1)
, Jensen gives lower control by
L^{1}
mass on
Q^{-}
.
Lemma
29
provides uniform positive
L^{1}
-mass on spatial cores for each time slab.
Cover
[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}
by finitely many cylinders and iterate Harnack chain,
yielding uniform
\underline{\rho}_{k}>0
.
Endpoint extension follows from boundary compatibility and one-sided parabolic estimates.

Corollary 41
(Lower bound on density minima over modes)

Define modal minimum

\mathfrak{m}_{k}:=\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{(t,x)\in[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\rho_{t}(x).

Then
\mathfrak{m}_{k}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}>0
. Consequently, singular concentration
(
\rho\to 0
on whole modal core) is excluded.

Proof

Immediate from Theorem
0..76
.

Definition 63
(Perturbed instance)

Let
\theta=(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda)
, and perturb

\theta^{\prime}=(\mu_{0}^{\prime},\mu_{T}^{\prime},u^{\star\prime},\lambda^{\prime})

with size

\Delta_{\theta}:=W_{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{0}^{\prime})+W_{2}(\mu_{T},\mu_{T}^{\prime})+\|u^{\star}-u^{\star\prime}\|_{\mathcal{U}}+|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|.

Let
\rho,\rho^{\prime}
be corresponding optimal densities.

Theorem 0..77
(Stability of density floors under perturbations)

Assume hypotheses of
E.4
stability and local regularity hold uniformly for
\theta,\theta^{\prime}
.
Then for each core
K_{k}\Subset A_{k}
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\rho^{\prime}_{t}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}-C_{k}\,\Delta_{\theta}.

In particular, if
\Delta_{\theta}<\underline{\rho}_{k}/(2C_{k})
, then

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\rho^{\prime}_{t}\geq\frac{1}{2}\underline{\rho}_{k}>0.

Proof

From
E.4
, trajectory perturbations are Lipschitz in endpoints/drift/budget:

\sup_{t}W_{2}(\mu_{t},\mu_{t}^{\prime})\leq L\Delta_{\theta}.

This implies local
L^{1}
perturbation control on
U_{k}
via transport inequalities and moment bounds:

\sup_{t}\|\rho_{t}-\rho_{t}^{\prime}\|_{L^{1}(U_{k})}\leq C\Delta_{\theta}.

Hence local mass floor from Lemma
29
degrades by at most
C\Delta_{\theta}
:

\int_{K_{k}}\rho_{t}^{\prime}\geq\underline{m}_{k}-C\Delta_{\theta}.

Apply the same Harnack-chain argument as Theorem
0..76
with perturbed coefficients
(
u^{\star\prime},w^{\prime}
); constants vary continuously under uniform coefficient bounds,
yielding

\inf\rho^{\prime}\geq\Phi_{k}(\underline{m}_{k}-C\Delta_{\theta})\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}-C_{k}\Delta_{\theta}.

Corollary 42
(Robust mode occupancy)

Under small perturbations
\Delta_{\theta}
, each mode retains both:

1.

set-mass floor:
\mu_{t}^{\prime}(A_{k})\geq(\underline{c}_{k}-\tilde{C}_{k}\Delta_{\theta})\pi_{k}
,

2.

density floor on cores:
\rho_{t}^{\prime}|_{K_{k}}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}-C_{k}\Delta_{\theta}
.

Thus mode coverage is perturbation-robust.

Proof

Set-mass floor is
E.4
/
G.3
stability; density floor is Theorem
0..77
.

Remark 35
(Interpretation for generative vision)

For semantic modes
A_{k}
(class/attribute regions in representation space),
the model not only preserves nonzero total mode probability, but also avoids thinning densities
to near-zero on modal cores. This formalizes robust anti-collapse behavior under realistic training perturbations.

Transition to Section
H
.

Section
H
generalizes these estimates to global perturbation theory:
trajectory Lipschitz bounds, velocity-field noise response, and initialization sensitivity with explicit constants.

H. Stability Under Perturbations

H.1. Lipschitz stability of trajectories

We establish global Lipschitz stability of optimal ECFM/SB trajectories with respect to
perturbations in endpoints, reference drift, and entropy budget.

Parameterization of instances.

Let an instance be

\Theta:=(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda),

and denote by

(\mu_{t}^{\Theta},v_{t}^{\Theta})_{t\in[0,T]}

its unique optimal trajectory (Sections E–G assumptions in force).
For two instances
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}
, define

\Delta_{0}:=W_{2}(\mu_{0}^{(1)},\mu_{0}^{(2)}),\quad\Delta_{T}:=W_{2}(\mu_{T}^{(1)},\mu_{T}^{(2)}),\quad\Delta_{\lambda}:=|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|.

For drift discrepancy, fix an admissible norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{U}}
(e.g. localized
L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}
+ tail control):

\Delta_{u}:=\|u_{1}^{\star}-u_{2}^{\star}\|_{\mathcal{U}}.

Set total perturbation size

\Delta_{\Theta}:=\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{T}+\Delta_{u}+\Delta_{\lambda}.

Assumption 0..78
(Uniform regularity envelope)

For all instances in a neighborhood
\mathfrak{N}
of interest:

1.

finite uniform action/Fisher/moment bounds:

\sup_{\Theta\in\mathfrak{N}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}^{\Theta}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\Theta}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}^{\Theta})\,dt+\sup_{t}m_{2}(\mu_{t}^{\Theta})\right)\leq M;

2.

unique minimizers and strict-convexity regime (
E.4
);

3.

local FP coefficient bounds ensuring Harnack/regularity constants are uniform.

Definition 64
(Trajectory distance)

For two trajectories
\mu^{1},\mu^{2}
, define

\mathbf{d}_{\infty}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}):=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2}),

and energy-weighted distance

\mathbf{d}_{2}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}):=\left(\int_{0}^{T}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2}\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

Lemma 30
(Differential inequality for coupled trajectories)

Let
\pi_{t}
be an optimal coupling of
(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})
. Then for a.e.
t
,

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle x-y,\ v_{t}^{1}(x)-v_{t}^{2}(y)\rangle\,d\pi_{t}(x,y).

Hence

\frac{d}{dt}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq\|v_{t}^{1}-v_{t}^{2}\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})}.

Proof

This is the standard metric derivative estimate in
W_{2}
-space for AC curves
satisfying continuity equations, obtained by Benamou–Brenier dynamic plan differentiation.

Lemma 31
(Velocity discrepancy decomposition)

With Schrödinger-potential representation (Section
E
):

v_{t}^{\Theta}=u_{t}^{\star}+\varepsilon\nabla\log\frac{\beta_{t}^{\Theta}}{\alpha_{t}^{\Theta}}+\mathcal{R}_{t}^{\Theta},

where
\mathcal{R}_{t}^{\Theta}
captures active entropy-multiplier correction (zero in inactive phases).
Then

\|v_{t}^{1}-v_{t}^{2}\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})}\leq\underbrace{\|u_{1}^{\star}-u_{2}^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})}}_{\mathrm{(I)}}+\underbrace{\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\log\frac{\beta_{t}^{1}}{\alpha_{t}^{1}}-\nabla\log\frac{\beta_{t}^{2}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})}}_{\mathrm{(II)}}+\underbrace{\|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{1}-\mathcal{R}_{t}^{2}\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})}}_{\mathrm{(III)}}.

Proof

Subtract the two velocity formulas and apply triangle inequality.

Lemma 32
(Control of terms (II) and (III))

Under Assumption
0..78
, there exist constants
C_{\Phi},C_{\eta}>0
such that
for a.e.
t
,

\mathrm{(II)}\leq C_{\Phi}\,W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})+C_{\Phi}(\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{T}+\Delta_{u}),

\mathrm{(III)}\leq C_{\eta}\,W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})+C_{\eta}\,\Delta_{\lambda}.

Proof

Term (II): Schrödinger potentials depend Lipschitz-continuously on endpoint marginals
and reference drift under uniform kernel/regularity bounds (Sinkhorn map stability in dynamic form).
Spatial gradient stability yields the stated affine bound.

Term (III): entropy-multiplier correction enters via KKT multiplier
\eta^{\Theta}
.
Dual sensitivity in
\lambda
and state variables gives

\|\eta^{1}-\eta^{2}\|_{L^{1}(0,T)}\lesssim\Delta_{\lambda}+\mathbf{d}_{\infty}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2}),

which transfers to
\mathcal{R}
-bound through
\nabla\log\rho
uniform integrability/Fisher control.

Theorem 0..79
(Global Lipschitz stability in
\mathbf{d}_{\infty}
)

Under Assumption
0..78
, there exists
L_{\infty}>0
depending only on the uniform envelope
(and
T,\varepsilon
) such that

\mathbf{d}_{\infty}(\mu^{\Theta_{1}},\mu^{\Theta_{2}})\leq L_{\infty}\,\Delta_{\Theta}.

More explicitly,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq e^{Ct}\Big(\Delta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\Delta_{u}+\Delta_{\lambda}+\Delta_{T})\,ds\Big)\leq L_{\infty}(\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{T}+\Delta_{u}+\Delta_{\lambda}).

Proof

From Lemma
30
and decomposition:

\frac{d}{dt}W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq\Delta_{u}+C_{\Phi}W_{2}+C_{\Phi}(\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{T}+\Delta_{u})+C_{\eta}W_{2}+C_{\eta}\Delta_{\lambda}.

Hence

\frac{d}{dt}D(t)\leq CD(t)+B,\quad D(t):=W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2}),

with

C:=C_{\Phi}+C_{\eta},\qquad B:=\Delta_{u}+C_{\Phi}(\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{T}+\Delta_{u})+C_{\eta}\Delta_{\lambda}.

By Grönwall:

D(t)\leq e^{Ct}D(0)+\frac{e^{Ct}-1}{C}B.

Since
D(0)=\Delta_{0}
, taking supremum over
t\in[0,T]
yields the bound.
Endpoint discrepancy
\Delta_{T}
enters via potential-stability constants (two-sided bridge condition).

Corollary 43
(Integrated trajectory stability)

There exists
L_{2}>0
such that

\mathbf{d}_{2}(\mu^{\Theta_{1}},\mu^{\Theta_{2}})\leq L_{2}\,\Delta_{\Theta}.

Proof

From Theorem
0..79
,

W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})\leq L_{\infty}\Delta_{\Theta}\ \forall t.

Therefore

\mathbf{d}_{2}\leq\sqrt{T}\,L_{\infty}\Delta_{\Theta}=:L_{2}\Delta_{\Theta}.

Proposition 48
(Stability of modal masses)

For each mode set
A_{k}
with Lipschitz boundary, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\mu_{t}^{\Theta_{1}}(A_{k})-\mu_{t}^{\Theta_{2}}(A_{k})\right|\leq C_{k}\,\mathbf{d}_{\infty}(\mu^{\Theta_{1}},\mu^{\Theta_{2}})\leq C_{k}L_{\infty}\Delta_{\Theta}.

Proof

Approximate
\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}
by bounded Lipschitz functions
\phi_{k,\eta}
with

\|\phi_{k,\eta}\|_{\mathrm{Lip}}\lesssim\eta^{-1}
, then optimize
\eta
using boundary regularity
to transfer
W_{2}
-control into set-mass control (via Kantorovich–Rubinstein-type smoothing argument).
Uniform in
t
, then apply Theorem
0..79
.

Remark 36
(Interpretation)

Optimal generation trajectories are Lipschitz-stable with respect to endpoint distribution shift,
drift-model mismatch, and entropy-budget tuning. Thus small training/data perturbations cannot
induce large transport-path deviations.

Output used next.

H.2
will establish stability w.r.t.
velocity-field noise
(stochastic/modeling perturbations),
including explicit action-gap and trajectory-error bounds.

H.2. Stability with respect to noise in the velocity field

We quantify how additive/modeling noise in the velocity field affects:
(i) trajectory error, (ii) action suboptimality, and (iii) mode-coverage margins.

Noisy dynamics model.

Let
(\mu_{t}^{\star},v_{t}^{\star})
be the optimal ECFM trajectory for fixed instance
\Theta
.
Consider a perturbed field

\tilde{v}_{t}(x)=v_{t}^{\star}(x)+\xi_{t}(x),

where
\xi
is a measurable perturbation (deterministic or random) with finite energy

\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}^{2}:=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\xi_{t}(x)|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}(x)\,dt<\infty.

Let
\tilde{\mu}_{t}
solve

\partial_{t}\tilde{\mu}_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\tilde{\mu}_{t}\tilde{v}_{t})=0,\qquad\tilde{\mu}_{0}=\mu_{0}.

Assumption 0..80
(One-sided Lipschitz regularity of optimal field)

There exists
L_{\mathrm{OSL}}\in L^{1}(0,T)
such that

\langle v_{t}^{\star}(x)-v_{t}^{\star}(y),x-y\rangle\leq L_{\mathrm{OSL}}(t)|x-y|^{2}\quad\text{for a.e. }t,\ \forall x,y.

Assumption 0..81
(Transportability of noise norm)

There exists
C_{\mathrm{tr}}\geq 1
such that

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\xi_{t}|^{2}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{t}dt\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\xi_{t}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}dt=C_{\mathrm{tr}}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}^{2}.

(Obtained, e.g., from density-ratio bounds along stable flows.)

Lemma 33
(Coupled error dynamics)

Let
\pi_{t}
be a coupling between
\mu_{t}^{\star}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{t}
induced by synchronized characteristics.
Then for
D(t):=W_{2}(\mu_{t}^{\star},\tilde{\mu}_{t})
,

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}D(t)^{2}\leq L_{\mathrm{OSL}}(t)D(t)^{2}+D(t)\,\|\xi_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t})}.

Equivalently (a.e. where
D(t)>0
),

\dot{D}(t)\leq L_{\mathrm{OSL}}(t)D(t)+\|\xi_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t})}.

Proof

Differentiate squared displacement under coupled flow:

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}|X_{t}-Y_{t}|^{2}=\langle X_{t}-Y_{t},\ v_{t}^{\star}(X_{t})-v_{t}^{\star}(Y_{t})-\xi_{t}(Y_{t})\rangle.

Use OSL bound on first difference term and Cauchy–Schwarz on noise term.
Integrate w.r.t. coupling law to get inequality for
D^{2}
, then divide by
D
if
D>0
.

Theorem 0..82
(Trajectory error bound under velocity noise)

Under Assumptions
0..80
–
0..81
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq\exp\!\Big(\!\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{OSL}}(s)\,ds\Big)\sqrt{T\,C_{\mathrm{tr}}}\,\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}.

More precisely,

W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq\int_{0}^{t}\exp\!\Big(\int_{s}^{t}L_{\mathrm{OSL}}(r)\,dr\Big)\,\|\xi_{s}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{s})}\,ds.

Proof

Apply Grönwall to Lemma
33
with
D(0)=0
:

D(t)\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}L_{\mathrm{OSL}}}\|\xi_{s}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{s})}ds.

Take supremum in
t
, bound exponential by
e^{\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{OSL}}}
, then Cauchy–Schwarz:

\sup_{t}D(t)\leq e^{\int_{0}^{T}L_{\mathrm{OSL}}}\sqrt{T}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\xi_{s}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{s})}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}.

Use Assumption
0..81
.

Proposition 49
(Action gap under additive noise)

Define kinetic objective

\mathcal{J}(v;\nu):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|v_{t}-u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d\nu_{t}dt.

Then

\mathcal{J}(\tilde{v};\tilde{\mu})-\mathcal{J}(v^{\star};\mu^{\star})\leq\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\langle v_{t}^{\star}-u_{t}^{\star},\xi_{t}\rangle\,d\tilde{\mu}_{t}dt}_{\mathrm{cross}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\xi_{t}|^{2}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{t}dt+\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{meas}},

where
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{meas}}
is measure-mismatch error:

\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{meas}}:=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int|v_{t}^{\star}-u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d(\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star})\right)dt.

Consequently,

\mathcal{J}(\tilde{v};\tilde{\mu})-\mathcal{J}(v^{\star};\mu^{\star})\leq C_{1}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}+C_{2}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}^{2},

with constants depending on uniform envelope and Theorem
0..82
.

Proof

Expand square:

|\tilde{v}-u^{\star}|^{2}=|v^{\star}-u^{\star}|^{2}+2\langle v^{\star}-u^{\star},\xi\rangle+|\xi|^{2}.

Integrate against
\tilde{\mu}
, subtract optimal reference term against
\mu^{\star}
, yielding formula.
Bound cross term by Cauchy–Schwarz + transportability:

|\mathrm{cross}|\leq\|v^{\star}-u^{\star}\|_{L^{2}(dt\,d\tilde{\mu})}\left(\int|\xi|^{2}\,d\tilde{\mu}dt\right)^{1/2}\leq C\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}.

Bound
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{meas}}
by Lipschitz/regularity of integrand and

W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})
, then apply Theorem
0..82
.

Theorem 0..83
(Feasibility robustness of entropy-rate constraint)

Assume additionally:

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\nabla\log\tilde{\rho}_{t}|^{2}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{t}dt\leq M_{I},

and define entropy-rate perturbation

\delta_{H}(t):=\left|\int\nabla\log\tilde{\rho}_{t}\cdot\tilde{v}_{t}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\int\nabla\log\rho_{t}^{\star}\cdot v_{t}^{\star}\,d\mu_{t}^{\star}\right|.

Then

\int_{0}^{T}\delta_{H}(t)\,dt\leq C_{H}\big(\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}+\mathbf{d}_{2}(\tilde{\mu},\mu^{\star})\big)\leq\tilde{C}_{H}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}.

Hence if the nominal trajectory has margin

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\star})\geq-\lambda+\gamma\quad\text{a.e.}

and
\tilde{C}_{H}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}<\gamma T
, then noisy trajectory remains feasible:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{\mu}_{t})\geq-\lambda\quad\text{a.e. (in weak integrated sense)}.

Proof

Use decomposition of
\delta_{H}
into velocity perturbation part and measure/density perturbation part.
Velocity part bounded by Cauchy–Schwarz with Fisher bound
M_{I}
.
Measure/density part bounded via stability of
\nabla\log\rho
in weighted
L^{2}
and trajectory distance.
Invoke Theorem
0..82
to express all terms in
\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}
.
Margin argument gives feasibility.

Corollary 44
(Mode-coverage robustness to field noise)

Under assumptions of
G.3
and Theorem
0..82
,
modal masses satisfy

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})-\mu_{t}^{\star}(A_{k})|\leq C_{k}\,\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}.

Therefore if nominal floor is
\mu_{t}^{\star}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}
, then

\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}-C_{k}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}},

and remains strictly positive for sufficiently small noise.

Proof

Combine Theorem
0..82
with set-mass stability transfer (as in
H.1
).

Remark 37
(Random noise specialization)

If
\xi
is random with
\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}
, then

\mathbb{E}\sup_{t}W_{2}^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq C\sigma^{2},

and expected action gap is
O(\sigma+\sigma^{2})
.
High-probability versions follow from concentration for
\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}
.

Output used next.

H.3
treats stability with respect to initialization perturbations
(
\mu_{0}
-mis-specification), deriving forward error propagation and endpoint mismatch bounds.

H.3. Stability with respect to initialization perturbations

We quantify sensitivity of optimal trajectories to perturbations of the initial distribution.
Terminal marginals, drift, and entropy budget are fixed unless explicitly noted.

Instances.

Let

\Theta=(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda),\qquad\tilde{\Theta}=(\tilde{\mu}_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda),

with unique optimal trajectories

(\mu_{t},v_{t}),\qquad(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\tilde{v}_{t}).

Define initialization mismatch

\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}:=W_{2}(\mu_{0},\tilde{\mu}_{0}).

Assumption 0..84
(Uniform envelope and contraction constants)

Assume Section
E
–
H
regularity holds uniformly on the two instances, and there exist:

1.

L\in L^{1}(0,T)
controlling one-sided Lipschitz growth of optimal velocity fields;

2.

\kappa\geq 0
controlling endpoint-conditioning amplification from Schrödinger system;

3.

finite uniform moment/Fisher/action bounds.

Lemma 34
(Forward perturbation inequality)

Let
D(t):=W_{2}(\mu_{t},\tilde{\mu}_{t})
. Then for a.e.
t
,

\dot{D}(t)\leq L(t)D(t)+\kappa\,\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Hence

D(t)\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}L(s)\,ds}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}L(r)\,dr}\,ds\ \Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Proof

Use
H.1
differential inequality:

\dot{D}(t)\leq\|v_{t}-\tilde{v}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\pi_{t})}.

Decompose
\|v_{t}-\tilde{v}_{t}\|
into state-dependent part (
\leq L(t)D(t)
)
and endpoint-conditioning part induced by changed initial Schrödinger factor (
\leq\kappa\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}
).
Apply Grönwall.

Theorem 0..85
(Global initialization Lipschitz stability)

Under Assumption
0..84
, there exists
C_{\mathrm{init}}>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\mu_{t},\tilde{\mu}_{t})\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}},

with explicit admissible choice

C_{\mathrm{init}}=\exp\!\Big(\int_{0}^{T}L(s)\,ds\Big)\Big(1+\kappa T\Big).

Proof

Take supremum in Lemma
34
and bound the convolution term by

T\exp(\int_{0}^{T}L)
.

Corollary 45
(Integrated trajectory bound)

\left(\int_{0}^{T}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t},\tilde{\mu}_{t})\,dt\right)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{T}\,C_{\mathrm{init}}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Proof

Immediate from Theorem
0..85
.

Proposition 50
(Initialization perturbation of endpoint fit)

Let

\mathcal{E}_{T}(\tilde{\Theta}):=W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{T},\mu_{T})

be endpoint mismatch produced by using perturbed-initial optimal policy without re-optimization.
Then

\mathcal{E}_{T}(\tilde{\Theta})\leq C_{T}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}},

where
C_{T}\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}
under the same envelope.

Proof

Evaluate Theorem
0..85
at
t=T
:

W_{2}(\mu_{T},\tilde{\mu}_{T})\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Set
C_{T}:=C_{\mathrm{init}}
.

Proposition 51
(Action sensitivity to initialization)

Let
\mathsf{V}(\mu_{0})
denote optimal value with fixed
(\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda)
.
Then locally

|\mathsf{V}(\mu_{0})-\mathsf{V}(\tilde{\mu}_{0})|\leq C_{V}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Moreover, for optimizers
(\mu,v)
,
(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{v})
,

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{1}{2}|v-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\mu_{t}dt-\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\frac{1}{2}|\tilde{v}-u^{\star}|^{2}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{t}dt\right|\leq C_{V}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Proof

Use value-sensitivity template from
E.4
with only endpoint perturbation active.
Construct transported competitor from
(\mu,v)
to perturbed initial condition via short connector path;
extra cost scales linearly in
\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}
.
Repeat symmetrically (swap roles) to get absolute value bound.

Theorem 0..86
(Stability of mode-coverage floors under initialization noise)

For each mode
A_{k}
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mu_{t}(A_{k})-\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})|\leq C_{k}^{\mathrm{init}}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

If nominal trajectory satisfies
\mu_{t}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}
, then

\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}-C_{k}^{\mathrm{init}}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Hence mode occupancy remains strictly positive whenever

\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}<\frac{\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}}{2C_{k}^{\mathrm{init}}}.

Proof

Combine Theorem
0..85
with set-functional stability (
H.1
Proposition on modal masses).

Corollary 46
(Density-floor robustness on modal cores)

Under
G.4
assumptions, modal core density floors satisfy

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\tilde{\rho}_{t}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}-\widetilde{C}_{k}\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}.

Proof

Apply
G.4
perturbation theorem with

\Delta_{\theta}=\Delta_{\mathrm{init}}
, since other parameters are fixed.

Remark 38
(Interpretation)

Initialization misspecification produces linearly controlled deformation of the full transport trajectory,
objective value, and mode coverage margins. Therefore ECFM interpolation is well-conditioned to
small dataset/source perturbations at
t=0
.

Output used next.

H.4
combines
H.1
–
H.3
into a unified perturbation theorem (joint perturbations in

\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda,\xi
) with a single end-to-end bound and constants bookkeeping.

H.4. Unified perturbation theorem and consolidated bounds

We combine
H.1
–
H.3
into a single end-to-end stability statement under simultaneous
perturbations of endpoints, reference drift, entropy budget, and velocity-field noise.

Base and perturbed systems.

Let base instance

\Theta=(\mu_{0},\mu_{T},u^{\star},\lambda),

with optimal trajectory
(\mu_{t}^{\star},v_{t}^{\star})
.
Let perturbed instance

\Theta^{\prime}=(\mu_{0}^{\prime},\mu_{T}^{\prime},u^{\star\prime},\lambda^{\prime})

with optimal trajectory
(\bar{\mu}_{t},\bar{v}_{t})
, and let an additional additive field noise

\xi
act on the perturbed trajectory:

\tilde{v}_{t}=\bar{v}_{t}+\xi_{t},\qquad\partial_{t}\tilde{\mu}_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\tilde{\mu}_{t}\tilde{v}_{t})=0,\quad\tilde{\mu}_{0}=\mu_{0}^{\prime}.

Define deterministic parameter perturbation

\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}:=W_{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{0}^{\prime})+W_{2}(\mu_{T},\mu_{T}^{\prime})+\|u^{\star}-u^{\star\prime}\|_{\mathcal{U}}+|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}|,

and noise magnitude

\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}:=\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{N}}=\left(\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|\xi_{t}|^{2}\,d\bar{\mu}_{t}dt\right)^{1/2}.

Total perturbation size:

\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}:=\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}.

Assumption 0..87
(Unified envelope)

All instances in a neighborhood of
\Theta
satisfy:

1.

uniform regularity/coercivity and uniqueness assumptions from
E.3
–
E.4
;

2.

trajectory Lipschitz constants from
H.1
bounded by
L_{\mathrm{par}}
;

3.

velocity-noise response constants from
H.2
bounded by
L_{\mathrm{noi}}
;

4.

local parabolic/Harnack constants from
G.4
uniformly bounded.

Lemma 35
(Two-step decomposition)

For any
t\in[0,T]
,

W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\bar{\mu}_{t})+W_{2}(\bar{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star}).

Consequently,

\sup_{t}W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq\sup_{t}W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\bar{\mu}_{t})+\sup_{t}W_{2}(\bar{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star}).

Proof

Triangle inequality in
(\mathcal{P}_{2},W_{2})
, pointwise in
t
, then take supremum.

Theorem 0..88
(Unified trajectory stability)

Under Assumption
0..87
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq L_{\mathrm{par}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}+L_{\mathrm{noi}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}\leq L_{\mathrm{tot}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}},

where
L_{\mathrm{tot}}:=\max\{L_{\mathrm{par}},L_{\mathrm{noi}}\}
(or any larger admissible constant).

Proof

By
H.1
/
H.3
(deterministic parameter perturbations),

\sup_{t}W_{2}(\bar{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq L_{\mathrm{par}}\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}.

By
H.2
(noise about perturbed optimum),

\sup_{t}W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\bar{\mu}_{t})\leq L_{\mathrm{noi}}\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}.

Apply Lemma
35
.

Corollary 47
(Integrated trajectory bound)

\left(\int_{0}^{T}W_{2}^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\,dt\right)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{T}\,\big(L_{\mathrm{par}}\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}+L_{\mathrm{noi}}\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}\big).

Proof

Bound the integrand by the supremum from Theorem
0..88
.

Proposition 52
(Unified action-gap estimate)

Let

\mathcal{J}(\nu,w):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int|w_{t}-u_{t}^{\star}|^{2}\,d\nu_{t}dt.

Then

\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{v})-\mathcal{J}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\leq C_{1}\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}+C_{2}\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}+C_{3}\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}^{2}+C_{4}\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}.

In particular, for
\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}\leq 1
,

\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{v})-\mathcal{J}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\leq C_{\mathcal{J}}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}.

Proof

Write

\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{v})-\mathcal{J}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})=\underbrace{\big[\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{v})-\mathcal{J}(\bar{\mu},\bar{v})\big]}_{\text{noise contribution}}+\underbrace{\big[\mathcal{J}(\bar{\mu},\bar{v})-\mathcal{J}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\big]}_{\text{parameter contribution}}.

Second bracket bounded by
H.3
/
E.4
value sensitivity:
O(\Delta_{\mathrm{par}})
.
First bracket bounded by
H.2
action-gap:
O(\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}}^{2})
,
with additional mixed term through measure mismatch controlled by
Theorem
0..88
, giving
O(\Delta_{\mathrm{par}}\Delta_{\mathrm{noi}})
.

Theorem 0..89
(Unified modal-mass robustness)

For each mode
A_{k}
(
G.1
), there exists
C_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})-\mu_{t}^{\star}(A_{k})\right|\leq C_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}.

Hence, if nominal floor is
\mu_{t}^{\star}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}
, then

\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})\geq\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}-C_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}.

Therefore strict positivity is preserved whenever

\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}<\frac{\underline{c}_{k}\pi_{k}}{2C_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

Proof

From Theorem
0..88
,
W_{2}
-distance is
O(\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}})
.
Apply the modal set-functional stability transfer from
H.1
/
G.3
(Lipschitz boundary smoothing argument).

Theorem 0..90
(Unified density-floor robustness on modal cores)

Let
K_{k}\Subset A_{k}
be modal cores from
G.4
with nominal floor

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\rho_{t}^{\star}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}>0.

Then there exists
\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}>0
such that

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\tilde{\rho}_{t}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}-\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}.

In particular, for

\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}<\frac{\underline{\rho}_{k}}{2\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}}},

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\tilde{\rho}_{t}\geq\frac{1}{2}\underline{\rho}_{k}>0.

Proof

Apply
G.4
floor-stability theorem twice:
(1) base
\mu^{\star}
vs deterministic perturbed optimum
\bar{\mu}
(parameter perturbation),
(2)
\bar{\mu}
vs noisy trajectory
\tilde{\mu}
(noise perturbation, using
H.2
trajectory bound and local FP stability).
Combine by triangle inequality on local
L^{1}
/coefficient perturbations and propagate through Harnack constants.

Corollary 48
(End-to-end robustness summary)

There exist finite constants
C_{W},C_{J},C_{M},C_{\rho}
(instance-local, envelope-uniform) such that

\sup_{t}W_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t}^{\star})\leq C_{W}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}},

\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{v})-\mathcal{J}(\mu^{\star},v^{\star})\leq C_{J}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}},

\sup_{t}|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(A_{k})-\mu_{t}^{\star}(A_{k})|\leq C_{M}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}},

\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{[\tau,T-\tau]\times K_{k}}\tilde{\rho}_{t}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}-C_{\rho}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}.

Thus ECFM is jointly stable in trajectory, objective, and anti-collapse guarantees.

Remark 39
(Constants bookkeeping)

A valid construction is:

C_{W}=L_{\mathrm{tot}},\quad C_{J}=C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{4},\quad C_{M}=C_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}},\quad C_{\rho}=\widetilde{C}_{k}^{\mathrm{tot}},

where each constant is explicit once coercivity, OSL bounds, Fisher envelope, and Harnack constants are fixed.

Transition to Section
I
.

Section
I
provides a constructive failure case without entropy constraint
(
\lambda=\infty
/ unconstrained FM), showing explicit singular concentration and modal collapse,
thereby proving necessity of entropy control for guaranteed mode coverage.

I. Failure Case Without Entropy Constraint

I.1. Counterexample construction for unconstrained flow matching

We construct an explicit class of unconstrained FM trajectories (
\dot{\mathcal{H}}
unrestricted)
that attains small/competitive velocity-matching risk while exhibiting mode collapse and singular concentration.

Goal of the construction.

Show that when entropy-rate control is removed, there exist admissible deterministic flows
that:

1.

match endpoint marginals (or approximate them arbitrarily well),

2.

keep FM regression objective finite (even small),

3.

collapse intermediate-time mass onto low-dimensional/single-mode regions.

This proves anti-collapse guarantees in Sections
G
–
H
are not inherited by classical unconstrained FM.

Definition 65
(Unconstrained FM functional)

Given supervision field
v^{\dagger}(x,t)
, define

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}(v):=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|v(x,t)-v^{\dagger}(x,t)\|^{2}\,\mu_{t}(dx)\,dt,

subject only to continuity equation

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}v_{t})=0,\quad\mu_{0},\mu_{T}\ \text{prescribed}.

No entropy-rate inequality is imposed.

Assumption 0..91
(Two-mode endpoint pair)

Work in
d=1
(extends to
d>1
by product construction).
Fix
\sigma>0
,
a\gg\sigma
, and set

\mu_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}(-a,\sigma^{2})+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}(a,\sigma^{2}),\qquad\mu_{T}=\mu_{0}.

Define modal sets

A_{-}:=(-\infty,0),\qquad A_{+}:=(0,\infty),

so
\mu_{T}(A_{\pm})=\frac{1}{2}
.

Definition 66
(Collapse-then-redisperse transport map family)

Fix small parameters
\varepsilon,\delta,\tau\in(0,1)
, with
0<\tau<T/2
.
Define piecewise-smooth maps
\Phi_{t}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
:

\Phi_{t}(x)=\begin{cases}(1-\frac{t}{\tau})x,&t\in[0,\tau],\\[2.58334pt]
\delta\,\mathrm{sgn}(x)+\varepsilon x,&t\in[\tau,T-\tau],\\[2.58334pt]
\text{smooth expansion returning to identity at }t=T,&t\in[T-\tau,T].\end{cases}

Let
\mu_{t}:=(\Phi_{t})_{\#}\mu_{0}
, and define Eulerian velocity

v_{t}(y):=\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1}(y)).

Lemma 36
(Well-posed CE trajectory)

(\mu_{t},v_{t})
from Definition
66
satisfies

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}+\partial_{x}(\mu_{t}v_{t})=0

in distributional sense, with
\mu_{|0}=\mu_{0}
,
\mu_{|T}=\mu_{T}
.

Proof

For smooth phases, pushforward by
\Phi_{t}\in C^{1}
yields classical transport solution.
At phase junctions, choose
C^{1}
time mollification of
\Phi_{t}
in windows of width
o(1)
,
preserving endpoints; then CE holds distributionally in the limit.

Proposition 53
(Intermediate-time collapse)

For
t\in[\tau,T-\tau]
,
\mu_{t}
concentrates near origin:

\mu_{t}\big((-\delta-\eta,\delta+\eta)\big)\geq 1-c_{1}e^{-c_{2}/\varepsilon^{2}}

for fixed
\eta>0
. In particular, modal masses satisfy

\mu_{t}(A_{+})\approx\mu_{t}(A_{-})\approx\tfrac{1}{2}

but
semantic two-mode separation is destroyed
: both modes overlap in an
O(\delta)
-tube.
As
\delta,\varepsilon\to 0
,
\mu_{t}\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}
on the plateau.

Proof

On plateau,
\Phi_{t}(x)=\delta\,\mathrm{sgn}(x)+\varepsilon x
.
Each Gaussian component maps to variance
\varepsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}
, means
\pm\delta
, hence both lie in
O(\delta)
.
Tail bound for Gaussian gives displayed mass estimate. Weak convergence to
\delta_{0}
follows as

\delta,\varepsilon\to 0
.

Definition 67
(Reference/teacher field)

Define a smooth teacher
v^{\dagger}
consistent with a non-collapsing interpolation
(e.g., displacement interpolation between
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
, here near-stationary).

Lemma 37
(Small FM risk despite collapse)

For every
\eta_{\mathrm{risk}}>0
, there exist parameters

\delta,\varepsilon,\tau
and a smooth approximation of
v
such that

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}(v)\leq\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}(v^{\dagger})+\eta_{\mathrm{risk}},

while Proposition
53
holds.

Proof

Choose
v^{\dagger}
bounded and small on most of space-time (near-stationary endpoints).
Construct
v
equal to
v^{\dagger}
except on short temporal windows
[0,\tau]\cup[T-\tau,T]
,
where contraction/expansion occurs. FM risk increment scales like

\int_{0}^{\tau}\!\!\int|v-v^{\dagger}|^{2}d\mu_{t}dt+\int_{T-\tau}^{T}\!\!\int|v-v^{\dagger}|^{2}d\mu_{t}dt\lesssim C\tau.

Take
\tau
sufficiently small, then smooth time junctions with arbitrarily small additional cost.
Thus excess risk
<\eta_{\mathrm{risk}}
, while plateau collapse persists on
[\tau,T-\tau]
.

Theorem 0..92
(Failure without entropy constraint)

Under Assumption
0..91
, in unconstrained FM
(Definition
65
), there exist admissible trajectories

(\mu_{t},v_{t})
such that:

1.

\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
are matched exactly;

2.

FM objective is arbitrarily close to a non-collapsing reference optimum;

3.

intermediate measures collapse to near-singular unimodal concentration.

Therefore unconstrained FM does not guarantee mode preservation/coverage.

Proof

Combine Lemma
36
, Proposition
53
,
and Lemma
37
.

Corollary 49
(Entropy-rate violation along collapsing paths)

For the collapsing family above, during contraction phase:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\ll-1

for sufficiently small
\tau,\varepsilon
. In particular, for any finite
\lambda
,

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda

is violated on a set of positive measure when collapse is strong enough.

Proof

Under scaling
x\mapsto\alpha x
, differential entropy shifts by
\log\alpha
.
Contraction from scale
1
to
O(\varepsilon)
over time
\tau
yields entropy drop
\sim\log\varepsilon
,
hence rate
\sim\frac{1}{\tau}\log\varepsilon\to-\infty
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0,\tau\downarrow 0
.

Remark 40
(Why endpoint matching is insufficient)

Even exact endpoint agreement does not control intermediate geometry.
Without entropy-rate regularization, trajectories may pass through singular bottlenecks
that erase modal structure and then re-expand to match the target.

Proposition 54
(Higher-dimensional extension)

In
d>1
, identical failure holds by applying 1D collapsing map on one coordinate and identity on others:

\Phi_{t}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=(\phi_{t}(x_{1}),x_{2},\dots,x_{d}).

Then intermediate concentration occurs on a codimension-
(d-1)
tube, with the same FM-risk argument.

Proof

Product structure preserves CE and endpoint matching; collapse proof reduces to 1D first coordinate.

Output used next.

I.2
will sharpen this into a
singular-limit theorem
:
construct a sequence of unconstrained FM solutions converging to measures with
atomic/intermittently singular intermediate limits, quantifying collapse severity.

I.2. Singular-limit theorem for unconstrained FM trajectories

We strengthen
I.1
by constructing a sequence of unconstrained FM trajectories
whose intermediate-time measures converge to singular (atomic) limits while
endpoint marginals and FM risk remain controlled.

Construction sequence.

Use the family from Definition
66
, indexed by
n
:

\varepsilon_{n}\downarrow 0,\qquad\delta_{n}\downarrow 0,\qquad\tau_{n}\downarrow 0,

with
\tau_{n}<T/2
. Let

\Phi_{t}^{(n)},\quad\mu_{t}^{(n)}:=(\Phi_{t}^{(n)})_{\#}\mu_{0},\quad v_{t}^{(n)}:=\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{(n)}\circ(\Phi_{t}^{(n)})^{-1}.

Choose time smoothing so
t\mapsto\Phi_{t}^{(n)}\in C^{1}
, uniformly Lipschitz away from
small junction windows.

Assumption 0..93
(Rate coupling)

Assume

\frac{|\log\varepsilon_{n}|}{\tau_{n}}\to\infty,\qquad\frac{\delta_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}}\to 0,\qquad\tau_{n}\to 0.

The first condition enforces diverging entropy-drop rate during contraction.

Lemma 38
(Endpoint exactness and CE admissibility)

For every
n
,

\mu_{0}^{(n)}=\mu_{0},\qquad\mu_{T}^{(n)}=\mu_{T},

and
(\mu_{t}^{(n)},v_{t}^{(n)})
solves

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}^{(n)}+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu_{t}^{(n)}v_{t}^{(n)})=0

in distributions.

Proof

By design,
\Phi_{0}^{(n)}=\mathrm{Id}
,
\Phi_{T}^{(n)}=\mathrm{Id}
, and
pushforward-flow identity gives CE (as in Lemma
36
).

Lemma 39
(Plateau-time weak singular convergence)

Fix any compact interval

I\Subset(0,T).

For all sufficiently large
n
,
I\subset[\tau_{n},T-\tau_{n}]
, and for each
t\in I
,

\mu_{t}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

(
d=1
; in
d>1
, convergence to
\delta_{0}\otimes\bar{\mu}_{\perp}
under product extension).

Proof

On plateau, map is
x\mapsto\delta_{n}\operatorname{sgn}(x)+\varepsilon_{n}x
.
Thus image variance is
O(\varepsilon_{n}^{2})
, means
\pm\delta_{n}\to 0
, so both components collapse to
0
.
Hence weak convergence to
\delta_{0}
.

Proposition 55
(Distributional-in-time singular limit)

Define measure-valued curves
\mu^{(n)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2})
.
Then up to subsequence,

\mu^{(n)}\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup}\mu^{\infty}\quad\text{in }L^{\infty}\!\big(0,T;\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\big),

with

\mu_{t}^{\infty}=\begin{cases}\delta_{0},&t\in(0,T)\ \text{a.e.},\\
\mu_{0},&t=0,\\
\mu_{T},&t=T,\end{cases}

in the sense that endpoint traces remain
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
, while interior times are singular.

Proof

From Lemma
39
, for any test
\varphi(t,x)
compactly supported in

(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int\varphi(t,x)\,d\mu_{t}^{(n)}dt\to\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t,0)\,dt.

This identifies interior weak-* limit as
\delta_{0}
.
Endpoint traces are fixed by Lemma
38
.

Theorem 0..94
(Singular-limit failure without entropy control)

For unconstrained FM, there exists a sequence
(\mu^{(n)},v^{(n)})
such that:

1.

exact endpoint matching:
\mu_{0}^{(n)}=\mu_{0},\ \mu_{T}^{(n)}=\mu_{T}
;

2.

interior-time singular limit:
\mu_{t}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
;

3.

FM risk remains asymptotically near reference:

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}(v^{(n)})\leq\inf\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}+o(1)

for suitable teacher/reference
v^{\dagger}
from
I.1
.

Hence unconstrained FM admits asymptotically near-optimal yet singular/collapsing trajectories.

Proof

(1)–(2): Lemma
38
, Proposition
55
.
(3): same short-window argument as Lemma
37
; choose
\tau_{n}\to 0
, smooth junctions with
o(1)
cost.

Corollary 50
(Unbounded negative entropy-rate spikes)

Along the sequence in Theorem
0..94
,

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{(n)})\to-\infty.

More quantitatively, during contraction windows:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{(n)})\lesssim-\frac{|\log\varepsilon_{n}|}{\tau_{n}},

which diverges to
-\infty
by Assumption
0..93
.

Proof

Entropy under scaling by factor
\alpha
changes by
\log\alpha
.
Contraction by
\varepsilon_{n}
over
\tau_{n}
gives rate
\sim\log(\varepsilon_{n})/\tau_{n}
.
Assumption
0..93
yields divergence.

Proposition 56
(Violation of any finite entropy budget)

For every finite
\lambda>0
, there exists
N
such that for all
n\geq N
,

\left|\{t:\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{(n)})<-\lambda\}\right|>0.

Therefore sequence is eventually infeasible for ECFM constraints.

Proof

By Corollary
50
, negative spikes exceed
-\lambda
for large
n
.
Since spikes occur on contraction windows of positive length
\sim\tau_{n}
, the violating set has positive measure.

Remark 41
(Contrast with Sections G–H)

Sections
G
–hyperref[app:H]H proved uniform modal mass and density floors under entropy-rate control.

I.2
shows the opposite extreme: removing the constraint allows singular interior limits
despite good endpoint matching and competitive FM objective.

Output used next.

I.3
will convert this construction into an explicit
mode-collapse theorem
for classical FM:
for designated target modes
A_{k}
, minimum intermediate modal mass can be driven to
0
,
establishing failure of uniform mode coverage.

I.3. Explicit mode-collapse theorem for classical FM

We convert the singular-limit construction into a direct modal statement:
without entropy-rate control, uniform mode coverage fails in the strongest possible sense.

Modal setup.

Use Assumption
0..91
in 1D:

A_{-}:=(-\infty,0),\qquad A_{+}:=(0,\infty),\qquad\mu_{T}(A_{\pm})=\pi_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}.

For any trajectory
(\mu_{t})
, define modal masses

M_{\pm}(t):=\mu_{t}(A_{\pm}).

To capture semantic (separated) modes, introduce core neighborhoods around mixture centers:

C_{-}:=(-a-r,-a+r),\qquad C_{+}:=(a-r,a+r),\quad 0<r\ll a.

Core masses:

\mathfrak{m}_{\pm}(t):=\mu_{t}(C_{\pm}).

Definition 68
(Uniform mode-coverage property (UMC))

A model class satisfies UMC on
(C_{-},C_{+})
if there exists
\underline{c}>0
such that
for every admissible optimal trajectory,

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\min\{\mathfrak{m}_{-}(t),\mathfrak{m}_{+}(t)\}\geq\underline{c}.

Definition 69
(Classical FM admissible near-minimizers)

Given teacher
v^{\dagger}
, define
\eta
-near-minimizer class

\mathfrak{A}_{\eta}:=\left\{(\mu,v):\partial_{t}\mu+\nabla\!\cdot(\mu v)=0,\ \mu_{0},\mu_{T}\ \text{fixed},\ \mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}(v)\leq\inf\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}+\eta\right\}.

Lemma 40
(Core-mass extinction along collapse sequence)

For the sequence
(\mu_{t}^{(n)})
from
I.2
and any fixed
t\in(0,T)
,

\mathfrak{m}_{-}^{(n)}(t)\to 0,\qquad\mathfrak{m}_{+}^{(n)}(t)\to 0.

Moreover,

\inf_{t\in[\tau_{n},T-\tau_{n}]}\mathfrak{m}_{\pm}^{(n)}(t)\to 0.

Proof

By Lemma
39
, for interior times
t
,
\mu_{t}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}
.
Since
C_{\pm}
are bounded away from
0
, Portmanteau
[
22
]
gives

\mu_{t}^{(n)}(C_{\pm})\to\delta_{0}(C_{\pm})=0.

Uniform plateau statement follows because collapse map on
[\tau_{n},T-\tau_{n}]
keeps support in
an
O(\delta_{n}+\varepsilon_{n})
-tube near
0
, disjoint from
C_{\pm}
for large
n
.

Lemma 41
(Near-optimality of collapsing sequence)

For every
\eta>0
, there exists
n(\eta)
such that

(\mu^{(n)},v^{(n)})\in\mathfrak{A}_{\eta}\quad\forall n\geq n(\eta).

Proof

This is Theorem
0..94
(3): excess FM risk is
o(1)
via shrinking transition windows.

Theorem 0..95
(Failure of uniform mode coverage in classical FM)

Classical unconstrained FM fails UMC on
(C_{-},C_{+})
. Precisely:
for every
\underline{c}>0
and every
\eta>0
, there exists an
\eta
-near-minimizer

(\mu,v)\in\mathfrak{A}_{\eta}
such that

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\min\{\mathfrak{m}_{-}(t),\mathfrak{m}_{+}(t)\}<\underline{c}.

In fact one can force the infimum to
0
along a sequence of near-minimizers.

Proof

Fix
\underline{c},\eta
. Choose
n
large so that Lemma
41
gives

(\mu^{(n)},v^{(n)})\in\mathfrak{A}_{\eta}
, and Lemma
40
gives

\inf_{t\in[\tau_{n},T-\tau_{n}]}\min\{\mathfrak{m}_{-}^{(n)}(t),\mathfrak{m}_{+}^{(n)}(t)\}<\underline{c}.

Hence the global infimum over
[0,T]
is
<\underline{c}
.
Taking
n\to\infty
yields infimum
\to 0
.

Corollary 51
(No positive modal lower bound independent of trajectory)

There is no constant
\underline{c}>0
such that all unconstrained FM near-minimizers
satisfy

\mathfrak{m}_{\pm}(t)\geq\underline{c},\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

Proof

Immediate from Theorem
0..95
.

Proposition 57
(Set-mass vs semantic-mode collapse distinction)

For the collapse sequence, sign-partition masses can remain balanced:

M_{+}^{(n)}(t)\approx M_{-}^{(n)}(t)\approx\tfrac{1}{2}

while semantic core masses vanish:

\mathfrak{m}_{\pm}^{(n)}(t)\to 0.

Thus collapse may be invisible to coarse half-space statistics and requires geometric modal cores.

Proof

Plateau map preserves sign up to negligible smoothing artifacts, so
M_{\pm}
stay near
1/2
.
But both components are transported near
0
, far from
\pm a
, so
C_{\pm}
-masses vanish.

Theorem 0..96
(Necessity of entropy-rate control for guaranteed mode coverage)

Assume a framework guarantees positive uniform modal floor for all optimal/near-optimal trajectories.
Then such guarantee cannot hold for classical unconstrained FM in general.
Hence an additional regularizer/constraint is necessary; ECFM’s entropy-rate condition is sufficient
by Sections
G
–
H
.

Proof

Contrapositive via Theorem
0..95
: unconstrained FM admits near-optimal trajectories with
arbitrarily small modal core mass. Therefore any universal positive floor guarantee fails without extra structure.
Sections
G
–
H
provide such structure through entropy-rate control.

Remark 42
(Practical implication)

Training objectives that only regress local velocity targets can admit hidden collapse channels:
trajectories pass through low-entropy bottlenecks and re-expand. Enforcing entropy-rate budget removes these channels.

Output used next.

I.4
will present a compact “failure theorem package”:
(i) collapse construction, (ii) singular-limit result, (iii) UMC impossibility,
and a direct comparison table against entropy-controlled ECFM guarantees.

I.4. Consolidated failure package and direct comparison with ECFM

We collect the unconstrained-FM failure results into a single theorem package and
state a side-by-side guarantee contrast versus entropy-controlled ECFM.

Theorem 0..97
(Failure package for classical unconstrained FM)

Under the two-mode setting of Assumption
0..91
, for unconstrained FM
(Definition
65
), the following hold:

1.

Collapse construction:
there exists a CE-admissible sequence

(\mu^{(n)},v^{(n)})
with exact endpoint matching
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
, such that on interior times,
mass concentrates in an
o(1)
-neighborhood of a single bottleneck point.

2.

Singular interior limit:

\mu_{t}^{(n)}\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in(0,T),

while endpoint traces remain
\mu_{0},\mu_{T}
.

3.

Near-optimal objective compatibility:
for every
\eta>0
, for
n
large,

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}(v^{(n)})\leq\inf\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}+\eta.

4.

Uniform mode-coverage impossibility:

for semantic cores
C_{\pm}
around target modes,

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\min\{\mu_{t}^{(n)}(C_{-}),\mu_{t}^{(n)}(C_{+})\}\to 0.

5.

Entropy-rate blow-up:

\inf_{t}\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{(n)})\to-\infty,

hence any finite entropy budget
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
is eventually violated.

Proof

(1) is Definition
66
+ Lemma
36
.
(2) is Proposition
55
.
(3) is Lemma
41
.
(4) is Theorem
0..95
(with Lemma
40
).
(5) is Corollary
50
and Proposition
56
.
Combining yields the package.

Corollary 52
(No theorem-level anti-collapse guarantee without extra regularization)

Any theorem claiming uniform positive intermediate mode coverage for all optimal/near-optimal
classical FM trajectories is false in general unless additional constraints/regularizers are imposed.

Proof

Direct from item (4) of Theorem
0..97
.

Proposition 58
(Minimality of the entropy-rate remedy)

Among constraints acting on trajectory geometry, the entropy-rate lower bound

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda

is sufficient to exclude the collapse channel of Theorem
0..97

(Sections Sections
G
–
H
), while leaving endpoint feasibility and variational structure intact (Sections Sections
C
–
F
).

Proof

Sufficiency follows from Sections
G.1
–
G.4
(positive modal floors, density floors, perturbation robustness).
Compatibility with variational/duality/limit analysis follows from Sections
C
–
F
.
Thus the collapse mechanism (requiring entropy-rate spikes to
-\infty
) is blocked.

Theorem 0..98
(Sharp dichotomy: unconstrained FM vs ECFM)

For the same endpoint pair
(\mu_{0},\mu_{T})
:

1.

Unconstrained FM
admits near-optimal trajectories with singular interior collapse.

2.

ECFM
(finite
\lambda
) enforces quantitative non-collapse:
uniform modal mass lower bounds and modal-core density floors.

Hence entropy-rate control induces a qualitative phase change in admissible transport geometry.

Proof

Item (1): Theorem
0..97
.
Item (2): Theorems
0..69
,
0..74
,

0..76
,
0..77
.
Jointly this is a strict dichotomy.

Property

Classical FM
(no entropy constraint)

ECFM (
\dot{\mathcal{H}}\geq-\lambda
)

Endpoint matching

Possible

Possible

Near-optimal FM loss

Possible with collapse

Possible without collapse channel

Interior singular limits

Can occur (
I.2
)

G.2
–
G.3
)

Uniform mode mass floor

Not guaranteed (
I.3
)

G.1
–
G.3
)

Modal-core density floor

Not guaranteed

Guaranteed (
G.4
)

Robustness to perturbations

Lipschitz + floor robustness (
