)

Remark 43
(Failure mechanism in one line)

Unconstrained FM can “compress–teleport-through-low-entropy-bottleneck–reexpand” at near-equal loss;
ECFM forbids the compression step by capping admissible entropy dissipation rate.

Transition to Section
J
.

Section
J
maps the theory to practical vision generators (diffusion, flow matching,
rectified flow), giving implementation-level interpretation of the entropy budget
\lambda

and its effect on mode preservation in high-dimensional visual synthesis.

J. Connections to Vision Models

J.1. Diffusion, and flow-based models under entropy-controlled transport

We instantiate the ECFM theory in canonical vision-generation parameterizations and
derive the exact mapping between model-level dynamics and the entropy-budget variable
\lambda
.

Unified state dynamics.

Let
x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
denote latent/image state, with law
\mu_{t}
, and consider

dx_{t}=b_{\theta}(x_{t},t)\,dt+\sqrt{2\varepsilon(t)}\,dW_{t},

where
\varepsilon(t)\geq 0
may be identically zero (deterministic flow models).
The associated Fokker–Planck equation is

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}b_{\theta})=\varepsilon(t)\Delta\rho_{t}.

Define current velocity

v_{\theta}(x,t):=b_{\theta}(x,t)-\varepsilon(t)\nabla\log\rho_{t}(x),

so that continuity form holds:

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}+\nabla\!\cdot(\rho_{t}v_{\theta})=0.

Lemma 42
(Entropy-rate identity in unified dynamics)

Assume sufficient integrability and smoothness. Then

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla\!\cdot b_{\theta}(x,t)\,\rho_{t}(x)\,dx+\varepsilon(t)\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}),

equivalently, in continuity form,

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}\,d\mu_{t}.

Proof

For FP form:

\frac{d}{dt}\int\rho\log\rho=\int(1+\log\rho)\partial_{t}\rho=-\int(1+\log\rho)\nabla\!\cdot(\rho b_{\theta})+\varepsilon\int(1+\log\rho)\Delta\rho.

Integrating by parts:

-\int(1+\log\rho)\nabla\!\cdot(\rho b_{\theta})=\int\nabla\!\cdot b_{\theta}\,\rho,

\varepsilon\int(1+\log\rho)\Delta\rho=\varepsilon\int\frac{|\nabla\rho|^{2}}{\rho}=\varepsilon\,\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

For continuity form,
\partial_{t}\rho=-\nabla\!\cdot(\rho v_{\theta})
gives

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\int\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}\,d\mu_{t}.

Definition 70
(Model-implied entropy budget)

For a trained model trajectory
(\mu_{t},v_{\theta})
, define effective required budget

\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}(\theta):=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})\Big)_{+}.

Then trajectory is ECFM-feasible iff
\lambda\geq\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}(\theta)
.

Proposition 59
(Diffusion-model feasibility window)

For diffusion/score SDEs with
\varepsilon(t)>0
,

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot b_{\theta}]+\varepsilon(t)\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}).

Hence anti-collapse is strengthened by the nonnegative Fisher term.
A sufficient ECFM budget is

\lambda\geq\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t}\left(-\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot b_{\theta}]-\varepsilon(t)\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})\right)_{+}.

Proof

Immediate from Lemma
42
and Definition
70
.

Proposition 60
(Deterministic FM / rectified flow specialization)

For deterministic models (
\varepsilon\equiv 0
):

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}].

Thus ECFM reduces to divergence-budget control:

\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}]\geq-\lambda\quad\text{a.e.}

Collapse channels correspond to large negative divergence spikes.

Proof

Set
\varepsilon=0
in Lemma
42
.

Theorem 0..99
(Model-class transfer of G/H guarantees)

Suppose a vision generator (diffusion/FM/rectified flow) induces trajectory

(\mu_{t},v_{\theta})
satisfying:

1.

CE/FP regularity from Sections
C
–
E
,

2.

entropy-rate lower bound
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})\geq-\lambda
a.e.,

3.

endpoint and coercivity assumptions of Sections
F
–
H
.

Then all Section G/H guarantees apply verbatim:

1.

quantitative mode-mass floors,

2.

modal-core density floors,

3.

perturbation robustness to endpoint/drift/noise/initialization shifts.

Proof

Sections
G
/
H
depend on trajectory-level properties (CE/regularity + entropy-rate budget),
not on specific parameterization of
v_{\theta}
. Hence any model class satisfying these
assumptions inherits the same conclusions.

Corollary 53
(Architecture-agnostic anti-collapse certificate)

Define certificate

\mathfrak{C}_{\lambda}(\theta):=\mathbf{1}\!\left\{\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}^{\theta})\geq-\lambda\ \text{a.e.}\right\}.

If
\mathfrak{C}_{\lambda}(\theta)=1
, model receives theorem-level anti-collapse guarantees from Sections
G
/
H
,
independent of whether it is diffusion, FM, or rectified flow.

Proof

Direct from Theorem
0..99
.

Remark 44
(Why this matters in high-dimensional vision)

In high-dimensional synthesis, objective-level fit can hide trajectory-level bottlenecks.
The entropy certificate
\mathfrak{C}_{\lambda}
is a trajectory-geometric condition that
rules out these bottlenecks irrespective of architecture details.

Output used next.

J.2
derives practical estimators of
\dot{\mathcal{H}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}
, and modal floors
from minibatch trajectories, giving implementable diagnostics for diffusion/FM/rectified-flow training.

J.2. Practical entropy estimators and coverage diagnostics

We derive computable estimators for

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t}),\qquad\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}},\qquad\text{mode-coverage floors},

directly from minibatch trajectories of diffusion/FM/rectified-flow models.

Sampling model.

At discrete times
0=t_{0}<\cdots<t_{N}=T
, assume i.i.d. particles

x_{i,n}\sim\mu_{t_{n}},\qquad i=1,\dots,B_{n},

and model outputs either drift/current velocity evaluations

b_{\theta}(x_{i,n},t_{n})
,
v_{\theta}(x_{i,n},t_{n})
, and (if available) score estimates

s_{\theta}(x,t)\approx\nabla\log\rho_{t}(x).

Definition 71
(Discrete entropy-rate target)

Define interval-average entropy rate

\dot{\mathcal{H}}_{[t_{n},t_{n+1}]}:=\frac{\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t_{n+1}})-\mathcal{H}(\mu_{t_{n}})}{\Delta t_{n}},\qquad\Delta t_{n}:=t_{n+1}-t_{n}.

Its continuous counterpart is
\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t)
.

Proposition 61
(Divergence-form estimator)

From Lemma
42
:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}(\cdot,t)].

Hence unbiased Monte Carlo estimator (if exact divergence available):

\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}^{\mathrm{div}}_{n}=\frac{1}{B_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}}\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}(x_{i,n},t_{n}).

If only Jacobian-vector products are available, use Hutchinson:

\nabla\!\cdot v(x)\approx\frac{1}{R}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\zeta_{r}^{\top}J_{v}(x)\zeta_{r},\qquad\zeta_{r}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})\ \text{or Rademacher}.

Then

\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}^{\mathrm{div\text{-}Hutch}}_{n}=\frac{1}{B_{n}R}\sum_{i,r}\zeta_{i,r}^{\top}J_{v}(x_{i,n},t_{n})\zeta_{i,r}.

Proof

Identity is from Lemma
42
.
Unbiasedness of Hutchinson trace estimator is standard:

\mathbb{E}_{\zeta}[\zeta^{\top}J\zeta]=\mathrm{tr}(J)
.
Averaging over samples gives unbiased Monte Carlo estimator.

Proposition 62
(FP-form estimator for diffusion models)

If model supplies
b_{\theta}
and score
s_{\theta}\approx\nabla\log\rho_{t}
, then

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot b_{\theta}]+\varepsilon(t)\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t}),\qquad\mathcal{I}(\mu_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}\|\nabla\log\rho_{t}\|^{2}.

Estimator:

\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}^{\mathrm{fp}}_{n}=\frac{1}{B_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}}\nabla\!\cdot b_{\theta}(x_{i,n},t_{n})+\varepsilon(t_{n})\frac{1}{B_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}}\|s_{\theta}(x_{i,n},t_{n})\|^{2}.

Proof

Direct Monte Carlo plug-in to FP identity in Lemma
42
.

Definition 72
(Finite-sample safety margin)

Given estimator
\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}
, define one-sided lower confidence bound

\mathrm{LCB}_{n}:=\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-\mathrm{rad}_{n}(\alpha),

where
\mathrm{rad}_{n}(\alpha)
is concentration radius at confidence
1-\alpha
.
Entropy-budget feasibility certificate at level
\lambda
:

\mathrm{LCB}_{n}\geq-\lambda\quad\forall n.

Theorem 0..100
(Uniform high-probability budget certification)

Assume per-time estimator errors are sub-Gaussian with proxy variance
\sigma_{n}^{2}
:

\mathbb{P}\!\left(|\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n})|>u\right)\leq 2e^{-u^{2}/(2\sigma_{n}^{2})}.

Set

\mathrm{rad}_{n}(\alpha):=\sigma_{n}\sqrt{2\log\frac{2(N+1)}{\alpha}}.

Then with probability at least
1-\alpha
, simultaneously for all
n
:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n})\geq\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-\mathrm{rad}_{n}(\alpha)=\mathrm{LCB}_{n}.

Hence if
\min_{n}\mathrm{LCB}_{n}\geq-\lambda
, the discrete trajectory is budget-feasible with confidence
1-\alpha
.

Proof

Apply union bound over
n=0,\dots,N
with sub-Gaussian tails.

Definition 73
(Effective budget estimators)

Discrete estimators:

\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\max}:=\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\big(-\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}\big)_{+},

\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{LCB}}:=\max_{n}\big(-\mathrm{LCB}_{n}\big)_{+}.

The second is conservative (high-probability upper bound on required budget).

Proposition 63
(Consistency of
\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}
)

If
N\to\infty
,
B_{n}\to\infty
, and estimators are uniformly consistent:

\max_{n}|\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n})|\xrightarrow{p}0,

then

\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\max}\xrightarrow{p}\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{disc}}:=\max_{n}(-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n}))_{+}.

If time discretization refines and
\dot{\mathcal{H}}
is continuous a.e., then

\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{disc}}\to\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t\in[0,T]}(-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t))_{+}.

Proof

Continuity of
x\mapsto x_{+}
, max-map stability under uniform convergence, then mesh-refinement argument.

Modal diagnostics.

Let semantic mode sets
\{A_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
(or learned cores
K_{k}\Subset A_{k}
).
Define empirical masses

\widehat{M}_{k,n}:=\frac{1}{B_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{i,n}\in A_{k}\},\qquad\widehat{m}_{k,n}:=\frac{1}{B_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{i,n}\in K_{k}\}.

Theorem 0..101
(Finite-sample modal floor certification)

For each
(k,n)
, Hoeffding gives

\mathbb{P}\left(|\,\widehat{M}_{k,n}-M_{k}(t_{n})\,|>\epsilon_{k,n}\right)\leq 2e^{-2B_{n}\epsilon_{k,n}^{2}}.

Set

\epsilon_{k,n}(\alpha):=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2B_{n}}\log\frac{2K(N+1)}{\alpha}}.

Then with probability at least
1-\alpha
, for all
k,n
:

M_{k}(t_{n})\geq\widehat{M}_{k,n}-\epsilon_{k,n}(\alpha),\qquad\mathfrak{m}_{k}(t_{n})\geq\widehat{m}_{k,n}-\epsilon_{k,n}(\alpha).

Hence certified discrete lower floors:

\underline{M}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}:=\min_{n}\big(\widehat{M}_{k,n}-\epsilon_{k,n}(\alpha)\big),\quad\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}:=\min_{n}\big(\widehat{m}_{k,n}-\epsilon_{k,n}(\alpha)\big).

Proof

Apply Hoeffding
[
18
]
per pair
(k,n)
, then union bound over
K(N+1)
events.

Proposition 64
(Density-floor proxy via local occupancy)

Let
B_{r}(x)\subset K_{k}
be fixed probes. Define

\widehat{p}_{k,n}(x):=\frac{1}{B_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}}\mathbf{1}\{x_{i,n}\in B_{r}(x)\}.

Then

\frac{\widehat{p}_{k,n}(x)-\epsilon}{|B_{r}|}

is a high-probability lower proxy for local average density on
B_{r}(x)
, yielding empirical
modal-core density-floor diagnostics consistent with G.4 as
r\downarrow 0
,
B_{n}\uparrow\infty
.

Proof

Occupancy estimates local mass; divide by volume for average density.
Concentration from binomial tails; consistency from Lebesgue differentiation under regularity.

Definition 74
(Practical ECFM diagnostic tuple)

At training/inference checkpoints, report:

\mathfrak{D}_{\theta}:=\Big(\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{LCB}},\ \{\underline{M}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}\}_{k=1}^{K},\ \{\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}\}_{k=1}^{K},\ \text{action gap proxy}\Big).

A model is
(\lambda,\beta)
-certified anti-collapse if

\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{LCB}}\leq\lambda,\qquad\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}\geq\beta_{k}>0,\ \forall k.

Remark 45
(Architecture-specific implementation notes)

•

Diffusion:
use FP estimator (Proposition
62
), score norm for Fisher term.

•

Flow matching / rectified flow:
use divergence estimator (Proposition
61
) via Hutchinson JVPs.

•

Latent diffusion in vision:
compute diagnostics in latent space and optionally map mode sets via encoder semantics.

Output used next.

J.3
converts diagnostics into training-time constrained updates:
dual ascent on entropy budget, adaptive
\lambda
-scheduling, and theorem-consistent practical objective.

J.3. Entropy-budget objective with dual updates and adaptive scheduling

We derive a practical constrained-training procedure consistent with Sections
C
–
H
:
optimize velocity matching subject to an entropy-rate lower bound, using primal–dual updates.

Constrained objective (discrete-time form).

Let
t_{0},\dots,t_{N}
be training times, weights
w_{n}>0
, and FM-style loss

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{fit}}(\theta):=\sum_{n=0}^{N}w_{n}\,\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu_{t_{n}}^{\theta}}\big\|v_{\theta}(x,t_{n})-v^{\dagger}(x,t_{n})\big\|^{2}.

Define entropy-rate residual at time
t_{n}
:

g_{n}(\theta;\lambda_{n}):=-\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}(\theta)-\lambda_{n}.

Feasibility means
g_{n}\leq 0
for all
n
.

Definition 75
(Discrete ECFM training program)

Given per-time budgets
\lambda_{n}\geq 0
, solve

\min_{\theta}\ \mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{fit}}(\theta)\quad\text{s.t.}\quad g_{n}(\theta;\lambda_{n})\leq 0,\ \forall n=0,\dots,N.

Uniform budget corresponds to
\lambda_{n}\equiv\lambda
.

Definition 76
(Augmented Lagrangian)

Introduce multipliers
\eta_{n}\geq 0
, penalty
\rho>0
:

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AL}}(\theta,\eta)=\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{fit}}(\theta)+\sum_{n=0}^{N}\eta_{n}\,g_{n}(\theta;\lambda_{n})+\frac{\rho}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{N}[g_{n}(\theta;\lambda_{n})]_{+}^{2}.

Proposition 65
(KKT conditions (discrete training))

At a local saddle point
(\theta^{\star},\eta^{\star})
, necessary conditions are:

\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{fit}}(\theta^{\star})+\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{\star}\nabla_{\theta}g_{n}(\theta^{\star};\lambda_{n})+\rho\sum_{n}[g_{n}(\theta^{\star};\lambda_{n})]_{+}\nabla_{\theta}g_{n}(\theta^{\star};\lambda_{n})=0,

\eta_{n}^{\star}\geq 0,\qquad g_{n}(\theta^{\star};\lambda_{n})\leq 0,\qquad\eta_{n}^{\star}g_{n}(\theta^{\star};\lambda_{n})=0,\ \forall n.

Proof

Standard KKT for inequality constraints with quadratic penalty term added in augmented Lagrangian form.

Theorem 0..102
(Convergence to first-order stationary KKT point)

Assume:

1.

\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AL}}
is
L
-Lipschitz on bounded iterates;

2.

unbiased stochastic gradients with bounded variance;

3.

step sizes satisfy Robbins–Monro:

\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}=\infty,\ \sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}<\infty
,

\sum_{k}\beta_{k}=\infty,\ \sum_{k}\beta_{k}^{2}<\infty
;

4.

Slater-type feasibility holds for the discrete constraints.

Then every limit point of iterates generated by Algorithm
1

is a first-order KKT point of Definition
75
(in expectation / a.s. subsequential sense).

Proof

Apply stochastic primal–dual convergence for nonconvex constrained programs with projected dual ascent.
Quadratic penalty stabilizes infeasible iterates; Slater condition ensures bounded multipliers.
Standard martingale arguments yield vanishing expected gradient mapping and asymptotic complementarity.

Definition 77
(Adaptive entropy-budget scheduler)

Let target feasibility margin
\gamma_{n}>0
. Update budgets by

\lambda_{n}^{k+1}=\Pi_{[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]}\Big(\lambda_{n}^{k}+\zeta_{k}\big(\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}(\theta^{k+1})+\gamma_{n}\big)\Big),

where
\Pi
is interval projection.

Theorem 0..103
(Scheduler stability and feasibility tracking)

Assume estimator bias is bounded and
\zeta_{k}
is slower timescale than
\alpha_{k},\beta_{k}

(two-timescale SA). Then
\lambda_{n}^{k}
tracks the minimal feasible budget:

\lambda_{n}^{k}\to\lambda_{n,\mathrm{eff}}^{\star}\quad\text{(up to estimator bias radius)},

and residuals satisfy

\limsup_{k\to\infty}g_{n}(\theta^{k};\lambda_{n}^{k})\leq 0.

Proof

On fast timescale,
(\theta,\eta)
equilibrate for quasi-static
\lambda
.
On slow timescale, projected recursion on
\lambda
is a stochastic approximation to ODE

\dot{\lambda}_{n}=\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}+\gamma_{n},

whose stable points satisfy nonpositive residual with margin.
Projection ensures boundedness.

Proposition 66
(Robust variant using lower confidence bounds)

Replacing
g_{n}=-\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-\lambda_{n}
with

g_{n}^{\mathrm{rob}}:=-\mathrm{LCB}_{n}-\lambda_{n}

(Definition
72
) yields high-probability conservative feasibility:
if
g_{n}^{\mathrm{rob}}\leq 0
for all
n
, then

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n})\geq-\lambda_{n}

holds simultaneously with confidence
1-\alpha
.

Proof

Direct consequence of Theorem
0..100
.

Definition 78
(Mode-aware constrained objective)

To directly enforce modal floors at training time, add constraints

h_{k,n}(\theta):=\beta_{k}-\widehat{m}_{k,n}(\theta)\leq 0

with multipliers
\nu_{k,n}\geq 0
, giving

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AL}}^{\mathrm{mode}}=\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{AL}}+\sum_{k,n}\nu_{k,n}h_{k,n}+\frac{\rho_{m}}{2}\sum_{k,n}[h_{k,n}]_{+}^{2}.

Theorem 0..104
(Theorem-consistent practical guarantee)

Suppose Algorithm
1
(optionally mode-aware) converges to

(\theta^{\star},\eta^{\star},\nu^{\star})
with robust residual feasibility:

g_{n}^{\mathrm{rob}}(\theta^{\star};\lambda_{n})\leq 0,\quad h_{k,n}^{\mathrm{rob}}(\theta^{\star})\leq 0\ \text{(if used)}.

Then, with confidence
1-\alpha
:

1.

discrete entropy-budget constraints hold;

2.

certified modal floors hold at sampled times;

3.

by Section H stability, small deployment perturbations preserve floors up to
O(\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}})
.

Proof

(1) from Proposition
66
.
(2) from Theorem
0..101
.
(3) from Theorem
0..88
.

Remark 46
(Computational cost)

Additional overhead is dominated by divergence/score diagnostics:

•

FM/rectified flow:
R
Hutchinson JVPs per sample-time pair.

•

Diffusion: divergence of drift + score-norm term (already available in many pipelines).

•

Dual variables scale with number of enforced time bins/modes; can be block-shared in practice.

Output used next.

J.4
provides implementation-level interpretation for vision practice:
choice of
\lambda
, discretization granularity, latent-vs-pixel diagnostics,
and recommended reporting protocol aligned with theory claims.

J.4. Vision protocol:
\lambda
, discretization, latent-vs-pixel diagnostics, reporting

This section turns
J.2
–
J.3
into a concrete protocol for theory-grounded practice.

Objective.

Given a generator (diffusion / FM / rectified flow), produce a
theory-aligned certificate

that links training/inference dynamics to:

\text{(i) entropy-budget feasibility},\quad\text{(ii) mode-coverage floors},\quad\text{(iii) perturbation robustness margins}.

Definition 79
(Protocol hyperparameters)

Choose:

1.

time grid
\{t_{n}\}_{n=0}^{N}
(uniform or curvature-adaptive);

2.

confidence level
1-\alpha
;

3.

entropy budget range
[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
;

4.

modal sets
\{A_{k}\}
and optional cores
\{K_{k}\Subset A_{k}\}
;

5.

minibatch sizes
B_{n}
, Hutchinson probes
R_{n}
;

6.

robustness radius target
\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\max}
(deployment shift envelope).

Proposition 67
(Time-grid adequacy criterion)

Let
\dot{\mathcal{H}}
be locally Lipschitz with modulus
L_{H}
on intervals.
If grid satisfies

\max_{n}\Delta t_{n}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{H}}{L_{H}},

then discretization error in peak negative entropy-rate obeys

\left|\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t}(-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t))_{+}-\max_{n}(-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n}))_{+}\right|\leq\epsilon_{H}.

Proof

For each interval
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, Lipschitz continuity gives

|\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t)-\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n})|\leq L_{H}|t-t_{n}|\leq L_{H}\Delta t_{n}\leq\epsilon_{H}.

Apply monotonicity/Lipschitz of
x\mapsto(-x)_{+}
, then take sup over intervals.

Definition 80
(Adaptive refinement trigger)

Refine interval
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
if any holds:

\mathrm{LCB}_{n}\in[-\lambda-\delta_{\lambda},\,-\lambda+\delta_{\lambda}],\quad\widehat{\mathrm{Var}}(\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n})>\tau_{H},\quad\min_{k}\big(\widehat{m}_{k,n}-\epsilon_{k,n}\big)<\tau_{m}.

This concentrates samples where certification is most fragile.

Definition 81
(Budget selection rule)

Given estimators from J.2, define conservative selected budget:

\lambda^{\star}:=\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{LCB}}+\delta_{\mathrm{safe}},\qquad\delta_{\mathrm{safe}}>0.

For per-time budgets:

\lambda_{n}^{\star}:=\big(-\mathrm{LCB}_{n}\big)_{+}+\delta_{\mathrm{safe},n}.

Proposition 68
(Guarantee from selected budget)

If training converges with robust residual feasibility under
\lambda^{\star}
, then with confidence
1-\alpha
:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t_{n})\geq-\lambda^{\star},\ \forall n.

With grid adequacy (Proposition
67
) and
\epsilon_{H}
-refinement:

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(t)\geq-(\lambda^{\star}+\epsilon_{H})\quad\text{a.e. }t\in[0,T].

Proof

Discrete statement: Theorem
0..100
+ definition of
\lambda^{\star}
.
Continuous-time extension: interpolation error bounded by Proposition
67
.

Latent vs pixel-space diagnostics.

Let encoder
E:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
, decoder
D:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
, latent law
\nu_{t}=E_{\#}\mu_{t}
.

Assumption 0..105
(Bi-Lipschitz semantic chart on data manifold)

On relevant manifold region
\mathcal{M}\subset\mathcal{X}
, there exist
0<\ell\leq L<\infty
:

\ell\|x-y\|\leq\|E(x)-E(y)\|\leq L\|x-y\|,\qquad x,y\in\mathcal{M}.

Theorem 0..106
(Transfer of modal floors between latent and pixel spaces)

Under Assumption
0..105
, for modal sets
A_{k}^{z}\subset\mathcal{Z}
, define

A_{k}^{x}:=D(A_{k}^{z})\cap\mathcal{M}
. Then certified latent mass floors imply pixel-space floors:

\nu_{t}(A_{k}^{z})\geq\beta_{k}\ \Longrightarrow\ \mu_{t}(A_{k}^{x})\geq\beta_{k}-\delta_{\mathrm{chart}},

where
\delta_{\mathrm{chart}}
captures manifold/decoder approximation error.
Likewise, perturbation robustness constants scale by at most bi-Lipschitz factors:

C_{W}^{x}\leq\frac{L}{\ell}C_{W}^{z}.

Proof

Pushforward/pullback of measurable sets through
E,D
with chart distortion error.
Distance and stability constant transfer follows from bi-Lipschitz inequalities.

Definition 82
(Recommended certification report tuple)

For each trained checkpoint, report

\mathcal{R}:=\Big(N,\ \alpha,\ \lambda^{\star},\ \widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{LCB}},\ \{\underline{M}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}\}_{k=1}^{K},\ \{\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}\}_{k=1}^{K},\ \widehat{C}_{W},\widehat{C}_{M},\widehat{C}_{\rho},\ \Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\max}\Big),

plus estimator settings
(B_{n},R_{n})
, refinement policy, and modal-set construction protocol.

Proposition 69
(Minimal theory-compliant table schema)

A theory-compliant experimental appendix table should contain columns:

\text{Model},\ \lambda^{\star},\ \widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{LCB}},\ \min_{k}\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}},\ \text{Feasible?},\ \widehat{C}_{W},\ \text{Robust floor at }\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\max}.

This is sufficient to support theorem-grounded anti-collapse claims without SOTA metrics.

Proof

Each column corresponds directly to hypotheses/conclusions of
J.2
–
J.3
and
H
:
budget feasibility, modal floors, and perturbation-preserved floors.

Theorem 0..107
(End-to-end implementation guarantee)

Assume:

1.

training follows Algorithm
1
with robust residuals;

2.

diagnostics satisfy
J.2
concentration conditions;

3.

grid adequacy/refinement condition in Proposition
67
;

4.

stability envelope constants are estimated conservatively.

Then with probability
1-\alpha
, the deployed model enjoys:

1.

entropy-budget feasibility up to discretization slack
\epsilon_{H}
;

2.

certified modal floors at sampled times and interpolated times under refinement;

3.

perturbation-robust mode/density floors for all shifts
\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}\leq\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\max}
:

\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{deploy}}\geq\underline{m}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}-\widehat{C}_{M}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\max},

\underline{\rho}_{k}^{\mathrm{deploy}}\geq\underline{\rho}_{k}^{\mathrm{cert}}-\widehat{C}_{\rho}\Delta_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\max}.

Proof

(1) from Proposition
68
.
(2) from Theorem
0..101
plus refinement/interpolation control.
(3) from unified perturbation theorem
H.4
with conservative constants.

Remark 47
(What we claim)

Our claims are
certificate-based
; that is under verified entropy-budget feasibility and stated regularity assumptions, the model
satisfies theorem-level anti-collapse and robustness guarantees.

Practical interpretation of standing assumptions (vision context).

Our analysis is posed on absolutely-continuous measures to make entropy and its time-derivative well-defined.
In vision generators, this is aligned with common continuous modeling choices:

•

(S1) Absolute continuity of endpoints.

While raw images live on a discrete grid, continuous generative training typically uses
dequantization

(or operates in a continuous latent space), yielding an effective absolutely-continuous target.
When endpoints are only approximately a.c., mollification/dequantization produces a nearby instance, and
our perturbation stability results quantify how certificates degrade under such small endpoint shifts.

•

Entropy-rate identity.

The constraint is enforced via the expected divergence identity

\dot{\mathcal{H}}(\mu_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t}}[\nabla\!\cdot v(\cdot,t)]
under the regularity conditions in App.
B
.
In neural parameterizations,
\nabla\!\cdot v
is estimated using Hutchinson/JVP diagnostics (App.
J.2
).

•

(S2) Feasibility.

Feasibility is mild in practice: one may start from the unconstrained FM solution (
\lambda=+\infty
)
and decrease
\lambda
until empirical feasibility holds with confidence (Sec.
2.2
and App.
J.4
).

Practical enforcement and certificate computation (implementation sketch).

We discretize time with a grid
\{t_{n}\}_{n=0}^{N}
and enforce the entropy budget using the entropy-rate/divergence identity
(
3
) and the equivalent divergence-budget constraint (
7
).
At training checkpoints, we estimate
\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}\approx\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{t_{n}}}[\nabla\!\cdot v_{\theta}(\cdot,t_{n})]

via the divergence/Hutchinson JVP estimator for FM/rectified flow (Proposition
61
),
or the Fokker–Planck form for diffusion models (Proposition
62
),
using minibatch sizes
B_{n}
and Hutchinson probes
R_{n}
.

Robust feasibility via confidence bounds.

Because divergence/score diagnostics are estimated from finite minibatches and Hutchinson probes, we certify feasibility using a lower confidence bound:
for each time bin
t_{n}
, compute
\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}
and an empirical standard error
\widehat{\mathrm{SE}}_{n}
(over probes/minibatches),
then form
\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}^{\mathrm{LCB}}_{n}:=\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-z_{1-\alpha}\widehat{\mathrm{SE}}_{n}

(optionally with a Bonferroni correction
\alpha/N
).
We declare the discrete entropy budget satisfied at confidence
1-\alpha
when

\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}^{\mathrm{LCB}}_{n}\geq-\lambda_{n}
for all
n
,
equivalently
g_{n}^{\mathrm{rob}}:=-\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}^{\mathrm{LCB}}_{n}-\lambda_{n}\leq 0
.

To
enforce
the constraint, we maintain nonnegative discrete dual variables
\eta_{n}\geq 0
and apply projected dual ascent
(as formalized by the discrete constrained program in Definition
75
and implemented in Algorithm
1
),
e.g.,

\eta_{n}\leftarrow\big[\eta_{n}+\gamma\,(-\widehat{\dot{\mathcal{H}}}_{n}-\lambda)\big]_{+}
,
while updating
\theta
using the corresponding Lagrangian/augmented objective (Definition
76
).
To
certify
, we report the diagnostic tuple in Definition
74
and declare
(\lambda,\beta)
-certification when

\widehat{\lambda}^{\mathrm{LCB}}_{\mathrm{eff}}\leq\lambda
and the certified mode floors exceed
\beta
;
App.
J.4
gives the full protocol (grid adequacy, latent-vs-pixel checks) and a minimal theory-compliant reporting schema
(Proposition
69
).

Conclusions.

Sections
A
–
J
now provide: measure-theoretic setup, entropy functional analysis, variational/dual formulations,
equivalence to Schrödinger bridges, convergence and
\Gamma
-limit theory, rigorous mode-coverage theorems,
stability analysis, explicit failure constructions without entropy control, and implementation-level certification protocol for vision models.
